### Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

### Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

### Scientific Series

# Phaladeepika
<note>Literally "The Lamp of Fruits" or "Light on Results," this is a foundational 16th-century Sanskrit treatise on Hindu predictive astrology, authored by Mantreshwara.</note>

### Chapter One

### Classifications of the Zodiac Signs

> Meditate upon the God who wears white garments, who is the color of the moon, who has four arms and a tranquil face, for the removal of all obstacles.
> <note>original: "Shuklam baradharam devam..." This is a traditional invocation to Lord Ganesha, the remover of obstacles, used at the start of many Indian texts.</note>

> May that supreme Light bestow upon us boundless prosperity; that Light which spreads the righteous path for the ancestors and the gods through the divisions of months, years, and seasons; which moves upward and travels sometimes clockwise and sometimes counter-clockwise.
> <note>original: "Saddharmanam vitanute..." This verse likely refers to the Sun or the celestial bodies (the "Light") that govern time and the calendar in the science of <term>Jyotisha|Vedic astrology</term>.</note>

> Having bowed with devotion to the Goddess of Speech, the family deity, my teachers, the nine planets beginning with the Sun—who grant the knowledge of Time—and to Lord Ganapati...
> <note>original: "Vagdevim kuladevatam..." This is a formal prostration (Vandana) identifying the spiritual lineage and the planetary forces (<term>Navagrahas|The nine celestial influencers</term>) that will be discussed in the book.</note>

<summary>This page serves as the formal opening of the Phaladeepika, featuring traditional invocations to Ganesha, the goddess Saraswati, and the nine planets, followed by the introduction of Chapter One on the signs of the zodiac.</summary>
<keywords>Phaladeepika, Astrology, Ganesha, Navagrahas, Zodiac Signs, Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao, Jyotisha</keywords>

--- Page 2 ---
<meta>This page continues from the introductory invocations. It establishes the author's intent to summarize ancient wisdom and begins the technical classification of the zodiac signs and their relationship to the human body and environments.</meta>

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

Having summarized the teachings spoken by great sages like Atri and Parashara, I, Mantreshwara—a knower of the divine—shall speak this "Phaladeepika" <note>Literally "The Lamp of Fruits," meaning a text that illuminates the results of karma through the stars.</note>, which is perfectly clear, for the delight of those who study the stars. ||2||

First, the specific time of birth or an event must be understood with great precision, down to the smallest units of time <note>original: "vighatikas." In traditional Indian timekeeping, a vighatika is approximately 24 seconds.</note>. By using mathematical calculations to understand the true movements and positions of the planets, an astrologer should then declare their strength and the resulting effects on a person's life. ||3||

The signs of the zodiac, starting from the <term>Lagna|Ascendant</term>, represent the various parts of the "Time-Person": the head, face, chest, heart, stomach, waist, pelvis, genitals, thighs, the pair of knees, the shins, and the pair of feet. <note>original: "Kaalapurusha." This is the concept of the zodiac personified as a cosmic human body; the twelve signs from Aries to Pisces correspond to these body parts in order.</note> The junctions between the end of certain signs and the beginning of others are known as the famous "zodiacal junctures" <note>original: "Bhasandhi." These are considered sensitive or precarious points in a birth chart, particularly at the transitions between water and fire signs.</note>. ||4||

The signs are also associated with specific environments: forests, grain fields, bedrooms, water-filled areas, mountains, grassy lands, caves, the dwellings of common people, watery forests, and temples. <note>The text here begins to categorize the twelve signs by their "habitats" (Rashisthana), which helps in predicting where lost objects might be found or where a person might find success.</note>

<vocab>Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao, Phaladeepika, Jyotisha (Astrology), Sanskrit, Mantreshwara, Atri, Parashara</vocab>

<summary>In these verses, the author Mantreshwara credits the sages Atri and Parashara for his knowledge and begins describing the "Kaalapurusha," the mapping of the twelve zodiac signs onto the human body.</summary>
<keywords>Mantreshwara, Phaladeepika, Kaalapurusha, zodiac body parts, Bhasandhi, astrological calculations, Atri, Parashara</keywords>

--- Page 3 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical classification of the zodiac signs (Rashis) initiated on the previous page. It moves from physical body correlations to the environmental dwellings of the signs, their planetary rulers, and the specific degrees of exaltation and debilitation.</meta>

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

...The environments <note>original: "Vasati." Refers to the symbolic "home" or natural habitat of a sign which informs its character.</note> for the signs starting from Aries are said to be: a forest, a field of grain, a merchant’s house, water, a mountain, a grassy region, a cave, a village, a river, a forest, a village, and the ocean respectively. || 4 ||

Mars, Venus, Mercury, the Moon, the Sun, Mercury, Venus, Mars, Jupiter, Saturn, Saturn, and Jupiter are declared, in that order, to be the lords of the signs starting from Aries. <note>This provides the foundational rulership: Aries is ruled by Mars, Taurus by Venus, etc.</note> || 6 ||

The exaltation signs <term>Ucchagrihas|Exaltation houses</term> <note>These are specific signs where a planet's influence is considered most potent and positive.</note> for the Sun and the other planets are Aries, Taurus, Capricorn, Virgo, Cancer, Pisces, and Libra. Their highest points of exaltation are the 10th, 3rd, 28th, 15th, 5th, 27th, and 20th degrees respectively. Their points of deep debilitation <note>original: "Neecha." The opposite of exaltation, where the planet’s power is most diminished.</note> occur at the same degree in the seventh sign from their exaltation point. || 6 ||

Leo, Taurus, Virgo, Sagittarius, Aries, Libra, and Aquarius are the "Root Trines" <term>Moolatrikona|Root Trine</term> <note>A status of strength second only to exaltation.</note> of the planets starting with the Sun. Within these signs, the planets are most powerful at the 20th, 3rd to 30th, 16th to 20th, 0 to 13th, 0 to 12th, 0 to 20th, and 0 to 20th degrees respectively.

The first half of Sagittarius, Gemini, Virgo, Aquarius, and Libra are classified as human signs. Scorpio is a centipede sign. Cancer, the latter half of Capricorn, and Pisces are watery signs. The remaining signs are quadrupedal <note>original: "Chatushpada." Four-legged signs like Aries, Taurus, Leo, and the first half of Capricorn.</note>. || 7 ||

Taurus, Cancer, Aries, Sagittarius, and Capricorn are known as "hind-rising" <note>original: "Prishthodaya." Signs that rise over the horizon from their back or tail first.</note>. Gemini and Pisces rise both ways <note>original: "Ubhayodaya."</note>. The remaining signs rise head-first <note>original: "Shirshodaya." Generally considered more auspicious for day-time births.</note>. Saturn’s signs <note>Capricorn and Aquarius</note> and the others are classified as nocturnal or diurnal signs in their proper sequence.

<note>The vocabulary section below defines the technical terms used in the verses above.</note>
**Vocabulary:** <term>Jyotisham|Astrology</term>, <term>Mesham|Aries</term>, <term>Rasheshwarah|Lords of the Signs</term>, <term>Ucchagrihah|Exaltation Houses</term>, <term>Rashichakramu|The Zodiac Wheel</term>

<summary>This page defines the planetary rulers of each zodiac sign and provides the specific degrees for exaltation and debilitation. It also categorizes signs based on their appearance (human, quadruped, watery) and their manner of rising on the horizon.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the technical categorization of the zodiac signs (Rashis) and transitions into the significations (Karakatvas) of the twelve astrological houses (Bhavas). # Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

...shines with equal and crooked movements, returning again from the house of the sharp-rayed Sun. || 8 ||

Starting from Aries, the signs are described as Movable <term>Chara|Movable</term>, Fixed <term>Sthira|Fixed</term>, and Dual <term>Ubhaya|Dual</term> <note>This refers to the three "modalities" of signs: Aries is movable, Taurus is fixed, Gemini is dual, and the pattern repeats.</note>. They are also known as the "Door," "Interior," and "Exterior" houses respectively. They are further classified as Mineral, Vegetable, and Animal <note>original: "Dhatu, Moola, Jeeva." Used to determine the nature of objects or life forms involved in a query.</note>, and are known as Cruel <term>Krura|Harsh</term> and Gentle <term>Saumya|Beneficent</term> signs. The signs starting from Aries, along with their trines, rule the directions starting from the East. The odd-numbered signs are Male and the even-numbered signs are Female. || 9 ||

The Ascendant <term>Lagna|The Rising Sign</term> is also called the "Hour" <term>Hora</term>, the "Health," the "Body," and the "Rising." It signifies one's physical form, the head, the present time, and birth. The Second House signifies wealth, education, food and drink, enjoyment, the right eye, the face, documents, speech, and family. || 10 ||

The Third House <note>original: "Duschikya." A technical term for the 3rd house.</note> signifies the right ear, the army, courage, valor, prowess, and brothers. The Fourth House signifies the home, fields <note>meaning landed property</note>, maternal uncles, nephews, relatives, friends, vehicles, and the mother. || 11 ||

The Tenth House signifies the kingdom <note>status or career</note>, cattle, buffaloes, fragrances, clothes, and ornaments. The Fourth House is also called the "Netherworld" <term>Patala</term> and "Hibuka," signifying happiness, nectar-like things, bridges, and rivers. The Fifth House signifies royal decrees, ministers, taxes, one’s own intellect, knowledge of the future, the life-force, children, the stomach, and the study of scriptures and memory. || 12 ||

The Sixth House signifies debts, weapons, thieves, wounds, diseases, enemies, battles, evil deeds, sins, fear, and contempt. The Seventh House <note>original: "Jamitra" and "Dyuna."</note> signifies the mind, excellent things, desires, the public, the husband’s path, and the wife. || 13 ||

The Eighth House signifies <note>original: "Mangalya."</note> auspiciousness for women <note>meaning the longevity of the husband</note>, impurities, defeat, lifespan, distress, scandal, death, impurity, obstacles, and servants...

> Sorrow, the feet, the left eye, decline, indicators of loss,

>

> 
> The eighth, sixth <note>original: "ripu" meaning enemy</note>, and twelfth houses are called "Difficult Houses" <term>Dusthana|Malefic house</term>; the other houses are designated as "Good Houses" <term>Susthana|Benefic house</term> which grant auspicious results.
> 
> The Ascendant, the tenth, the seventh, and the fourth houses are called the "Center" <term>Kendra|Angle</term>, also known by the names "Thorn" <term>Kantaka</term> and "The Four Squares" <term>Chatushtaya</term>. || 17 ||
> 
> The houses immediately following the Centers are called "Succedent" <term>Panaphara</term>; the ones following those are "Cadent" <term>Apoklima</term>.
> 
> Furthermore, the eighth and the fourth houses are called "The Square" <term>Chaturasra</term>; the eighth is also the "Death" house <term>Naidhana</term> and the fourth is the "Kinsman" house <term>Bandhubha</term>.

> Now, the houses of growth <term>Upachaya|The 3rd, 6th, 10th, and 11th houses</term> are the Sky <note>original: "vyoma" meaning the 10th house</note>, Valor <note>original: "shaurya" meaning the 3rd house</note>, Enemies <note>original: "ari" meaning the 6th house</note>, and Gains <note>original: "labha" meaning the 11th house</note>.
> 
> The pair consisting of the ninth and the fifth houses is known as the auspicious Trine <term>Trikona|The 1st, 5th, and 9th houses, though here specifically referring to 5 and 9</term>. || 18 ||

## Chapter Two
### Distinctions of the Planets

> The wise should declare the following significations from the Sun <note>original: "Arka"</note>:
> 
> The physical self, wealth, the father, auspicious results, the soul's happiness, and power.
> 
> Courage, valor, victory in assemblies, service to the king <note>modern equivalent: government service</note>, and fame.
> 
> Rituals for Shiva, traveling in forests and mountains, inclination toward fire sacrifices <term>Homa|Vedic fire rituals</term>,
> 
> Temples, sharpness of mind, and enthusiasm. || 1 ||

> From the Moon <note>original: "Chandra"</note>, one should speak of:
> 
> The well-being of the mother, peace of mind, bathing in the ocean, the white fly-whisk <note>a symbol of royalty</note>,
> 
> The ceremonial umbrella, good people, fruits, gentleness, flowers, crops, and agriculture.
> 
> Reputation, pearls, bell-metal <note>original: "kansya"</note>, silver, sweet things, milk, clothing, water, cows,
> 
> The acquisition of a wife, pleasant meals, physical comfort, and beauty. || 2 ||

...clothing, happiness from a wife, perfumes, flowers, sexual activities, the bedroom and the home,

Prosperity, the joy of poetry, association with many women, grace, passion,

The role of a minister, witty speech, and the celebration of marriage are all known from Venus <note>original: "Bhṛguja," meaning "the descendant of Bhrigu," a common name for the planet Venus</note>. || 6 ||

Longevity, death, fear, degradation, misery, humiliation, illness,

Poverty, the reproach of servitude, sins, impurity, and accidents,

Steadiness, seeking refuge with low-minded people, buffaloes, donkeys, debt, iron,

Slavery, agricultural tools, persistence in work, and imprisonment are known from Saturn <note>original: "Ravisuta," meaning "Son of the Sun"</note>. || 7 ||

He is of a bilious <term>Pitta|One of the three bodily humors in Ayurveda, associated with fire, heat, and digestion</term> nature with strong bones, thin hair, and a reddish-dark complexion with honey-gold eyes.

Wearing saffron-colored clothes, his body is square-shaped; he is brave, fierce, broad-shouldered, and noble <note>This verse describes the physical appearance of the Sun</note>. || 8 ||

He is robust, appearing both young and old <note>This refers to the Moon’s changing phases, waxing and waning</note>, white in color, with beautiful eyes and dark, fine hair.

Rich in blood, soft-spoken, wearing white garments, fair-skinned, of a windy and phlegmatic <term>Vata-Kapha|Ayurvedic humors relating to air/movement and water/structure respectively</term> nature, and gentle. || 9 ||

Slender at the waist, with curly and radiant hair, cruel eyes, a bilious nature, and a fierce intellect...

<page-num>9</page-num>

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

...Dressed in red, with a reddish body, possessing great vitality, fierce, very generous, youthful, and rich in marrow <term>Majjā|One of the seven tissues (dhatus) in Ayurveda, referring to bone marrow and nerve tissue</term>. || 10 ||

His body has the dark-green hue of Durva grass; he is a mixture of the three humors <note>Referring to the Ayurvedic Vata, Pitta, and Kapha</note>, with visible veins and sweet speech. With long, reddish eyes, wearing green garments, having strong skin, Mercury is slender, radiant, and well-proportioned. || 11 ||

He has a golden luster, with brownish hair and eyes, a broad and raised chest, and a large body. Possessing a phlegmatic <term>Kapha|The Ayurvedic humor associated with water and earth, stability and lubrication</term> nature, of superior intellect, worshiped by the gods, having a voice like a lion or a deep drum, and focused on wealth and excellence. || 12 ||

Wearing multicolored clothes, with curly black hair, a stout body, and a phlegmatic and windy <term>Kapha-Vata|Ayurvedic humors relating to water and air</term> nature. He has the complexion of a Durva sprout, is handsome, has large eyes, and is the master of increasing reproductive essence <note>original: "Shukra," meaning Venus and also referring to the reproductive tissue in Ayurveda</note>. || 13 ||

He is lame, with sunken eyes, a thin and tall body, prominent veins, and a lazy disposition. Dark-bodied, of a windy <term>Vata|The Ayurvedic humor associated with air and movement</term> nature, very deceitful, dominated by the sinews, and pitiless. Foolish, with large nails and teeth, coarse hair, impure, driven by inertia <term>Tamas|One of the three Gunas (qualities), associated with darkness, lethargy, and ignorance</term>, fierce, prone to anger, looking aged, and dressed in black is the son of the Sun <note>original: "Bhaskari," a common name for Saturn</note>. || 14 ||

The place of the Sun is a temple of Shiva, an open illuminated place, or a desert; his direction is the East. For the Moon, it is a fort, a cremation ground, a dusty place, a place of medicinal herbs, or a place where honey is kept; her direction is the Northwest.

<meta>This page continues the descriptions of planetary associations from page 9. <page-num>10</page-num>

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

The dwellings of Mars are said to be among thieves, outcasts, places of fire, and battlefields; his direction is the South <term>Dakshina|The southern direction, associated with Yama, the god of death, and Mars in Vedic astrology</term>. || 15 ||

The North <term>Uttara|The northern direction, associated with wealth and Mercury</term> is known as the place for scholars, Brahmins, assemblies, pleasure gardens, and mathematicians or astrologers.

The dwellings of Jupiter are treasure-houses, Peepal trees, temples, and the residences of the twice-born <note>original: "dvija"; referring to the upper three castes, particularly Brahmins, who have undergone the sacred thread ceremony</note>; his direction is the North-East <term>Ishanya|The direction governed by Ishana (Shiva), considered the most auspicious corner</term>. || 16 ||

The South-East <term>Agneya|The direction of the fire god, Agni</term> is the direction of Venus, and his places are the streets where courtesans live, harems, and bedrooms.

The West <term>Varuna|The direction governed by Varuna, the god of the oceans</term> is the direction of Saturn, and his dwellings are the houses of the low-born and filthy places.

Anthills, snake holes, and dark pits are the places associated with the shadow planet Ketu <note>original: "Shikhi"; a name for the descending lunar node, Ketu</note>. || 17 ||

Followers of Shiva, physicians, kings, performers of Vedic sacrifices, and ministers are associated with the Sun. So too are the tiger, the deer, and the Chakravaka bird.

Those who know the scriptures, washermen, and manual laborers are associated with the Moon. So too are the rabbit, the deer, the crane, and the Chakora bird. || 18 ||

Mars is associated with the kitchen, those who carry weapons, and goldsmiths.

Goats, roosters, jackals, monkeys, vultures, and thieves are also governed by Mars. |

<page-num>11</page-num>

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

...cowherds, eunuchs, artisans, the best of accountants <term>Ganaka|In this context, it refers to both mathematicians and astrologers who "count" the movements of the stars</term>, devotees of Vishnu, teachers, blue jays <note>original: "kiki"</note>, parrots, and cats are associated with the Moon-born <term>Shashija|A common name for Mercury, who in Vedic mythology is the son of the Moon</term>. || 18 ||

Astrologers, ministers, teachers, Brahmins, and the leaders of ascetics are governed by the Teacher of the Gods <term>Suraguru|A title for Jupiter, who serves as the preceptor and counselor to the deities</term>. His animals are pigeons, horses, and swans.

Singers, the wealthy, gallants, merchants, actors, weavers, and courtesans—along with peacocks, buffaloes, parrots, and cows—are the domain of the son of Bhrigu <term>Bhrigu|Venus, named after the great sage Bhrigu</term>. || 19 ||

Oil-sellers, servants, the low-born, hunters, blacksmiths, elephants, crows, and cuckoos belong to Saturn.

Followers of the Buddha <note>original: "Bauddha"; used here to denote those outside the traditional Vedic fold, often associated with the shadow planets</note>, enemies, fire-worshippers, donkeys, goats, wolves, camels, snakes, darkness, mosquitoes, bedbugs, and owls are associated with the nodes <note>referring to Rahu and Ketu</note>. || 20 ||

Mercury <term>Saumya|The "gentle" or "placid" one, a name for Mercury</term> is neutral toward Saturn and Venus. The Sun's enemies are Saturn and Venus. The Moon's friends are the Sun and Mercury, while the others are neutral. For Mars, Saturn and Venus are neutral, but Jupiter is an enemy... <note>This marks the beginning of the "Nisarga Maitri" or Natural Friendship rules, where planets are classified as friends, enemies, or neutrals to one another based on their inherent nature.</note>

<meta>This page continues the technical classification of planetary relationships (friendships and enmities) and introduces the concepts of planetary "vision" or aspects, as well as their governance over time, social classes, and the three fundamental qualities of nature.</meta>

<page-num>12</page-num>

## Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

Mercury’s <note>original: "Saumyasya"</note> enemy is the Moon <note>original: "Shitagu"</note>; his friends are Venus <note>original: "Sita"</note> and the Sun <note>original: "Arka"</note>. || 21 ||

For Jupiter <term>Suri|The Wise One, a title for Jupiter</term>, the enemies are Venus and Mercury; Saturn is neutral <note>original: "Samah"</note>. For Venus <note>original: "Kavi"</note>, Mars <note>original: "Kuja"</note> and Jupiter <term>Bhrugu|Another name for Jupiter in this specific neutral context</term> are neutral; Saturn and Mercury are friends. For Saturn <note>original: "Ravija"</note>, Mercury and Venus are friends; Jupiter is neutral. Those planets not mentioned here as friends or neutrals should be understood as enemies of the respective planets. || 22 ||

Planets occupying the 2nd, 3rd, 4th, 10th, 11th, and 12th houses from each other become temporary friends <term>Tatkalika Mitra|Friendship based on current positions in a specific birth chart rather than natural disposition</term>. By combining this with their natural <note>original: "Naisargika"</note> dispositions, one should determine the final relationship—whether they are great friends, friends, neutral, enemies, or great enemies.

Saturn looks with a full "vision" <term>Drishti|The influence or 'glance' a planet casts on other signs</term> upon the 3rd and 10th houses; Jupiter upon the 5th and 9th; and Mars upon the 4th and 8th. All planets look fully at the 7th house from their position. In other houses, their vision increases by quarters <note>meaning they have partial influence on other houses</note> in a gradual sequence. || 23 ||

The time periods governed by the Sun and the other planets are: a solstice <term>Ayana|A six-month period</term> (Sun), a moment <note>original: "kshana"</note> (Moon), a day (Mars), a season (Mercury), a month (Jupiter), a fortnight (Venus), and a year (Saturn). 

The social classes <note>original: "Varnas"</note> are assigned as follows: Venus and Jupiter are Brahmins; the Sun and Mars are Kshatriyas <note>warriors/rulers</note>; the Moon is a Vaishya <note>merchant</note>; and Mercury is a Shudra <note>laborer/artisan</note>; Saturn is the lowest. 

The qualities of nature <term>Guna|The three fundamental psychological and spiritual tendencies</term> are assigned thus: the Moon, Jupiter, and the Sun are declared to be Sattvic <note>pure/harmonious</note>; Venus and Mercury are Rajasic <note>active/passionate</note>; the others <note>Mars and Saturn</note> are Tamasic <note>dark/inert</note>.

<page-num>13</page-num>

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

...Summer is ruled by the Sun <note>original: "Arka"</note> and Mars <note>original: "Kuja"</note>. The Moon <note>original: "Shashi"</note>, Mercury <note>original: "Shashisuta"</note>, Jupiter <note>original: "Jiva"</note>, Saturn <note>original: "Shani"</note>, and Venus <note>original: "Bhargava"</note> [rule the other seasons respectively]. || 14 ||

For those born during the day, the Sun and Venus are considered the indicators of the father and mother. For those born at night, Saturn <note>original: "Prabhakari"</note> and the Moon are known as such. By reversing this order, they represent the paternal uncle and maternal aunt. The Sun is said to be the right eye, and Mars the left <note>the text notes "the other eye"</note>. Jupiter signifies the elder brother, and Mercury <note>original: "Shashisuta"</note> is accepted as the indicator for an adopted son. || 15 ||

The Moon is the body <note>original: "Deha"</note> and the Sun is the soul <note>original: "Dehi"</note>. The Sun, Mars, and Saturn are those that cause affliction to them. Smell is governed by both Venus and the Moon; taste is governed by the Sun and Mars; Jupiter governs sight and sound; the remaining planets are designated as governing the sense of touch. || 16 ||

The waning Moon, Sun, Mars, Rahu <note>original: "Ahi"</note>, Ketu, and Saturn are the "malefic" <term>Papa|Cruel or challenging</term> planets; Mercury is also malefic when associated with them. Ketu, Mercury, and Saturn are neuter; the Moon, Rahu <note>original: "Tamah"</note>, and Venus are female; the others are male. The presiding deities are Shiva <note>original: "Rudra"</note>, Parvati <note>original: "Amba"</note>, Skanda <note>original: "Guha"</note>, Vishnu, Brahma <note>original: "Dhata"</note>, Lakshmi <note>original: "Kamala"</note>, Yama <note>original: "Kala"</note>, the Serpent King <note>original: "Ahi"</note>, and the Unborn <note>original: "Aja"</note>. From the Sun onwards, the planets are composed of Fire, Water, Fire, Earth, Space <note>original: "Kha"</note>, Water, and Air respectively. || 17 ||

Wheat, rice, sesame, chickpeas, horse gram, pigeon pea, millet, and green gram;
Flat beans and black gram are [assigned to] the Sun, Moon, Saturn, Jupiter, Ketu, Mars, Rahu, and Mercury <note>original: "Arka, Indu, Asita, Guru, Shikhi, Krura, Vidhu, Saumya"</note>.

[The regions of] Kalinga, Saurashtra, Avanti, Sindhu, Magadha, the Yavana <note>referring to the Greeks or foreigners to the northwest</note> lands, and the mountain ranges [are also ruled by them]. || 28 ||

Coral <note>original: "Vidruma"</note> for Mars; Emerald <note>original: "Marakata"</note> for Mercury, the son of the Moon;

Yellow Topaz <note>original: "Pushyaraga"</note> for Jupiter, the teacher of the gods; Diamond <note>original: "Vajram"</note> for Venus, the counselor of the titans; Blue Sapphire <note>original: "Nilam"</note> for Saturn; and for the two nodes [Rahu and Ketu], Hessonite <note>original: "Gomedha"</note> and Cat's Eye <note>original: "Vaidurya"</note> are prescribed. || 29 ||

Copper, bronze, brass, tin, gold, silver, and iron are the metals of the Sun and the other planets.
Their garments are of their respective colors; specifically, Saturn's clothing is old or worn <note>original: "jirnam"</note>, and Mars's is singed by fire <note>original: "agnidagdham"</note>. || 30 ||

The taste of the Sun is pungent; that of Mars <note>original: "Bhumisuta"</note> is bitter; the Moon's is salty; that of Mercury <note>original: "Chandraja"</note> is mixed;
Jupiter's is sweet; Venus's is sour; and Saturn's is astringent. || 31 ||

### Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

For the Sun, Jupiter, Mercury, and Mars <note>original: "Bhāsvad-Gīṣpati-Candraja-Kṣitibhū"</note>, the auspicious mark is on the right side. For the remaining planets, it is on the other side. On the head and the back, they possess the nature of the Sun.

In the armpit, the shoulder, the face, and near the feet, there are signs resembling birds. From the Sun's position, the rim [of the planetary circle] did not reach the shore... <note>This verse (32) describes the physiognomy or bodily markings (lakshanam) of the planetary deities used in divination or iconographic identification. The second half contains complex technical terms regarding the "rim" or "shore" of planetary influence.</note> || 32 ||

Blue-hued, tall-statured, of foul complexion, suffering from skin ailments, sinful, and prone to hiccups;
A speaker of untruths, deceptive, big-bellied, a slanderer of others, yet intelligent—such is Rahu. || 33 ||

With red, fierce eyes, poisonous speech and body, armed, and fallen from grace is Ketu;
Smoke-colored <note>original: "Dhumra-dyutih"</note>, a consumer of smoke, always marked by scars, lean, and cruel. || 34 ||

Lead <note>original: "Sīsaṃ"</note> and worn-out garments are prescribed for Rahu <note>referred to here as "Tamas," meaning darkness</note>;
For Ketu, various multi-colored cloths and variegated patterns are ordained.
The Sun and Mercury <note>original: "Arka" and "Arya" (Jupiter/Mercury context)</note> are the friends of Rahu and Ketu;
Mars is said to be neutral, and the rest are their enemies. || 35 ||

<meta>This page concludes the general descriptions of planetary conditions and their associations with the natural world (specifically vegetation) before transitioning into Chapter Three, which details the mathematical divisions of the zodiac signs (Vargas).</meta>

<page-num>16</page-num>

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

Even if a planet is "combust" <note>original: "Mūḍha"; a planet becomes "blinded" or weak when it is too close to the Sun</note>, in its sign of debilitation, in an enemy’s sign, or situated in the 6th, 8th, or 12th houses <term>Duḥsthāna|Malefic houses in a birth chart associated with loss, illness, or obstacles</term>, it is considered "poorly placed." In any other position, it is said to be "well-placed."

If the Moon is situated in the 11th, 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses <note>The OCR reads "tanu-patnu-takaama," likely referring to the 1st (Tanu), 4th (Patnu/Pathala), and 7th (Kaama) houses</note>, it signifies an increase in water levels and indicates success in ventures involving growth and development. || 36 ||

Trees that are tall, reddish, and have a strong inner core are associated with the Sun. Creepers and vines are the domain of Venus <note>original: "Sita"</note>. Shrubs and bushes are linked to the nodes, Ketu and Rahu. Thorny trees are governed by Mars and Saturn.

Jupiter <note>original: "Vāgīśa"</note> is associated with fruit-bearing trees, while Mercury governs fruitless ones. Milky trees and flowering trees are ruled by Venus and the Moon. The Moon is also the lord of medicinal herbs <note>original: "Oṣadhi"</note>. Saturn governs the sturdy Sal tree and plants that lack core strength. || 37 ||

## Chapter Three
### Classification of Divisional Charts

The sign <note>original: "Kṣetra"</note>, the third part <note>Drekkana</note>, the ninth part <note>Navamsha</note>, the tenth part <note>Dashamsha</note>, the half-sign <note>Hora</note>, the thirtieth part <note>Trimshamsha</note>, the seventh part <note>Saptamsha</note>, and the sixtieth part <note>Shashtiamsha</note>, along with the sixteenth part <note>Kalāṃśa</note>...

When these are combined with the twelfth part <note>Dvadashamsha</note>, they are known as the <term>Daśa-varga|The ten-fold division system used to analyze a planet's strength and specific areas of life influence</term>. A planet is called <term>Vargottama|The 'best division'</term> when it occupies the same zodiac sign in both the primary birth chart and the Ninth-part <note>Navamsha</note> chart.

The "Seven-fold divisions" <note>Sapta-varga</note> are these same categories, excluding the tenth, sixteenth, and sixtieth parts.

<summary>This page describes how a planet's strength is judged by its house placement and lunar position, assigns various types of vegetation to the planets, and introduces the Ten-fold system of divisional astrology charts.</summary>
<keywords>astrological houses, combustion, planetary botany, Varga, Dashavarga, Vargottama, lunar influence, vegetation</keywords>

--- Page 17 ---
<meta>This page continues from the previous discussion on the basic qualities of signs and transitions into the mathematical calculation of the Vargas (divisional charts). It defines how planetary strength is distributed across these divisions.</meta>

<page-num>17</page-num>

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

Some authorities state that the results produced by the ninth division <term>Navāmśa|A 1/9th division of a zodiac sign, used to determine the planet's underlying strength and marriage prospects</term> are equal in importance to the results of the entire sign and house position within the system of the six divisions <term>Ṣadvarga|The six primary types of divisional charts: Sign, Hora, Drekkana, Navamsha, Dwadashamsha, and Trimshamsha</term>. || 2 ||

In the primary signs <term>Kṣetra|The base 30-degree zodiac sign</term>, the results are said to be full. In other divisional charts, the results are half. In smaller subdivisions, such as the tenth and sixtieth parts <note>original: "kalādaśamaṣaṣṭilaveṣu"</note>, the results are only a quarter.

A planet's influence also depends on its "age" or state <note>original: "Avasthā"</note>. These states are: Infant, Adolescent, Youth, Old, and Dead. In odd signs <note>Aries, Gemini, etc.</note>, these six-degree increments follow this order. In even signs <note>Taurus, Cancer, etc.</note>, the order is reversed. || 3 ||

A half-sign (15 degrees) is called a <term>Horā</term>. In odd signs, the first half is ruled by the Sun and the second by the Moon. In even signs, this is reversed.
The lords of the three parts <term>Dreṣkāṇa|10-degree divisions, often called Decans</term> are the lords of the 1st, 5th, and 9th signs from the sign in question.
The twelfth part <term>Dvādaśāṃśa|A 2.5-degree division</term> is calculated by counting from the sign itself.

In the thirty divisions <term>Triṃśāṃśa|1-degree divisions used to identify specific character traits</term>, the lords for odd signs are Mars, Saturn, Jupiter, Mercury, and Venus (ruling 5, 5, 8, 7, and 5 degrees respectively). In even signs, the order of lords and degrees is reversed.
The ninth divisions <note>Navāmśa</note> for the signs beginning with Aries, Capricorn, Libra, and Cancer follow their respective sequences. || 4 ||

The following are the names of the sixty divisions <term>Śaṣṭyaṃśa|Half-degree divisions (1/60th of a sign) used for fine-tuning predictions</term> for a planet in an odd sign:

1. Sacrificial <note>original: "Yajñyam"</note>
2. Jewel <note>original: "Ratna"</note>
3. People <note>original: "Janam"</note>
4. Wealth <note>original: "Dhanam"</note>
5. Justice/Policy <note>original: "Naya"</note>
6. Cloth <note>original: "Paṭam"</note>
7. Form/Beauty <note>original: "Rūpam"</note>
8. Parrot/Pure <note>original: "Śukam"</note>
9. Small/Servant <note>original: "Ciṭina"</note>
10. Elephant/Serpent <note>original: "Nāgam"</note>
11. Union <note>original: "Yoga"</note>
12. Fortune/Beauty <note>original: "Bhagam"</note>
13. Strength <note>original: "Balam"</note>
14. Destiny/Part <note>original: "Bhaga"</note>
15. Stone <note>original: "Śilā"</note>
16. Dust <note>original: "Dhūli"</note>
17. New <note>original: "Navam"</note>
18. Sound <note>original: "Prasvanam"</note>
19. Gain <note>original: "Lābham"</note>
20. Universal <note>original: "Viśva"</note>
21. Divine <note>original: "Divam"</note>
22. Pleasing <note>original: "Rama"</note>
23. Resonant <note>original: "Dhama"</note>...

<meta>This page continues the technical breakdown of the divisional charts (Vargas) from page 17. It concludes the rules for the 30th division (Trimshāmsha) and introduces the calculations for the 7th and 16th divisions, as well as the naming conventions for planetary strength across multiple charts.</meta>

<page-num>18</page-num>
<header>Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series</header>

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

In even signs, this order is reversed for the two cruel planets <note>Mars and Saturn</note>; the remaining lords are known as the gentle ones <note>Jupiter, Mercury, and Venus</note>. || 5 ||

The seventh division <term>Saptāṃśa|A 4°17' division used to study children and descendants</term> is calculated starting from the sign itself in odd signs, and from the seventh sign thereafter in even signs.

For the sixteenth division <term>Kalāṃśa or Shodashāmsha|A 1°52' division used to study vehicles and general luxuries</term>, the lords of the divisions in odd signs are Brahma, Vishnu, Shiva, and the Sun <note>original: "vidhi-hari-īśa-arkāḥ"</note>; in even signs, the order is reversed. Authorities have described systems consisting of seven, ten, and thirteen divisions based on these calculations. || 6 ||

One should combine the thirteen divisions <note>referring to the Saptavarga, Dashavarga, or Shodashavarga systems</note> when a planet occupies a friendly sign, its own sign, or its exaltation sign. Depending on the frequency—two, three, five, four, seven, seven, eight, or six times—different names are given to the planet's state.
These are known as: **Parijata**, **Uttama**, **Gopura**, **Simhasana**, **Devaloka**, **Suraloka**, and **Paravata**. || 7 ||

If a planet reaches the **Parijata** degree <note>meaning it is well-placed in two divisional charts</note>, the person will enjoy traditional virtues, wealth, and happiness.
If it is in the **Uttama** degree <note>well-placed in three charts</note>, the person follows good conduct, is filled with humility, and is highly skilled.
A planet in the **Gopura** section grants a beautiful intellect and ownership of lands or houses.
One in the **Simhasana** <note>literally "Throne"</note> section makes a person beloved by the greatest of kings or even equal to a king themselves. || 8 ||

<page-num>19</page-num>

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

One who has a planet in the **Paravata** state <note>original: "pārāvatādhiṣṭhitaḥ"; a planet well-placed in eight divisional charts</note> attains the splendor of excellent horses, elephants, and vehicles.
If the planet is in the **Devaloka** state <note>well-placed in seven divisional charts</note>, along with the favor of the gods, the person attains great fame and becomes a ruler of the earth.
One whose planet is in the **Airavata** state <note>original: "trairāvatāṃśasthitaḥ"; well-placed in nine divisional charts</note> is worshipped by kings, resembles the King of Gods <note>Indra</note>, and possesses great fortune, wealth, grain, and sons. || 9 ||

---

I shall now describe in order the results of planets being weak or strong across all divisions.
If the divisions are entirely weak, it results in death, sorrow, worthlessness, and a lack of happiness.
If the divisions are entirely strong, the person becomes a favorite of kings, wealthy, a noble king, or the best of rulers.
Growth, happiness, and royalty, or disease and death, are the results determined by the planetary states such as infancy <term>Bālādi Avasthas|A system where planetary strength is judged by its 'age' in a sign: Infant, Youthful, Adolescent, Old, or Dead</term>. || 10 ||

---

If a person has auspicious planets and qualities in the six divisions <term>Ṣaḍvarga|The six primary divisional charts: Rashi, Hora, Drekkana, Navamsha, Dwadashamsha, and Trimshamsha</term>, they will be prosperous and live a long life.
If cruel portions <note>influences of Mars or Saturn</note> predominate in the Ascendant <term>Lagna|The sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of birth</term>, the person will be poor and short-lived.
If the lords of those divisions are strong, if the Navamsha lord, the Drekkana lord, and the Ascendant lord are progressively strong, the person is happy, equal to a king, a lord of the earth, and full of virtues. || 11 ||

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of planetary divisions from page 19, moving from the general concept of "Vargas" (divisional charts) to the specific analysis of the Hora (half-sign division) and the Drekkana (one-third sign division). It details how the specific qualities of these divisions—whether they are "cruel," "auspicious," or resemble specific animals—influence the wealth, appearance, and lifespan of the individual.</meta>

<page-num>20</page-num>

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

If the Sun occupies its own **Hora** <term>Horā|A division of a zodiac sign into two equal parts of 15 degrees each; the first half of an odd sign is ruled by the Sun, the second by the Moon</term> in an odd sign, the individual becomes powerful, fierce, and very wealthy.
If the Moon's division in an even sign is occupied and aspected by auspicious planets, the person is endowed with radiance, humility, pleasant speech, and great beauty.
If these positions are reversed <note>meaning the Moon's division in an odd sign or the Sun's division in an even sign</note>, or if the influences are mixed, the results are said to be mediocre. If both the **Ascendant** <term>Lagna|The specific point of the zodiac rising on the eastern horizon at the time of birth</term> and the Moon are strong, and their respective rulers are also powerful, the person will live a long life, be free from sorrow, and attain great fame. || 12 ||

---

In the signs of Leo, Aries, Sagittarius, and Libra, the first **Drekkana** <term>Drekkāna|A division of a zodiac sign into three equal parts of 10 degrees each, often represented by symbolic figures</term> is human-like. Others are described as having the heads of horses or goats.
In the middle of Gemini and Virgo, the figures are depicted as bearing weapons. A "Noose" <note>original: "pāśa"</note> is found in the middle of Scorpio.
The first part of Capricorn is known as the "Fetters" <note>original: "nigala"</note>. The first parts of Leo and Aquarius are "Vulture-faced." The final part of Taurus is a "Bird," and the beginning of Cancer is "Boar-faced." || 13 ||

---

The first part of Scorpio, the end of Cancer, the end of Capricorn, and the middle of Leo are considered "Quadrupeds" <note>four-legged creatures</note>. Those born under these influences are said to be poor, cruel, or held in low regard.
Experts in this science say that the three decanates result in low, middling, or excellent fruits respectively. In fixed signs, the results are said to be auspicious, mediocre, or inauspicious. Even in fixed signs, the results of the Ascendant are judged as auspicious, mediocre, or inauspicious based on these specific divisions. || 14 ||

---

If the Lord of the Decanate is in its own division, or is associated with auspicious planets, or is in its sign of exaltation or a friendly sign...

<note>The text concludes mid-sentence, likely continuing on the following page to explain the specific benefits of well-placed decanate lords.</note>

<page-num>21</page-num>

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

Similarly, if the ruler of the **Trimsamsa** <term>Triṃśāṃśa|A divisional chart where a sign is divided into thirty parts; it is traditionally used to assess a person's character, misfortunes, and specific traits</term> is strong, or if the ruler of the **Dwadasamsa** <term>Dvādaśāṃśa|A division of a zodiac sign into twelve equal parts of 2.5 degrees each, often linked to ancestral influences</term> is strong, or if the Lord of the **Hora** <term>Horā|A division of a sign into two halves; the ruler of this division influences wealth and temperament</term> is powerful, the individual will be endowed with a multitude of virtues, remain eternally pure and skilled, live a long life, be compassionate, and be blessed with children, wealth, fame, and the enjoyments of a king. || 15 ||

The **Ascendant** <term>Lagna|The sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of birth, representing the self</term> is said to be located in a trine <note>original: "trikoṇa"; the 1st, 5th, or 9th positions from a sign</note> relative to the lord of the sign occupied by **Mandi** <term>Māndi|Also called Gulika, a sensitive mathematical point in Vedic astrology considered a "shadow planet" or the offspring of Saturn; it is used to verify the accuracy of the birth time</term>. Alternatively, it may be in a trine relative to the lord of the sign that Mandi occupies in the **Navamsa** <term>Navāṃśa|The 1/9th division of a sign, considered the most important sub-chart for evaluating a planet's true strength</term> chart. If the Moon is particularly strong, one should determine these positions from the Moon instead of Mandi. || 16 ||

The Moon, when situated in its own or a friend's division, bestows a beautiful appearance and auspicious character. One born in the highest divisions <note>vargas</note> acquires excellent qualities, while in other divisions, they take on the traits of the respective lords. Planets situated in their own **Trimsamsa** manifest the qualities they naturally signify. If even a single planet is aspected by or joined with a friendly planet while in its sign of exaltation, the person becomes a wealthy ruler. || 17 ||

A planet in its sign of exaltation is described as "Blazing" <note>original: "pradīpta"</note>; in its **Moola-trikona** <term>Mūlatrikoṇa|A specific range of degrees in a sign where a planet is exceptionally strong, though slightly less than its exaltation point</term> it is "Happy" <note>original: "sukhita"</note>; in its own home it is "At Ease" <note>original: "svastha"</note>; and in a friend's sign it is "Joyful" <note>original: "mudita"</note>.

<summary>This page details how the strength of planetary lords in various sub-divisions (vargas) like the Trimsamsa and Dwadasamsa grants virtues and wealth. It also provides an astrological formula using the point 'Mandi' to verify the correct rising sign (Lagna) and introduces the emotional states of planets based on their positions.</summary>

<page-num>22</page-num>

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

A planet is considered **Peaceful** <note>original: "Śānta"</note> when it is located in the divisions of benefic planets. It is considered **Powerful** <note>original: "Śakta"</note> when its web of rays is clearly visible and luminous. || 18 ||

A planet defeated in planetary war is called **Oppressed** <note>original: "Nipīḍita"</note>. One located in the division of a malefic planet is **Wicked** <note>original: "Khala"</note>. A planet residing in the house of an enemy is **Exceedingly Sorrowful** <note>original: "Sudukhita"</note>. When in its sign of debilitation, it is **Terrified** <note>original: "Atibhīta"</note>, and when it is combust <note>original: "astayāta"; disappearing in the sun's glare</note>, it is termed **Mutilated** <note>original: "Vikala"</note>. || 19 ||

The auspicious results are full when a planet is **Radiant** <note>original: "Pradīpta"; in its exaltation</note> and nil when it is **Mutilated**. For the states in between, the results are determined proportionally. These planets produce good or bad effects that correspond exactly to their names. || 20 ||

## Chapter Four
### Planetary Strength

The strength <term>Bala|The calculation of a planet's potency to produce results</term> of the celestial bodies is said to be of six kinds: **Temporal** strength <note>Kālaja Bala</note>, **Motility** strength <note>Ceṣṭā Bala</note>, **Exaltation** strength <note>Svoccaja Bala</note>, **Directional** strength <note>Digbala</note>, **Solstitial** strength <note>Ayanabala</note>, and **Positional** strength <note>Sthānabala</note>.

Mars, the Moon, and Venus are strong during the night; the others (the Sun, Jupiter, and Saturn) are strong during the day. Mercury is always powerful, regardless of day or night. Benefic planets are strong during the bright half of the lunar month, while others are strong during the dark half. They also gain strength according to their respective year, month, day, and hour of the birth. || 1 ||

The Full Moon possesses **Motility strength**, as does the Sun when it is in its northern course <term>Uttarāyaṇa|The six-month period when the sun moves north, considered spiritually auspicious</term>. Retrograde planets <term>Vakra|Planets that appear to move backward from Earth's perspective</term>, planets victorious in planetary war, and those with abundant luster also possess this strength. **Exaltation strength** is attained when a planet is in its highest point of influence...

<summary>This page defines the various emotional and physical states of planets (such as 'Terrified' or 'Radiant') and introduces the "Shadbala" system—the six traditional methods for calculating a planet's mathematical strength in a horoscope.</summary>
<keywords>Shadbala, planetary strength, planetary states, Motility strength, Temporal strength, Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao, astrology</keywords>

--- Page 23 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the six-fold planetary strengths (Shadbala), moving from the general introduction of the categories on page 22 to the specific rules for calculating Directional, Positional, and Motility strength.</meta>

<page-num>23</page-num>

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

Directional strength <term>Digbala|The strength a planet gains by being in a specific cardinal direction or house</term> is gained by the Sun and Mars when they are in the tenth house <note>the Zenith</note>. The Moon and Venus gain this strength in the fourth house <note>the Nadir or "Heart"</note>. Mercury and Jupiter possess it when located in the Ascendant <note>the first house or East</note>, and Saturn is known to be strong in the seventh house <note>the West</note>. || 2 ||

Positional strength <term>Sthānabala|Strength derived from a planet's zodiacal placement</term> is said to be possessed by planets when they are in their signs of exaltation, their own signs, the signs of their friends, or within their six favorable divisions <note>vargas</note>. This strength is full when in a quadrant <note>houses 1, 4, 7, and 10</note>, half in the succedent houses, and a quarter in the cadent houses. 

Planets classified as masculine, neuter, and feminine are powerful at the beginning, middle, and end of a sign respectively. In terms of inherent natural strength <note>Naisargika Bala</note>, Saturn, Mars, Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, the Moon, and the Sun are increasingly powerful in that order <note>meaning the Sun is naturally the most potent and Saturn the least</note>. || 3 ||

A planet that has entered a retrograde motion <term>Vakra|Apparent backward motion of a planet</term> is full of brilliant rays and is powerful even if it is located in its sign of debilitation or in an enemy's sign. 

Conversely, a planet—even if located in its sign of exaltation, a friend's house, or its own sign—is considered weak if its rays are lost <note>Hata-dīdhiti; referring to combustion by the Sun or being "defeated" in a planetary war</note>, just as the Moon appears weak when its light is diminished. || 4 ||

All planets are powerful when in their signs of exaltation. For the Moon, the strength of its lunar phase <note>Pakṣa-bala; whether it is waxing or full</note> is highly praised. The Sun's directional strength is its primary power, while for other planets, their strength is derived from their retrograde motion and their eclipses <note>Grahaṇa</note>.

<summary>This page details the specific calculations for Directional, Positional, and Inherent planetary strengths, emphasizing that retrograde motion can override debilitation while combustion can nullify exaltation.</summary>
<keywords>Digbala, Sthānabala, Retrograde, Exaltation, Quadrants, Shadbala, Planetary Strength</keywords>

<page-num>24</page-num>

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

Ketu is strong in Cancer, Aries, Aquarius, Scorpio, Taurus, Leo, and the latter half of Sagittarius. It is also powerful in the western direction, during the appearance of a "Parivesha" <note>original: "పరివేష"; a celestial halo or atmospheric circle around the Sun or Moon</note>, and during night-time conjunctions of the Sun and Moon <note>referring to New Moons or eclipses</note>. || 5 ||

Human signs <term>Manushabha|Zodiac signs represented by human figures, such as Gemini, Virgo, Libra, the first half of Sagittarius, and Aquarius</term> possess one full unit <note>original: "Rupa"</note> of strength. The sign Scorpio and the second halves of other signs possess half strength. The strength of a zodiac sign is also considered equal to the strength of its planetary ruler. If the ruler is located in an 'Upachaya' house <note>the 3rd, 6th, 10th, or 11th houses, which are associated with growth and improvement</note>, the sign becomes exceptionally potent.

Signs are also considered powerful if they are occupied or aspected <note>viewed</note> by their own lords, by Jupiter, or by Mercury, provided they are not influenced by other planets. Certain signs are said to be strong at night, while the remaining ones are strong during the day. || 6 ||

A planet's strength is full in its sign of exaltation, three-quarters in its 'Moolatrikona' <note>a specific favorable sign placement slightly less powerful than exaltation</note>, one-half in its own sign, and one-quarter in a friend's sign. In an enemy's sign, the strength is very slight. When a planet is in its sign of debilitation <note>its weakest point</note> or is combust <note>original: "Asta"; rendered invisible by proximity to the Sun</note>, its strength is considered fruitlessly lost. || 7 ||

The strength of planets located in the quadrants <term>Kendras|The four foundational houses of a chart: the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th</term> is recognized as follows: their power is said to increase progressively in the order of the Ascendant, the fourth house, the seventh house, and the tenth house. || 8 ||

The seventh aspect <term>Drishti|The influence a planet exerts on the house directly opposite to it (180 degrees away)</term> is always to be called the most excellent and significant. Other partial aspects are not considered as significant. Some authorities do not acknowledge "special aspects" as being effective in the formation of planetary combinations <term>Yogas</term> because they yield diminished results. || 9 ||

**Natural friendship and enmity** between planets should be the proper standard for correctly determining the results they produce.

The temporal relationship between planets should be judged based on their current positions; such friendship or enmity is temporary and not permanent. || 10 || <note>In Vedic astrology, planets have 'natural' friendships, but their 'temporal' (Tatkalika) relationship changes based on which houses they occupy relative to each other in a specific birth chart.</note>

In the removal of all defects and the increase of auspiciousness, the strength of Jupiter <term>Guru|The planet Jupiter, regarded as the Great Benefic and teacher of the gods</term> is greater than all other planets. Mercury and Venus possess a quarter and a half of that strength respectively. This strength is considered the foundational seed of the strength of all planets. || 11 ||

->**The Moon's Activities and States**<-

->**Results of the Moon's Activities**<-

One who is fallen from their position, one who has lost their wealth, one who is devoted to the wives of others, a gambler, a leader of elephants;

In <term>Jataka|The study of birth charts or Nativity</term>, in <term>Muhurta|The selection of auspicious moments for starting activities</term>, and in <term>Prashna|Horary astrology, where a chart is cast to answer a specific question</term>, the activities of the Moon and other such factors provide accurate results; therefore, one should contemplate them with special care. || 20 ||

They say that for the Moon, the strength arising from its zodiac sign is particularly significant, whereas for the other planets, the strength arising from their specific residential position is superior. When combined with other factors, this strength increases significantly; many other similar factors should also be considered. || 21 ||

### The Mass of Strength (Bala-pinda)

The Sun is strong with ten and a half units; the Moon with fifteen; and Mars, the son of the Earth, with seven. The strength of Mercury, the son of the Moon, is like that of the Sun <note>original: "రవివద్" (ravivad); meaning 10.5 units.</note>, while Jupiter's is five and a half. Venus is also strong; || 22 ||

Saturn's strength is five. These are the components of the <term>Shadbala|The traditional Hindu system of calculating six types of planetary strengths</term>; if these are not combined, errors in prediction will occur. Thus, having well-considered the relative strengths and weaknesses of the planets, one should announce the results. || 23 ||

When the total strength of the lord of the <term>Lagna|The Ascendant or the sign rising on the eastern horizon at birth</term> and other houses is increased by one unit, it is called the <term>Bala-pinda|The 'Total Mass of Strength' used for longevity and power calculations</term>. One must also consider the directional strength of the house and the influence of planetary aspects. || 24 ||

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

## Chapter Five

### Section on Determining Livelihood

One should predict the acquisition of wealth based on the strength of the planets situated in the <term>Karmasthana|The tenth house of a horoscope, representing career, status, and one's actions in the world</term> from the Sun or the Moon. This wealth may come from the father, mother, enemies, friends, brothers, or through women's property, or even from servants. Among the Sun, the <term>Lagna|The Ascendant or rising sign</term>, and the Moon, the strongest determines the primary influence. Experts in this science say that one's livelihood is governed by the lord of the sign where the ruler of the tenth house is placed in the <term>Navamsha|The ninth divisional chart, used to see the hidden potential and true results of a planet</term> chart. || 1 ||

If the tenth lord is in the Sun’s division <note>original: "పద్మజవల్లభాంశ" (padmajavallabha-amsha); refers to the Sun's portion</note>, the person lives by fruit-bearing trees, gold or fire-related work, acts of bravery, through knowledge, blankets, or medicine. One might also earn through various religious rituals or by being honored by rulers. || 2 ||

If the tenth lord is in the Moon's division, the person earns through the trade of water-born products <note>such as pearls, coral, or shells</note>, agricultural activities, products derived from cows and buffaloes, traveling to holy pilgrimage sites, through the support of women, or by the sale of clothing. || 3 ||

If the tenth lord is in the division of Mars, the person earns through metals, gems, or physical combat. Income may come from fire-based industries, medicines, or through causing distress to others, using weapons, and acts of daring. One might also find livelihood by serving foreigners <note>original: "మ్లేచ్ఛ" (mleccha); historically referring to those outside the Vedic culture, often implying outsiders or non-traditional sectors</note> or through the work of an informant or a thief. || 4 ||

<summary>This page begins the fifth chapter on career and livelihood, explaining how to determine a person's profession by analyzing the tenth house and the Navamsha positions of the Sun, Moon, and Mars.</summary>

<keywords>Astrology, Livelihood, Tenth House, Navamsha, Sun, Moon, Mars, Career Prediction, Vedic Astrology</keywords>

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

If the tenth lord is in the division of Mercury <note>original: "Budhamsha"</note>, the person’s livelihood arises from poetry, scriptures, writing, and administrative skills. It also comes from the knowledge of astronomy and astrology, or from teaching the Vedas for others, spiritual chanting, and through the role of a family priest. || 5 ||

If the tenth lord is in the division of Jupiter <note>original: "Jivamsha"</note>, one gains support from scholars and divine institutions, or through the grace of rulers. Success is found in teaching the Puranas <note>ancient legendary lore</note>, scriptures, traditional knowledge, ethics, and religious instruction, or through the practice of money-lending <term>Kusida|The traditional practice of lending money for interest, often viewed as a legitimate mercantile profession</term>. || 6 ||

If the tenth lord is in the division of Venus <note>original: "Shukramsha"</note>, wealth is gained through associations with women, or through gold, buffaloes, elephants, and horses. It also comes from dealing in perfumes, silver, dairy products, jewelry, and fine textiles, or by serving as a minister or through poetic talent. || 7 ||

If the tenth lord is in the division of Saturn <note>original: "Shanyamsha"</note>, livelihood is derived from roots, fruits, and through physical labor or serving others. It may involve working with low-status individuals, carrying heavy loads, or even through questionable or dishonest paths. One may also find work in woodcraft, sculpting, or other manual arts. || 8 ||

If the lord of the Navamsha division is strong, wealth is obtained effortlessly; if the lord is weak, the predicted results will be meager. The results manifest during the planetary period <term>Dasha|A system of planetary cycles used to time the events of a person's life</term> associated with the ruler of the tenth house. If the ruler is joined with or aspected by other planets, the wealth is earned in one's own country; if not, it is earned in a foreign land. If the lord of the Navamsha is in a fixed sign, the wealth is firmly established in one's own country. || 9 ||

### Section on Planetary Combinations (Yogadhyaya)

<meta>This page transitions from the planetary divisional influences discussed on page 30 to a specialized section on the "Pancha Mahapurusha Yogas"—the five combinations of "Great Men." While the previous page focused on the tenth house and livelihood, this page defines the specific character traits and physical markings associated with the five primary planetary yogas formed by Mars, Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, and Saturn.</meta>

<page-num>31</page-num>
<header>Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series</header>

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

The five yogas known as Ruchaka, Bhadra, Hamsa, Malavya, and Shasha are famously proclaimed. One should declare these respectively when the five planets starting with Mars <note>original: "Kshitisuta," referring to Mars as the 'Son of the Earth'</note> are situated in their own signs or in their signs of exaltation, while also being placed in a quadrant <term>Kendra|The 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses of a birth chart, considered the pillars of strength</term>. || 1 ||

In the **Ruchaka Yoga**, the person has a long face, attains wealth through many courageous acts, is a hero, a destroyer of enemies, and is powerful. They are highly eminent, famous for their good qualities, a commander of an army, and always victorious. 

In the **Bhadra Yoga**, the person is long-lived, possesses an intellect as sharp as the tip of kusha grass <note>A metaphor for extreme precision and sharpness of mind</note>, is pure, and is praised by the learned. Such a person becomes a ruler, possesses great wealth, and is a master who subdues chaos. || 2 ||

In the **Hamsa Yoga**, the person is a ruler who possesses fine elephants. They are marked on their hands and feet with signs of the conch, lotus, fish, and elephant goad <note>original: "Shankha-Abja-Matsya-Ankusha"; these are traditional auspicious symbols in Indian physiognomy</note>. They have a beautiful body, enjoy wholesome food, and are righteous.

In the **Malavya Yoga**, the person has well-developed limbs, is courageous, and wealthy. They are blessed with the good fortune of children and daughters-in-law, and they prosper. They enjoy comforts, possess fine vehicles and fame, are learned, and have serene senses. || 3 ||

In the **Shasha Yoga**, the person is praised by all people and has the strength of good servants. They may be a village head or a king. Though they may occasionally engage in questionable conduct <note>original: "Durvrittah"</note> or covet the wealth and partners of others, they remain powerful and enjoy a life of happiness.

<page-num>32</page-num>

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

These five yogas, when formed even by the Lord of the Ascendant <term>Lagnesha|The planet that rules the first house of the birth chart</term>, grant the attainment of an empire <note>original: "Samrajyasiddhi"</note>. Among these, if one yoga is present, the person is fortunate; if more are present, they become like a king, a king, or a supreme emperor. || 4 ||

Regarding the Moon <note>original: "Vidhu," a poetic Sanskrit name for the Moon</note>: **Sunapha**, **Anapha**, and **Dhurdhura** yogas are formed by planets—excluding the Sun—occupying the second house, the twelfth house, or both houses from the Moon's position. If no planets are so situated, it is called **Kemadruma**. However, it is said that Kemadruma does not occur if there are planets in the quadrants <term>Kendra|The 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses</term> from the Moon. || 5 ||

In **Sunapha Yoga**, the person earns wealth through their own efforts, is a king or equal to one, and is endowed with intelligence, riches, and fame. In **Anapha Yoga**, the person is a leader, possesses a healthy body, is virtuous and famous, enjoys worldly pleasures, dresses well, and is content. || 6 ||

One born in **Dhurdhura Yoga** is blessed with the enjoyment of pleasures, happiness, fortune, and many vehicles <note>original: "Vahana," historically meaning chariots or elephants, now referring to any modern transport</note>. They are generous and respected in assemblies. In **Kemadruma Yoga**, the person is unkempt, miserable, lowly, and destitute.

<summary>This page concludes the discussion on the Five Great Men yogas and details the four primary lunar yogas—Sunapha, Anapha, Dhurdhura, and Kemadruma—explaining how the Moon's neighboring planets determine a person's wealth, status, and character.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of planetary combinations (yogas) that define a person's character and destiny. While the previous page concluded with the "Kemadruma Yoga"—a combination indicating poverty and hardship—this page finishes that description and transitions into the solar yogas: Vesi, Vasi, and Ubhayachari, which are determined by the position of planets relative to the Sun.</meta>

<page-num>33</page-num>

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

...[they become] servants and wicked people, even if they are born into a royal lineage. || 7 || <note>This completes the description of Kemadruma Yoga from the previous page, where even high birth cannot offset the difficulties of the yoga.</note>

When planets occupy the second house, the twelfth house, or both houses from the Sun <note>original: "Savituh," referring to the Sun God</note>, they form the **Vesi**, **Vasi**, and **Ubhayachari** yogas respectively. If these are formed by benefic <term>Shubha|Auspicious or "good" planets like Jupiter, Venus, or a strong Moon</term> planets, they are considered auspicious; if formed by malefic <term>Papa|Inauspicious or "cruel" planets like Mars or Saturn</term> planets, they are known as "sinful" combinations. When auspicious planets are in the second house from the Ascendant <term>Lagna|The sign rising on the eastern horizon at the time of birth</term>, a very favorable yoga is formed, provided they are not aspected by malefics. || 8 ||

One born under these auspicious solar yogas is fortunate, happy, a treasure-house of virtues, and a righteous <note>original: "Dharmika," one who follows the path of moral law</note> ruler. They are famous, beloved by all, exceptionally lucky, generous, and dear to kings. Such a person is learned, speaks kindly, is appreciative of others, has refined taste, is eloquent, renowned, and wealthy. These results manifest in full or in part according to the strength of the planets in the Vesi, Vasi, and Ubhayachari positions. || 9 ||

Conversely, [if the yoga is formed by malefics], the person becomes unjust, disliked by people, and loses their natural luster <note>original: "Hatarucih," meaning one whose radiance or appetite for life is destroyed</note>. They prefer the company of the lowly, are wicked, deceptive, and prone to criticizing others. They associate with the vile and study forbidden or ignoble subjects. In this world, they suffer from infamy and a sorrowful mind, being deprived of education, wealth, and good fortune... || 10 ||

<vocab>స్మృతి (Smriti - memory/tradition), వంశజాతాః (Vamshajata - born of a lineage), లగ్న (Lagna - ascendant), యోగ (Yoga - planetary combination), ధార్మికో (Dharmika - righteous), విద్యార్థభాగ్యైశ్చ్యుతో (Vidyartha-bhagyaichyuto - fallen from learning, wealth, and luck), సుశుభో (Sushubha - very auspicious), మహీశప్రియః (Mahishapriya - dear to the lord of the earth/king)</vocab>

--- Page 34 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical classification of astrological yogas (planetary combinations). Page 33 ended with the initial description of the solar yogas (Vesi, Vasi, and Ubhayachari). Page 34 completes those descriptions and introduces the Kartari (Scissor) yogas, Amala (Spotless) yoga, and the prestigious Maha Bhagya (Great Fortune) and Kesari (Lion) yogas.</meta>

<page-num>34</page-num>

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

One born under the auspicious Vesi, Vasi, or Ubhayachari yogas receives the specific benefits of those combinations in sequential degrees. || 10 ||

One born under **Shubha-Kartari Yoga** <note>original: "Shubhakartari Yoga," a combination where a house or planet is "sandwiched" between two benefic planets, acting like a protective "auspicious scissor"</note> will have a long life <note>original: "Jaivatruka"</note>, be free from fear, disease, and enemies, and will be enriched with great wealth and prosperity. 

Conversely, one born under **Papa-Kartari Yoga** <note>original: "Papakartari Yoga," where a house or planet is surrounded by malefic planets, like a "sinful scissor"</note> will be poor, impure, devoid of happiness from a spouse or children, lacking in knowledge, and will lead a short life. || 11 ||

One born under **Amala Yoga** <note>original: "Amala Yoga," meaning the "Spotless" or "Pure" combination, formed when a benefic planet occupies the 10th house from the Ascendant or the Moon</note> is well-behaved, a knower of the righteous path <term>Dharma|The moral and cosmic law</term>, cheerful, fortunate, and respected by rulers. Such a person is gentle by nature, speaks with a smile, and is wealthy. || 12 ||

In the Shubha-Kartari yoga and the Vesi yogas, the results should be understood in the same way as the Sunabha yoga <note>a lunar yoga mentioned in previous sections</note>. Good results are known to come from benefic planets in sequence; the opposite results occur when malefic <term>Asad-graha|Cruel or "bad" planets like Mars or Saturn</term> planets are involved. || 13 ||

If a male is born during the day and the Sun, the Moon, and the Ascendant <term>Lagna|The sign rising on the eastern horizon</term> are all in odd-numbered signs <note>Aries, Gemini, Leo, etc.</note>, it is called **Maha Bhagya Yoga** <note>The Yoga of Great Fortune</note>. For women, this yoga occurs in the opposite condition <note>born at night with those three points in even-numbered signs like Taurus or Cancer</note>. When Jupiter is situated in a central house <term>Kendra|The 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses</term> from the Moon, it is called **Kesari Yoga** <note>original: "Kesari" means Lion; this yoga signifies strength and influence</note>...

<summary>This page defines several major planetary combinations, including the protective "Scissor" (Kartari) yogas and the "Pure" (Amala) yoga, which dictates a person's character and social standing. It also introduces the "Great Fortune" and "Lion" (Kesari) yogas, which are markers of significant success and longevity.</summary>

--- Page 35 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical classification of planetary yogas (combinations), moving from solar and lunar "scissors" combinations to more complex arrangements involving Jupiter and the Moon, and the "Great Fortune" combination for both men and women.</meta>

<page-num>35</page-num>

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

When Jupiter is situated in the sixth or eighth house from the Moon, it is known as <term>Shakata Yoga|The "Cart" combination, named for the way life's fortunes fluctuate like a revolving wheel</term>. However, if the Moon is in a quadrant from the Ascendant <note>original: "Lagna"</note>, this yoga is canceled. Furthermore, the combinations known as **Adhama** (Low), **Sama** (Equal), and **Varishtha** (Excellent) are well-known based on the position of the Moon in relation to the Sun. || 14 ||

One born in **Maha Bhagya Yoga** <note>original: "Great Fortune combination," a rare alignment of the Sun, Moon, and Ascendant in specific signs based on day or night birth</note> brings delight to the eyes of all, is generous, famous, a ruler of the earth, lives to the age of eighty, and is pure of heart. If this yoga occurs in the horoscopes of women, they will be endowed with wealth and the "handfuls of flowers" of prosperity; they will be blessed with sons and grandsons, attaining long-lasting auspiciousness and a virtuous character. || 15 ||

Like a lion destroying a pack of enemies, the person born under **Kesari Yoga** <note>original: "Gaja-Kesari Yoga" or "Lion combination," formed by a specific relationship between Jupiter and the Moon</note> is a masterful speaker in assemblies and possesses a royal bearing. Such a person is long-lived, highly famous, possesses a sharp intellect, and conquers all through their brilliance. || 16 ||

One born under **Shakata Yoga** <note>literally "Cart Yoga"</note> occasionally falls from their fortune, only to regain all that was lost again and again. Such a person is not well-known in the world and suffers from a persistent mental "thorn" <note>an unavoidable source of inner pain or irritation</note> and is very sorrowful <note>The original text likely had a typo "ati-dakhi" for "ati-duhkhi"</note>. || 17 ||

In the yogas named **Kashta** (Difficult), **Madhyama** (Middle), and **Vara** (Excellent), the results are granted in terms of wealth, vehicles, fame, happiness, and prosperity. Similarly, the fruits of wisdom, intellect, humility, skill, education, charity, and enjoyment are experienced according to the specific grade of the combination. || 18 ||

--- Page 36 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical classification of auspicious planetary combinations (Yogas), specifically focusing on those that grant wealth (Vasumati Yoga), abundance (Pushkala Yoga), and leadership (Gauri Yoga), following the previous discussion of Lunar and Solar combinations.</meta>

<page-num>36</page-num>

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

If all benefic planets are situated in the <term>Upachaya|The "houses of growth"—specifically the 3rd, 6th, 10th, and 11th houses from a starting point</term> positions from either the Moon or the Ascendant, the combination known as **Vasumati** <note>literally "The Wealthy One"</note> is formed. If the lord of the Moon sign and the lord of the Ascendant are together in a <term>Kendra|A "quadrant" or "angle"—the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses</term> or in the sign of a "great friend," and if a strong planet aspects the Ascendant, a very powerful yoga called **Pushkala** <note>literally "Abundant"</note> occurs. || 19 ||

One born under **Vasumati Yoga** will always reside in their own home and possess no small amount of wealth. One born under **Amala Yoga** <note>original: "Amala" means "Stainless"; a yoga formed by benefics in the 10th house</note> will be wealthy, blessed with children and fame, endowed with prosperity, and live a moral life. The person born under **Pushkala Yoga** is magnificent, honored by the best of kings, famous, adorned with beautiful clothes and ornaments, speaks sweet words, and becomes a supreme lord. || 20 ||

When all benefic planets are placed in five, six, or seven houses, it is known as **Mala Yoga** <note>literally "Garland combination"</note>; however, if they are placed in houses such as the eighth or twelfth, they are remembered as inauspicious. If the lord of the ninth house <note>original: "Bhagyesha"</note> and Venus are both in their own signs or signs of exaltation, and are located in a quadrant or a <term>Kona|A "trine" house—the 1st, 5th, or 9th</term>, and if the Moon <note>original: "Himakara," literally the "cool-rayed one"</note> is aspected by Jupiter, it is called **Gauri Yoga** <note>named after the Goddess Gauri, representing auspiciousness and power</note>. || 21 ||

Such a person becomes a leader of the people, praised by the protector of the earth (the king), an enjoyer of pleasures, a generous giver, and one who works for the benefit of others.

<summary>This page describes the formation and results of several auspicious planetary combinations including Vasumati, Pushkala, and Gauri Yogas, which are said to grant wealth, royal status, and leadership qualities.</summary>
