<page-num>37</page-num>

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

### Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

> One born under the combination called **Sumala** <note>original: "Sumala-yogajatah"; literally "Born of the Good Garland combination"</note> is widely beloved, blessed with a virtuous son and wife, and is courageous and steadfast. || 22 ||

> Under the combination known as **Ashubha Malika** <note>literally "Inauspicious Garland"</note>, the person follows a wicked path, is sorrowful, causes harm to others, and is ungrateful. They lack devotion to <term>Brahmanas|The priestly or scholarly class, often referred to as "gods on earth" (Bhusuras)</term>, are condemned by the world, and are fond of quarrels. || 23 ||

> Such a person constantly delights in the company of a refined woman, remains free from disease, and is wealthy. He is radiant, protects his kinsmen, and is a sanctuary for the grace of **Mahalakshmi** <note>The Goddess of wealth and auspiciousness</note>. He travels in a magnificent palanquin, surrounded by mighty elephants, brings joy to the world, is a superior ruler among kings, a generous giver, and an embodiment of prosperity. || 24 ||

> With a beautiful body, a son who is highly praised, and being a friend to the ruler, he is himself a son of good character. With a face like a lotus and acclaimed by the virtuous, he shines brilliantly, having been born under the **Gauri Yoga** <note>A combination named after Goddess Gauri, associated with beauty and high status</note>. || 25 ||

> When Venus, Jupiter, and Mercury are situated in <term>Kendras|Quadrants (the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses)</term> or <term>Konas|Trines (the 1st, 5th, and 9th houses)</term>, or are in the second house, and when Jupiter is particularly strong in its sign of exaltation or its own sign, this is declared to be **Sarasvati Yoga** <note>Named after the Goddess of Learning and Arts</note>. || 26 ||

The wise person [born under this yoga] is skilled in drama, prose, poetry, mathematical calculations, and aesthetics...

<summary>This page defines several planetary Yogas, contrasting the virtuous Sumala and Gauri Yogas with the negative Ashubha Malika, and concludes by defining the Sarasvati Yoga which grants intellectual and artistic mastery.</summary>

--- Page 38 ---
<meta>This page continues the astrological descriptions of auspicious planetary combinations (Yogas). It completes the description of Saraswata Yoga before defining the "Trinity Yogas"—Shrikantha, Shrinatha, and Vairinchi—which are named after Shiva, Vishnu, and Brahma respectively. These combinations are said to bestow specific religious inclinations and social status upon the individual.</meta>

<page-num>38</page-num>

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

One born under **Saraswata Yoga** <note>original: "Sārasvata-yogajo"; a combination bestowing the grace of Saraswati, the Goddess of wisdom</note> is highly proficient in the composition of poetry and literary works and has mastered the deeper meanings of the scriptures. He is famous throughout the three worlds, is extremely wealthy, and is blessed with a virtuous wife and children. He is honored by the greatest of kings and is exceedingly fortunate. || 27 ||

---

If the Lord of the <term>Lagna|The Ascendant or first house of the birth chart</term>, the Sun, and the Moon are situated in <term>Kendras|Quadrants (1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses)</term> or <term>Konnas|Trines (1st, 5th, or 9th houses)</term> while occupying their signs of exaltation, their own houses, or the houses of a friend, it is known as **Shrikantha Yoga**. 

In the same way, if Venus, the Lord of the ninth house <note>The house of fortune and piety</note>, and Mercury are similarly placed, it is **Shrinatha Yoga**. 

If Jupiter, the Lord of the fifth house, and Saturn are likewise positioned, it is called **Vairinchi Yoga**. || 28 ||

---

One born under **Shrikantha Yoga** <note>Named after Lord Shiva, the "One with the Auspicious Throat"</note> wears ornaments of <term>Rudraksha|Sacred seeds used for prayer beads, traditionally worn by devotees of Shiva</term> and is covered in the white glow of sacred ash. He is a great soul who constantly meditates on Lord Shiva within his own heart. He is disciplined, initiated into Shaivite vows, and a benefactor to holy men. He bears no malice toward other faiths, is radiant, and finds great joy in the worship of Shiva. || 29 ||

---

One born under **Shrinatha Yoga** <note>Named after Lord Vishnu, the "Lord of Lakshmi"</note> is endowed with great wealth, is an expert in witty and pleasing speech, and follows the commands of Lord Narayana. He is pleasing to the eyes of all and is exceedingly fortunate. || 30 ||

<summary>This page defines and describes the effects of four major planetary combinations: the wisdom-bestowing Saraswata Yoga and the Trinity Yogas (Shrikantha, Shrinatha, and Vairinchi) which influence a person's religious devotion and social prosperity.</summary>

<keywords>Saraswata Yoga, Shrikantha Yoga, Shrinatha Yoga, Vairinchi Yoga, Vedic Astrology, Jyotisha, Shiva devotion, Vishnu devotion, Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao</keywords>

--- Page 39 ---
<meta>This page continues the description of the "Trinity Yogas," concluding the effects of Vairinchi Yoga (related to Lord Brahma) before moving into a systematic classification of sixty-six planetary combinations involving the exchange of house lords. It specifically defines the Dainya (misery) and Kahala (drum/fame) yogas.</meta>

<page-num>39</page-num>

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

One born under **Vairinchi Yoga** <note>original: "vairiñci-yogodbhavaḥ"; Vairinchi is another name for Brahma, the creator deity, symbolizing wisdom and creation</note> is devoted to the supreme knowledge of Brahman, is highly esteemed, and considers the knowledge of the *Vedas* as his true wealth. He is virtuous, does not deviate from the Vedic path, and attracts a circle of famous disciples. With gentle speech, he is blessed with great wealth, a wife, and sons. He shines with the brilliance of a great soul, possesses a long life, has mastered his senses, and remains humble. || 31 ||

---

When the lords of the houses, starting from the <term>Lagna|The Ascendant or 1st house</term>, occupy each other's signs, people speak of sixty-six combinations (Yogas). Thirty of these are declared to be **Dainya Yogas** <note>original: "dainya"; meaning misery, poverty, or wretchedness</note>, which arise when the lords of the house of loss (12th), the house of enemies (6th), or the house of death (8th) are involved. Those arising from the lord of the house of courage (3rd) are called **Khala Yogas** <note>original: "khala"; meaning wicked or harsh</note>, and the remaining combinations are known as the **Maha Yogas** <note>Great combinations</note>. || 32 ||

---

One born under a **Dainya Yoga** will be a fool, a slanderer, and a doer of sinful deeds. He is constantly tormented by enemies, speaks with harsh words, possesses an unstable mind, and finds his works and efforts frequently interrupted. 

---

One who is the abode of the grace of the Goddess of Fortune (Lakshmi), a lord among men, and honorable, who wears fine and colorful garments: such a person is born when the lords of the fourth and ninth houses are in <term>Kendras|Quadrants (1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses)</term> from each other, while the lord of the Lagna is strong. This is famously known as **Kahala Yoga**. <note>original: "kahala-yoga"; Kahala refers to a large trumpet or drum used in processions, symbolizing authority and public renown.</note>

<summary>This page concludes the description of Vairinchi Yoga and introduces the categorization of sixty-six planetary exchanges, detailing the unfortunate Dainya Yogas and the prestigious Kahala Yoga.</summary>
<keywords>Vairinchi Yoga, Dainya Yoga, Kahala Yoga, planetary exchanges, Vedic astrology, house lords, Brahma, prosperity, adversity</keywords>

--- Page 40 ---
<meta>This page continues the astrological discourse from page 39, moving from the definitions of Dainya and Kahala Yogas to describe the specific auspicious results of Parvata, Raja, Bheri, and Shankha Yogas, followed by an introduction to the seven numerical Sankhya Yogas.</meta>

<page-num>40</page-num>

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

...the lord of the Ascendant <note>original: "lagnādhipa"</note> and the lord of the 8th house...

> Increasing in prosperity, noble, wise, and serene; a source of well-being, born of the Kahala influence <note>Refers to the Kahala Yoga mentioned on the previous page</note> and honored by men. He enjoys permanent happiness, remains steady in his endeavors, and becomes a ruler of the earth—such is one born under **Parvata Yoga** <note>original: "parvata-yoga"; literally 'Mountain Combination,' symbolizing stability and height of status</note>. || 36 ||

> When the lords of the 9th house <term>Dharma|The house of fortune and righteousness</term> and the 10th house <term>Karma|The house of profession and public action</term> are joined together and situated in an auspicious house, it is called **Raja Yoga** <note>The 'Royal Combination' indicating power and high status</note>. Similarly, any union between the lords of the <term>Kendra|Angular houses: 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th</term> and the <term>Kona|Trinal houses: 1st, 5th, and 9th</term> is also designated by this term. || 37 ||

> Accompanied by the resounding noise of drums and soft ceremonial garments, surrounded by umbrellas of state, elephants, horses, and palanquins, and praised by court bards, such a person is a ruler of the land. He is multifaceted like the god Shiva <note>original: "hara"; a name for Lord Shiva</note>, sought after by virtuous people, and becomes a king. Under **Shankha Yoga** <note>original: "śaṅkha-yoga"; named after the sacred Conch shell</note>, he is filled with prosperity and the enjoyment of many excellent women. || 38 ||

> The **Sankhya Yogas** <note>original: "saṅkhyā-yogāḥ"; combinations based on the number of signs the seven planets occupy</note> are seven. As the number of occupied signs decreases from seven down to one, they are named: **Vallaki** (Veena), **Dama**, **Pasha**, **Kedara**, **Shula**, **Yuga**, and **Gola**, excluding other combinations previously mentioned. || 39 ||

> In **Veena Yoga** <note>original: "vīṇā-yoga"; where planets occupy seven signs</note>, one is a lover of dance and music and a seeker of wealth. In **Dama Yoga** <note>planets in six signs</note>, one is charitable, prosperous, and helpful. In **Pasha Yoga** <note>planets in five signs</note>, one is an enjoyer of life, possesses a good character, and has many relatives. In **Kedara Yoga** <note>planets in four signs</note>, one is endowed with wealth through agriculture and land. || 40 ||

It concludes the series of "Sankhya Yogas" (numerical combinations) started on the previous page and introduces "Adhi Yoga"—one of the most prestigious configurations in Vedic astrology—along followed by the beginning of a sequence of twelve house-based Yogas, starting with Chamara Yoga.</meta>

<page-num>41</page-num>

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

> Under **Shula Yoga** <note>original: "śūla-yoga"; literally 'Spear Combination'</note>, one is prone to violence, possesses a quick temper, and suffers from poverty. Such a person may become a heretic <note>original: "pāṣaṇḍī"; one who opposes established spiritual traditions</note>. Under **Yuga Yoga** <note>original: "yuga-yoga"; 'Pair Combination'</note>, one lacks wealth, is prone to sinful acts, associates with outcasts <note>original: "mleccha"; historically referring to those outside the traditional Vedic social or linguistic order</note>, and is a poor craftsman. One born under **Gola Yoga** <note>original: "gola-yoga"; 'Globe/Sphere Combination'</note> is destitute, very poor, and short-lived. || 41 ||

> When <term>benefic planets|Planets considered naturally auspicious, such as Jupiter, Venus, Mercury, and a strong Moon</term> are situated in the 6th, 7th, and 8th houses from either the Moon or the Ascendant, it results in **Adhi Yoga** <note>original: "adhi-yoga"; meaning 'Superior' or 'Surpassing' combination</note>. A person born with this becomes a leader, a minister, or a king. They are famous, wealthy, long-lived, and wise. || 42 ||

> One born under Adhi Yoga becomes a lord of men, possessing stable wealth and supporting many relatives. By this influence, enemies are defeated, and the individual attains a very long life and great renown. || 43 ||

> When the <term>houses|The twelve divisions of the zodiac representing different life areas</term> are joined or aspected <note>Influenced by the gaze of a planet</note> by benefic planets, and their lords are placed in auspicious positions, or in their own signs of exaltation <note>The specific sign where a planet is at its maximum strength</note>, twelve distinct Yogas are formed starting from the Ascendant. These are known as:
> 1. **Chamara** (The Royal Fly-whisk)
> 2. **Dhenu** (The Cow of Plenty)
> 3. **Shaurya** (Valour)
> 4. **Jaladhi** (The Ocean)
> 5. **Chhatra** (The Royal Umbrella)
> 6. **Astra** (The Weapon)
> 7. **Kama** (Desire)
> 8. **Asura** (The Formidable)
> 9. **Bhagya** (Fortune)
> 10. **Khyati** (Fame)
> 11. **Suparijata** (The Celestial Tree)
> 12. **Musala** (The Pestle)
> These have been described by the sages according to their effects. || 44 ||

> Like the waxing moon in the bright half of the month, the person born under **Chamara Yoga** <note>original: "cāmara-jāta"</note> increases in prosperity every day. They are of noble character, famous, a ruler of people, long-lived, and a treasury of wealth. || 45 ||

Abounding in food and drink, rich in all forms of knowledge, and possessing great family prosperity; enriched with gold, gems, wealth, and grains, one shines like the King of Kings <note>original: "rāja-rāja"; an epithet for Kubera, the god of wealth</note> under **Dhenu Yoga** <note>original: "dhenu-yoga"; literally 'Cow Combination', symbolizing sustenance and abundance</note>. || 47 ||

Praised by famous younger siblings, cherished, and endowed with great valor; born of heroism, he becomes like Rama, dedicated to royal service and highly renowned. <note>This describes **Saurya Yoga** (Heroism Combination)</note>. || 48 ||

A home shining with the wealth of cows, riches, and grains; having many sons; possessing excellent women, gems, clothes, and ornaments, and an exalted, supreme position. One born under this influence <note>original: "amuṣ-prayoga-jaḥ"; referring to the results of the specific planetary placement</note> attains stable happiness, travels on elephants, horses, and vehicles; is praised by kings, dedicated to serving the <term>twice-born|original: "dvija"; the three upper social classes in Vedic tradition, referring to spiritual rebirth</term> and the gods, and builds wells and water-troughs along the roads. || 49 ||

An abode of a good family, fortune, children, and prosperity; famous, well-spoken, and wise. He is a minister to the king, venerable, wealthy, of good conduct, and sharp intellect under **Viveka Yoga** <note>original: "viveka-yoga"; 'Discernment Combination'</note>. || 49 ||

Forcibly restraining powerful enemies, possessing a fierce nature and pride; knowledgeable in treating wounds but prone to conflict, one born under **Astra Yoga** <note>original: "astra-yoga"; 'Weapon Combination'</note> possesses a strong physique. || 50 ||

He turns away from the wives of others, is supported by a noble wife and close relatives...

...Greater than his father, he attains great glory through auspicious qualities under **Kamaja Yoga** <note>original: "kāma-ja"; literally 'born of desire' or 'love-born', referring to a specific planetary alignment</note>. || 50 ||

---

One born under **Asura Yoga** <note>original: "asura-yoga"; 'Demonic Combination', usually indicating a challenging or malefic influence</note> sabotages the work of others, is a backbiter, and is solely focused on his own interests. He remains poor and stubborn, inviting a continuous series of misfortunes upon himself through his own actions and the performance of evil deeds. || 52 ||

---

Surrounded by the sound of rhythmic instruments and the waving of <term>chamara|original: "cāmara"; ceremonial whisks used as symbols of royalty and divinity</term>, one attains permanent prosperity and travels in a <term>palanquin|original: "āndolikā"; a portable litter used by nobility</term>. Revering the path of righteousness and receiving the salutations of great men, he pleases his ancestors, the gods, and the <term>twice-born|original: "dvija"; members of the upper social classes dedicated to spiritual duties</term> through their preferred rituals of worship. Such a person is well-conducted, the pride of his lineage, and kind-hearted under **Bhagya Yoga** <note>original: "bhāgya-yoga"; 'Fortune Combination'</note>. || 53 ||

---

Performing virtuous deeds approved by all the world, he protects good people like a king. Blessed with sons, friends, wealth, and a wife, one born under **Khyati Yoga** <note>original: "khyāti-yoga"; 'Fame Combination'</note> becomes world-renowned. || 54 ||

---

Ever generous with offerings, a lord of the earth with a vast store of accumulated wealth and a happy family; devoted to listening to sacred stories, one born under **Parijata Yoga** <note>original: "pārijāta-jananaḥ"; named after the celestial wish-granting tree</note> is a source of auspiciousness and protection. || 55 ||

---

Obtaining wealth only through great hardship, and having been humbled <note>original: "paribhūtaḥ"; likely referring to one who has faced significant social or personal setbacks</note>, he possesses unstable fortune but maintains the character to spend what he has appropriately. || 56 ||

### Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

...He never obtains stability; during this period, he becomes a wicked person, blunt as a pestle, and fickle. || 56 ||

---

When the lords of the various houses <note>original: "bhāva-gṛheśvaraiḥ"; the planetary rulers of the twelve houses of the zodiac</note> are placed in the "evil" houses—the sixth, eighth, or twelfth—or are joined with or aspected by malefic planets, they produce negative combinations. In order, these twelve are known as: **Ava**, **Nisva**, **Mriti**, **Kuhu**, **Pamara**, **Harsha**, **Dushkriti**, **Sarala**, **Nirbhagya**, **Duryoga**, **Daridra**, and **Vimala**. Wise men have declared these twelve <term>Ava-yogas|original: "avayoga"; combinations that bring about downfall or difficulty</term>. || 57 ||

---

Under **Ava Yoga** <note>original: "ava-yoga"; literally 'downward' or 'degraded' combination</note>, one suffers from obscurity, unbearable misery, and a short lifespan. He faces humiliation from wicked people, possesses a bad character and a frail or sickly body, and lives in a state of constant instability. || 58 ||

---

Under **Nisva Yoga** <note>original: "niḥsva-yoga"; 'combination of the property-less' or 'poverty'</note>, one is devoid of kind speech, has an unsuccessful family, and associates with a base social circle. He suffers from poor eyesight or dental issues. Lacking intelligence, children, education, and prosperity, his wealth is eventually seized by his enemies. || 59 ||

---

Overwhelmed by enemies and lacking the support of siblings, one suffers from mental distress and the destruction of both strength and wealth. Under **Mriti Yoga** <note>original: "mṛti-yoga"; 'combination of death' or 'destruction'</note>, he is exhausted by improper labor and possesses a distorted or perverse nature. || 60 ||

---

Under **Kuhu Yoga** <note>original: "kuhū-yoga"; named after the New Moon, implying darkness or hidden trouble</note>, one is bereft of a mother's care, vehicles, friends, happiness, ornaments, and relatives. He lacks a permanent home or status; whatever place he takes refuge in is eventually ruined. Furthermore, he is devoted to wicked or unfaithful women. || 61 ||

### Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

One who leads a life of misery, speaks lies, lacks discrimination, and is a deceiver; even if he has children, they may pass away, or he remains childless. He is an atheist, associates with base or low-minded people, and is gluttonous—this occurs under **Pamara Yoga**. <note>original: "pāmara-yoga"; 'pāmara' refers to a person who is vulgar, unrefined, or base.</note> || 92 ||

---

Endowed with happiness, enjoyment, fortune, and a strong physique, he overcomes his enemies and is fearful of committing sins. He becomes a favorite of eminent and powerful leaders, and is blessed with wealth, brilliance, friends, fame, and children—this is the result of **Harsha Yoga**. <note>original: "harṣa-yoga"; 'harṣa' means joy or thrill. This is considered a "Vipareeta Raja Yoga" where a lord of a difficult house in another difficult house produces positive results.</note> || 93 ||

---

He suffers separation from his wife, desires the wives of others, and wanders aimlessly during the heat of the day. He suffers from diseases like diabetes, <term>urinary ailments|original: "prameha"</term>, secret diseases, and trouble from the government or rulers; he also faces the grief of being rejected by his own relatives—this is said of **Dushkriti Yoga**. <note>original: "duṣkṛti-yoga"; 'duṣkṛti' means an evil deed or sin.</note> || 94 ||

---

Long-lived, firm-minded, and fearless; blessed with prosperity, education, children, and wealth. He achieves success in all his undertakings, conquers his enemies, remains pure in character, and becomes widely famous—this is the influence of **Sarala Yoga**. <note>original: "sarala-yoga"; 'sarala' means simple, honest, or straightforward.</note> || 95 ||

---

He destroys ancestral property such as lands and houses, insults holy men and teachers, and abandons the path of righteousness. He wears worn-out or ragged clothes, lives in poverty, and becomes a vessel for great sorrow—this occurs under **Nirbhagya Yoga**. <note>original: "nirbhāgya-yoga"; 'nirbhāgya' literally means "without luck" or "misfortune."</note> || 96 ||

---

Whatever work he performs with great physical effort ends in fruitlessness, and he suffers from a low reputation among the people. He betrays others, cares only for filling his own belly, and is constantly wandering or living away from home—this is the fate of one born under **Duryoga**. <note>original: "duryoga"; literally "bad combination."</note> || 97 ||

---

One who is burdened by debt, is fierce or cruel, is considered the foremost among the poor, suffers from ear diseases, and lacks good relations with his brothers; he engages in improper deeds, speaks in a way that is offensive or discordant, and lives as a servant to others—this occurs under **Daridra Yoga**. <note>original: "daridra-yoga"; 'daridra' means poor or needy. This yoga occurs when the lord of the 11th house (gains) is placed in a "dusthana" or difficult house (6, 8, or 12).</note> || 68 ||

---

With very little expenditure, he achieves a great increase in wealth and gains the favor and cooperation of all people. He is happy, independent, follows an honorable profession, and is celebrated for his virtues—this is the result of **Vimala Yoga**. <note>original: "vimala-yoga"; 'vimala' means pure or stainless. In astrology, this occurs when the lord of the 12th house is placed in the 12th house itself, turning a house of loss into a source of purity and controlled spending.</note> || 69 ||

---

If the lords of the <term>difficult houses|original: "chidra-ari-vyaya"</term> (the 8th, 6th, and 12th) are powerful and placed in <term>cardinal houses|original: "kendra"</term> or <term>trines|original: "trikona"</term>, while the lords of the 1st, 10th, 4th, and 9th houses are situated in the 6th, 8th, or 12th houses, being weak and devoid of luster, then it is known as **Duryoga**. However, if the opposite is true, he becomes a **Yogavan**—a man of great fortune, king-like, happy, and righteous. <note>original: "duryoga" means 'bad union.' This verse defines the structural difference between a life of hardship and a life of power (Yogavan) based on whether the 'good' or 'bad' house lords are dominant.</note> || 70 ||

# Chapter Seven

## Rajayoga (Royal Combinations)

If three or more planets are in their signs of exaltation or in their own signs and are situated in the <term>cardinal houses|original: "kendra"</term>, the person becomes a famous king. If five or more such planets are so placed, even if born into an ordinary family, he becomes a lord of the earth, endowed with many elephants and horses. <note>original: "rājayoga"; these are specific planetary alignments that indicate power, leadership, and high status.</note> || 1 ||

<summary>This page concludes the descriptions of planetary yogas with Daridra, Vimala, and Duryoga, and begins Chapter Seven, which defines Rajayogas—combinations that lead to royal status and great power.</summary>
<keywords>Daridra Yoga, Vimala Yoga, Duryoga, Rajayoga, planetary exaltation, kendra, Vedic astrology, kingship</keywords>

--- Page 47 ---
<meta>This page transitions from the general discussion of planetary combinations (yogas) to specific "Raja Yogas" or Royal Combinations, outlining the celestial requirements for an individual to achieve the status of a sovereign or a person of king-like influence.</meta>

<page-num>47</page-num>

# Vemuri Durganageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

If those born into a royal lineage are not born under a <term>discordant combination|original: "duryoga"</term>, and if their hearts are guided by planets as radiant as the sun and lotus, situated in their own signs or signs of exaltation, they surely become kings. If three or more planets are positioned in <term>cardinal houses|original: "kendra"</term> (the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses) while in their <term>own signs|original: "svabhā"</term> or <term>exaltation|original: "ucca"</term>, those born of the earth become sovereigns. Even those born in other, non-royal families may sometimes become equal to kings under these conditions. <note>original: verse 2. This verse explains that while lineage is a traditional factor, astrology suggests that specific "Kendra" placements—the pillars of a birth chart—can elevate even a commoner to the status of a ruler.</note> || 2 ||

---

If even a single planet, radiant with a multitude of rays, is well-placed and in <term>retrograde|original: "vakra"</term> motion—even if it occupies a <term>debilitated|original: "nīca"</term> sign—it can make a person like a king. If two or three such planets are present, they truly create a ruler. If many such planets are situated in auspicious <term>divisional signs|original: "śastāṃśa-rāśi"</term>, they produce a monarch who is adorned with a crown, a royal umbrella, and shimmering ceremonial whisks. <note>original: verse 3. The crown, umbrella (chatra), and whisk (chamara) are the three traditional symbols of Indian royalty. "Shastamsha" refers to favorable subdivisions within the zodiac signs.</note> || 3 ||

---

If two, three, or more planets possess <term>directional strength|original: "dig-bala"</term>, a person born into a royal family becomes a victorious ruler of the earth. Excluding the <term>malefics|original: "manstham"</term>, if four or five planets possess directional strength, even one born into a different lineage becomes a king. <note>original: verse 4. 'Dig-bala' is the "strength of direction"; for example, Jupiter and Mercury are strongest in the East (the 1st house), while the Sun and Mars are strongest in the South (the 10th house).</note> || 4 ||

By three planets that are not in the divisions of their enemies or in their signs of debilitation...

> ->If at the time of birth the <term>Moon|original: "mṛgalāñchanaḥ" (the deer-marked one)</term> is as white as cow's milk or a conch shell, is positioned in its own <term>divisional sign|original: "svāṃśōpagaiḥ"</term>, is endowed with strength, and is aspected by <term>benefic planets|original: "śubhadṛṣṭijuṣṭaiḥ"</term>, that person becomes a <term>lord of the earth|original: "bhūmipatiḥ"</term> who has conquered his enemies. || 12 ||<-

> ->If a strong planet <note>Literally "a sky-traveler" (vyōmacārī).</note> aspects the <term>Moon|original: "kumudagahanabandhuṃ" (the friend of the night-blooming lilies)</term> while it is situated in a <term>watery sign|original: "ambhaṃ prapannaṃ"</term>, and if the person is born in the <term>Ascendant|original: "udayabhavanasaṃsthaḥ"</term> <note>The first house or the sign rising at birth.</note> without the influence of <term>malefic planets|original: "pāpasaṃjñō"</term>, he becomes a lord of men, a universal emperor <term>|original: "sarvabhaumaḥ"</term>, possessing a beautiful and healthy body. || 13 ||<-

> ->If <term>Jupiter|original: "jīva"</term>, <term>Mercury|original: "budha"</term>, <term>Venus|original: "bhṛgusuta"</term>, or the <term>Moon|original: "niśākara"</term> are located in the <term>Ninth House|original: "dharme"</term> <note>The house of fortune and righteousness.</note>, and are pure in form, radiating clear beams of light, and are aspected by or associated with <term>friendly planets|original: "mitrairnirīkṣitayutā"</term> at the time of birth, they create a great king who is like a god on earth. || 14 ||<-

When his army marches, the clouds of dust raised by them enter the path of the **Sun**, so that the <term>lotus plants|original: "kamalinī"</term>—confused into thinking it is already night—begin to close their petals. || 15 || <note>This is a classic poetic hyperbole (Alankara) describing a king so powerful that his massive cavalry kicks up enough dust to block the sun, tricking the flowers into their nightly sleep.</note>

If the <term>Moon|original: "śiśirakiraṇe" (the cool-rayed one)</term> is positioned in **Cancer** at night, and **benefic planets** are in their <term>exaltation signs|original: "soccaṃ"</term>, or are located in the third, sixth, or <term>angular houses|original: "krestagairvā"</term> <note>The text uses "kresta," likely a variant of "kendra," referring to the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses.</note> while being pure and bright, the person born becomes a **Lord of Warriors** who brings the three worlds under the shade of a single sovereign umbrella. || 16 || <note>The "single umbrella" is a traditional symbol of universal emperorship.</note>

If the full **Moon** is situated in the <term>Vargottama|original: "vargottame"</term> <note>A highly auspicious state where a planet occupies the same zodiac sign in both the main birth chart and the ninth divisional chart (Navamsha).</note> position, it produces a ruler of unprecedented and delightful fame; one the dust from whose thundering cavalry hooves so overcomes the **Sun** that it takes on the pale appearance of the early morning moon. || 17 ||

If **Jupiter** and the **Moon** are both located in the <term>tenth house|original: "krestagau"</term> at the time of birth, and are <term>aspected|original: "bhārgavadṛṣṭau"</term> by **Venus**... <note>The text cuts off here, mid-description of a planetary alignment involving Jupiter, the Moon, and Venus.</note>

...He becomes a king of incomparable fame, provided that no planet in this configuration is in its <term>sign of debilitation|original: "nīcago"</term>. || 18 ||

If a planet is situated in the <term>ninth divisional chart|original: "navāṃśaka"</term> of a **watery sign**, and is positioned in the **first house** <note>The "first house" (Tanu-bhava) represents the self and the physical body in a birth chart.</note> within its own auspicious divisions, he becomes a king who is the lord of many elephants, surely free from misfortune. || 19 || <note>The "watery signs" in this system are Cancer, Scorpio, and Pisces. A "lord of many elephants" is a traditional poetic way to describe a ruler with a massive military force.</note>

**Venus**, when aspected by **Jupiter**, produces a king born of a royal line. **Jupiter**, when placed in the **first house**—provided it is not in the sign of **Capricorn**—produces a ruler possessed of intoxicated war-elephants. If the **lord of the birth sign** is endowed with great strength and located in an <term>angular house|original: "kendre"</term>, it makes one a lord of the earth. When **Mercury** is aspected by **Jupiter** (the "Lord of Speech"), other rulers of the earth shall follow his commands. || 20 || <note>Jupiter is considered "debilitated" or weakest in the sign of Capricorn, which is why the text specifies its exclusion here. The "angular houses" (1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th) are the pillars of power in a horoscope.</note>

Even a single planet, if located in its **exaltation sign** and aspected by a friendly planet, can make one a king who is wealthy through the union of friends. If the **Sun** is in its own division and the **Moon** is in an auspicious state, it creates a sovereign lord of horses and elephants. || 21 ||

<page-num>53</page-num>

## Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

...He becomes a chief among kings, one who brings joy to the world. || 21 <insert>continued</insert> ||

If the **Moon** <note>original: "tuhināṃśu", literally "the cool-rayed one"</note> is at its full brilliance and situated in its sign of **exaltation**, the person becomes a lord of the world, renowned for his great wisdom and extraordinary generosity. || 22 ||

If the **Moon** is situated in a divisional portion belonging to a "great friend" <note>In Vedic astrology, planets have relationships categorized as friends, enemies, or neutral. A "great friend" (adhimitra) status significantly boosts a planet's strength.</note> and is aspected by **Venus**, he becomes a king endowed with the blessings of <term>Lakshmi|goddess of wealth</term>. If, in that same position, the Moon is aspected by **Jupiter** <note>original: "vāsavamantri", meaning the Counselor of Indra/Jupiter</note>, he shall rule over the entire earth. || 23 ||

The wise declare these four types of **Royal Combinations** <term>Raja Yogas|combinations for power</term>: 
If the **malefic planets** are placed in the third, sixth, or eleventh houses; if the **lord of the birth sign**, **Mars**, and **Mercury** are in the second house from the ascendant; if the **Sun** and **Venus** are in the **fourth house** <note>original: "hibuka", a technical term derived from Greek 'hypogeion' used in Sanskrit astrology for the 4th house</note>; and if **Mars, Mercury, and Jupiter** are positioned in the tenth, eleventh, and first houses respectively. || 24 ||

If even one among the lords of the **eleventh house** (Gains), the **ninth house** (Fortune), or the **second house** (Wealth) is situated in an **angular house** <term>kendra|the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses</term> relative to the **Moon**, and if **Jupiter** is the lord of the fifth and eleventh houses, the person attains the status of an emperor over an undivided empire. || 25 ||

### Neechabhanga Raja Yoga
<note>This term refers to the "Cancellation of Debilitation," a specialized rule where a planet's weakness is transformed into a source of great power.</note>

<vocab>Raja Yoga (Royal Combination), Neechabhanga (Cancellation of Debilitation), Memorial Book Series, Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao, Jyotisha (Astrology)</vocab>

<summary>This page details specific planetary alignments involving the Moon and Jupiter that elevate an individual to royal or imperial status. It concludes by introducing the concept of Neechabhanga Raja Yoga, where astrological weaknesses are overturned to create success.</summary>
<keywords>Raja Yoga, Moon exaltation, Jupiter, Venus, house lords, wealth, empire, Indian astrology</keywords>

--- Page 54 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of royal astrological combinations from page 53, specifically moving into the "Neechabhanga Raja Yoga." While the previous page focused on the strength of exalted planets and specific house placements, this page details how a planet's weakness (debility) can be transformed into a source of supreme power and imperial status if certain conditions are met.</meta>

<page-num>54</page-num>

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

> If a planet is in its **debilitated** <note>original: "nīcasthito", referring to a planet in its weakest zodiacal sign</note> position at birth, but the lord of that sign or the lord of the planet's **exaltation sign** <note>original: "taduccanāthaḥ", the planet that rules the sign where the debilitated planet would be at its strongest</note> is in a **cardinal house** <term>Kendra|the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses from the starting point, considered the pillars of a birth chart</term> relative to the Moon or the Ascendant, that person shall become a king and a righteous emperor. || 26 ||

---

> If even one planet is in a debilitated sign, but the lord of that sign or the lord of its exaltation sign is in a **cardinal house**, the person born under this influence becomes an **emperor** <note>original: "cakravartī", a universal ruler</note> whose feet are honored by all the kings of the earth. || 27 ||

---

> If the planet that rules the sign <note>original: "rāśīśena"</note> where a certain celestial body is placed gazes upon <note>original: "prekṣita", refers to an astrological aspect or 'drishti'</note> that body, it makes the individual a famous **ruler of the earth**. If that planet is also situated in a favorable house, how much more then shall they be a supreme lord of kings? || 28 ||

---

> When a planet sits in its sign of debility, if the lord of that house or the lord of its exaltation sign is in a **cardinal house** from the Ascendant or from the Moon, the person will attain complete prosperity. They shall be a righteous emperor whose feet are bowed to by other kings, endowed with brilliance, fame, and great fortune. || 29 ||

---

> If a planet is debilitated, and the lords of its debility and exaltation signs—either both or even just one of them—are situated in a **cardinal house**, the person becomes an emperor and a ruler honored by all other kings. || 30 ||

<summary>This page describes the "Neechabhanga Raja Yoga," a configuration where a planet's weakness is cancelled by the strong placement of its dispositors, resulting in the individual attaining the status of a righteous emperor.</summary>
<keywords>Neechabhanga Raja Yoga, cancellation of debility, cardinal houses, Kendra, dispositors, imperial status, Vedic astrology, planetary aspects</keywords>

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

## Chapter 8:

### The Results of Planetary House Placements <note>original: "Bhāvāśraya Phala"</note>

If the **Sun** <term>Surya|The Sun, representing the soul, ego, and authority in Vedic astrology</term> is situated in the **Ascendant** <term>Lagna|The First House, representing the physical body and self</term>, the person will have little hair, be brilliant <note>original: "lasatamaḥ"</note>, prone to anger, fierce, tall, and proud. They will have rough-looking eyes, a lean body, and be brave, yet impatient and lacking in mercy.

However, the specific sign matters: if the Ascendant is **Aries** <note>original: "Kriyā"</note>, the person is brilliant; if **Cancer** <note>original: "Śaśibhe"</note>, they suffer from cataracts or dimmed vision; if **Leo** <note>original: "Siṃhē"</note>, they suffer from night blindness; and if the Sun is in **Libra** <note>original: "Tulāyaṃ"</note> in the first house, the person will be poor, defeated, and suffer the loss of their children. || 1 ||

---

If the Sun is in the **Second House** <note>original: "Dhanagataḥ", the house of wealth and speech</note>, the person is deprived of education, humility, and wealth, and possesses a stammer or faltering speech.

If the Sun is in the **Third House** <note>original: "Sahajagaḥ", the house of siblings and courage</note>, the person possesses great strength, bravery, and prosperity; they are generous, though they may view their own relatives as enemies.

If the Sun is in the **Fourth House** <note>original: "Suhṛdi", the house of mother, home, and happiness</note>, it causes a lack of happiness, and trouble regarding relatives, lands, and friends. The person remains attached to their home, serves the king, inherits father’s wealth, and may suffer from "windy" <note>original: "Vāyu", referring to Vata-type ailments like joint pain or gastric issues</note> complaints. || 2 ||

---

If the Sun is in the **Fifth House** <note>original: "Ātmanya", the house of intelligence and children</note>, the person may have happiness, wealth, and long life, but will be without children; they are wise but prone to wandering in forests.

If the Sun is in the **Sixth House** <note>original: "Ariṣṭaḥ", the house of enemies and health</note>, the person becomes famous, a ruler of the earth, endowed with good qualities and wealth, and is always victorious.

If the Sun is in the **Seventh House** <note>original: "Aste", the house of marriage and partnerships</note>, the person faces opposition from the king, has a weak or deformed body, travels frequently, and though generous, is often treated with disrespect or dishonor. || 3 ||

<summary>This page initiates Chapter 8, detailing the physical, social, and character-based consequences of the Sun being placed in the first seven astrological houses (the First House through the Seventh House).</summary>
<keywords>Sun, Surya, Houses, Lagna, Astrology, Aries, Libra, Health, Wealth, Family</keywords>

--- Page 56 ---
<meta>This page continues the specific astrological predictions based on the placement of the Sun in the various houses of the zodiac, concluding the Sun's cycle and beginning the descriptions for the Moon. The previous page discussed the Sun's placement from the First House (Ascendant) through the Fourth House.</meta>

<page-num>56</page-num>

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

...[the Sun] destroys wealth and lifespan, is heartless toward friends, and suffers from failing vision if located in the **Eighth House** <term>Nidhanaga|The house of death and transformations</term>. || 3 ||

If the Sun is in the **Ninth House** <term>Tapasi|The house of fortune, father, and spirituality</term>, the person is skilled in their work, blessed with children and relatives, and possesses a mind devoted to the Gods and the learned <note>original: "deva-dvija-manaḥ"; literally "Gods and the twice-born (Brahmins)"</note>.

If the Sun is in the **Tenth House** <term>Khē|The house of career and public status</term>, they are blessed with sons, vehicles, praise, intelligence, wealth, strength, and fame; they are like a ruler of the earth.

If the Sun is in the **Eleventh House** <term>Bhavagata|The house of gains and ambitions</term>, they possess great wealth and a long life, remain free from sorrow, and become a leader of people.

If the Sun is in the **Twelfth House** <term>Vyayagatē|The house of expenditure and loss</term>, they are hostile toward their father and friends, suffer from eye defects, and face the loss or suffering of their children. || 4 ||

---

### The Results of the Moon's Placement <note>original: "Candra Phala"</note>

If the **Moon** <term>Chandra|The Moon, representing the mind, emotions, and mother</term> is in its **bright phase** <note>original: "sitē"; referring to the waxing Moon or the bright fortnight</note> and situated in the **Ascendant** <term>Lagna|The First House</term>, the person will have a strong body, a very long life, and be fearless, powerful, and wealthy. However, if the Moon is in its **waning phase** <note>original: "kṣayagatē"</note>, the results are the opposite.

If the Moon is in the **Second House** <term>Dhanya/Vāci|The house of wealth and speech</term>, the person is very wealthy, a scholar <note>original: "antarvāṇiḥ"</note>, and enjoys sensory pleasures, though their speech may be faltering.

If the Moon is in the **Third House** <term>Sahaje|The house of siblings and courage</term>, the person has siblings, joy, strength, and valor, but is extremely miserly <note>original: "atikṛpaṇaḥ"</note>. || 5 ||

If the Moon is in the **Fourth House** <term>Suhṛdi|The house of home, happiness, and friends</term>, the person is happy, enjoys life's luxuries, is charitable, and possesses good friends, vehicles, and fame.

If the Moon is in the **Fifth House** <term>Sutagatē|The house of children and intelligence</term>, they have good children, are highly intelligent, possess a graceful gait, and act as a minister or advisor.

If the Moon is in the **Sixth House** <term>Kṣatē|The house of enemies and illness</term>, they have a short lifespan, a restless or "Moon-like" mind, suffer from stomach ailments, and experience humiliation.

If the Moon is in the **Seventh House** <term>Smarē|The house of marriage and partnerships</term>, and is aspected by benefic planets, the person is handsome, beloved of a virtuous wife, and is exceptionally fortunate. || 6 ||

<summary>This page completes the astrological effects of the Sun's placement in the 8th through 12th houses and details the effects of the Moon's placement in the first seven houses, distinguishing between the waxing and waning Moon.</summary>
<keywords>Sun in houses, Moon in houses, Jyotisha, Vedic Astrology, Bhava Phala, Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao, planetary effects</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the astrological predictions regarding planetary placements. It concludes the cycle for the Moon (Shashi) and begins the descriptions for Mars (Kuja). The previous page ended with the Moon in the First House.</meta>

<page-num>57</page-num>

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

If the **Moon** <term>Shashi|The Moon, representing the mind and emotions</term> is in the **Eighth House** <term>Mrtyau|The house of death and longevity</term>, the person is sickly and has a short lifespan. If in the **Ninth House** <term>Tapasi|The house of penance, fortune, and father</term>, they are auspicious, righteous, and blessed with sons.

In the **Tenth House** <term>Nabhasi|The house of the sky or career</term>, they are victorious, successful in their endeavors, and a performer of virtuous deeds and Vedic rites <note>original: "trayikaraḥ"; refers to the three Vedas: Rig, Yajur, and Sama</note>.

In the **Eleventh House** <term>Labha|The house of gains</term>, they are high-minded and enjoy a long life along with wealth, children, and servants.

In the **Twelfth House** <term>Vyayē|The house of expenditure and loss</term>, they are hated by others, wicked, humiliated, and extremely lazy. || 7 ||

---

### The Results of Mars' Placement <note>original: "Kuja Phala"</note>

If **Mars** <term>Kuja|The planet Mars, representing energy, war, and siblings</term> is in the **First House** <term>Tanau|The house of the body or Ascendant</term>, the person has a scarred or wounded body <note>original: "kshata-tanu"</note>, is very cruel, has a short lifespan, and is adventurous or reckless with their wealth.

In the **Second House** <term>Vacasi|The house of speech and wealth</term>, they are disagreeable in speech, lack education and wealth, and associate with wicked people.

In the **Third House** <term>Anujē|The house of younger siblings</term>, they are virtuous, wealthy, brave, and a leader; they are happy, though they may lack younger siblings <note>The presence of a "malefic" planet like Mars in the house of siblings is often interpreted as causing a lack of brothers or sisters despite other successes.</note>.

In the **Fourth House** <term>Suhrdi|The house of friends and the heart</term>, they lack true friends but possess the comforts of land, houses, and vehicles. || 8 ||

In the **Fifth House** <term>Sutē|The house of children and intelligence</term>, they lack happiness from their children, are prone to accidents or troubles, are treacherous, and possess little intelligence.

In the **Sixth House** <term>Ripau|The house of enemies and debts</term>, they have strong physical desires, are wealthy and famous, and are like a king who is victorious over all enemies.

In the **Seventh House** <term>Dāra|The house of the spouse</term>, they engage in improper actions, suffer from diseases, are treated by poor physicians, and have a difficult or unfaithful spouse <note>original: "nṛta-dāravān"</note>.

In the **Eighth House** <term>Chidrē|The house of vulnerabilities or the eighth house</term>, they have a deformed body, lack wealth, have a short lifespan, and are criticized by society. || 9 ||

<summary>This page details the astrological effects of the Moon's placement in the final houses and Mars' placement in the first eight houses, focusing on health, character, and social status.</summary>
<keywords>Jyotisha, Moon (Shashi), Mars (Kuja), Vedic Astrology, House placements, Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao</keywords>

--- Page 58 ---
<meta>This page continues the astrological delineations. It completes the descriptions for Mars (Kuja) in the final three houses and begins the extensive descriptions for Mercury (Budha), starting from the First House (Ascendant) through the Ninth House.</meta>

<page-num>58</page-num>

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

If **Mars** <term>Kuja|The planet Mars, representing energy and aggression</term> is in the **Tenth House** <term>Nabhasi|The house of the sky, representing career and public standing</term>, the person is a friend to kings yet still hated by others; they are cruel, but also charitable and praised by prominent people.

In the **Eleventh House** <term>Bhave|The house of gains and income</term>, they are endowed with wealth and happiness, free from sorrow, brave, and possess a virtuous character.

In the **Twelfth House** <term>Vyaye|The house of expenditure and loss</term>, they suffer from eye deformities, are cruel, lack a spouse <note>original: "adāraḥ"; can also imply a lack of generosity</note>, act as a backbiter, and are considered base or lowly. || 10 ||

---

### The Results of Mercury's Placement <note>original: "Budha Phala"</note>

If **Mercury** <term>Budha|The planet Mercury, representing intellect, speech, and commerce</term> is in the **First House** <term>Janmani|The house of birth or Ascendant</term>, the person is known to have a long life, sweet and clever speech, and a deep understanding of the meaning of all scriptures.

In the **Second House** <term>Vaci|The house of speech and wealth</term>, they earn wealth through their own diligent efforts, are a poet of pure expression, and enjoy eating delicious, sweet foods.

In the **Third House** <term>Shaurye|The house of valor and younger siblings</term>, they are brave and have a moderate lifespan; they are accompanied by good siblings, yet they are hardworking and may experience moments of misery.

In the **Fourth House** <term>Suhridi|The house of friends, mother, and heart</term>, they are learned <note>original: "saṅkhyāvaṇ"; literally 'one who can count,' implying a scholar or mathematician</note>, speak charming words, have good friends, and enjoy the wealth and pleasures provided by others. || 11 ||

In the **Fifth House** <term>Panchamasthe|The house of children and intelligence</term>, they possess education, happiness, and power; they have many children and are experts in sacred chants and rituals <note>original: "māntikaḥ"; a practitioner of mantras</note>.

In the **Sixth House** <term>Dvishi|The house of enemies</term>, they are prone to anger, argumentative, a destroyer of the enemy’s power, yet lazy and harsh in their speech.

In the **Seventh House** <term>Aste|The house of the setting sun or marriage</term>, they are wise, well-dressed, full of glory, and marry a wife who brings wealth.

In the **Eighth House** <term>Jyeshtham|Here referring to the eighth position</term>, they are famous, long-lived, the supporter of their lineage, a leader, and a dispenser of justice <note>original: "daṇḍanētā"; one who leads with the rod of punishment/justice</note>. || 12 ||

In the **Ninth House** <term>Tapasi|The house of penance and fortune</term>, when **Mercury** <term>Budhe</term> is present, the person becomes an expert in education, wealth, proper conduct, and righteousness, and is an exceptionally eloquent speaker.

<summary>This page concludes the astrological effects of Mars's placement in the final houses and details the characteristics of a person born with Mercury in various houses, emphasizing intellect, speech, and social standing.</summary>

<page-num>59</page-num>

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

If **Mercury** <term>Budha|The planet of intellect and communication</term> is in the **Tenth House** <term>Khe|The 'sky' house, representing career and public life</term>, the person is successful in their endeavors, well-educated, strong, intelligent, happy, devoted to good deeds, and truthful.

In the **Eleventh House** <term>Labhage|The house of gains and income</term>, they enjoy a long life, are true to their word, possess vast wealth and happiness, and are supported by many attendants or servants.

In the **Twelfth House** <term>Vyaye|The house of loss and expenditure</term>, the person becomes miserable, lacks education, is subject to humiliation by others, and is prone to cruelty and laziness. || 13 ||

---

### The Results of Jupiter's Placement <note>original: "Guru Phala"</note>

If **Jupiter** <term>Surau|Literally 'The Teacher of the Gods', referring to Jupiter</term> is in the **First House** <term>Lagne|The Ascendant or house of self</term>, the person is handsome, performs virtuous deeds, lives a long life, is fearless, and is blessed with children.

In the **Second House** <term>Vitte|The house of wealth and speech</term>, they are an eloquent speaker, enjoy high-quality food, possess a handsome face, are wealthy, and are recognized as a scholar.

In the **Third House** <term>Shaurye|The house of valor and siblings</term>, the person may be treated with contempt or be stingy; they are well-known among their siblings but may engage in sinful acts and possess a wicked mind.

In the **Fourth House** <term>Sukhe|The house of comforts and mother</term> <note>original OCR: "వణౌ" (vanau); likely a transcription error for "సుఖే" (sukhe)</note>, they are endowed with the love of their mother, friends, attendants, children, and spouse, and possess abundant happiness and grains. || 14 ||

In the **Fifth House** <term>Sutasthe|The house of progeny and intelligence</term>, the person is wise and serves as a minister or advisor to a king, though they may experience some distress regarding their children.

In the **Sixth House** <term>Shashthe|The house of enemies and health</term>, they may be lazy and face some humiliation, yet they are a conqueror of enemies and are highly skilled in the use of sacred chants and incantations <term>Mantrabhichara|The practice of mystical formulas or spells</term>.

In the **Seventh House** <term>Madau|The house of partnership and marriage</term>, they are blessed with a noble spouse and children; they are exceedingly fortunate and even more generous than their father was.

In the **Eighth House** <term>Ashtame|The house of longevity and hidden things</term>, the person lives a life of service to others and may be poor or inclined toward sinful behavior, yet they are granted a long life. || 15 ||

In the **Ninth House** <term>Shubhe|The house of fortune and religion</term>, the person becomes famous, serves as a high-ranking minister, is wealthy, has children, and is always eager to perform righteous and religious deeds.

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

In the **Tenth House** <term>Nabhasi|Literally 'in the sky', referring to the highest point in the chart</term>, if **Jupiter** <term>Jive|Another name for Jupiter, meaning 'the living force'</term> is present, the person is of good conduct, possesses great fame, is exceedingly wealthy, and is a favorite of the king.

In the **Eleventh House** <term>Labhe|The house of gains</term>, they enjoy a long life, are wealthy and fearless, though they may have few children. They are long-lived, possess many vehicles for travel, and are generally successful.

In the **Twelfth House** <term>Vyaye|The house of expenditure or loss</term>, the person becomes an object of hatred, their speech is dismissed or censured, and they remain without children. O gentle one <note>The text addresses the reader as 'Sadhu', a common convention in Sanskrit verses</note>, such a person is lazy and lives as a servant to others. || 19 ||

### The Results of Venus's Placement <note>original: "Sukra Phala"</note>

In the **First House** <term>Tanau|The house of the body or self</term>, **Venus** <term>Kavir|Literally 'the poet', a common title for Venus or Sukra</term> makes a person have a beautiful body and attractive eyes; they are happy and blessed with a long life.

In the **Second House** <term>Arthage|The house of wealth</term>, Venus makes the person a scholar or poet, endowed with immense riches.

In the **Third House** <term>Vikrame|The house of valor and siblings</term>, the person lacks domestic happiness and wealth; they are miserly, unpleasant to others, and lack influence.

In the **Fourth House** <term>Sukhe|The house of comforts</term>, the person is blessed with excellent vehicles, beautiful homes, jewelry, fine clothing, and fragrant perfumes. || 20 ||

In the **Fifth House** <term>Atmaje|The house of children and intelligence</term>, they possess uninterrupted wealth, live like a king, are highly intelligent, and are blessed with children.

In the **Sixth House** <term>Shasthe|The house of enemies and debts</term>, their wealth is diminished, they are slandered or disgraced by women, and they live in a state of distress.

In the **Seventh House** <term>Made|The house of passion and partnership</term>, the person has a good spouse but may be inclined toward unchaste behavior; they are wealthy and may have a spouse involved in the arts.

In the **Eighth House** <term>Ashtame|The house of longevity</term>, the person is long-lived, a lord of the land, and very wealthy. || 21 ||

In the **Ninth House** <term>Shubhe|The 'auspicious' house of fortune and father</term>, they are blessed with a spouse, friends, and children, and their fortune is obtained through the favor of kings.

In the **Tenth House** <term>Nabhasi|The house of career</term>, the person achieves great fame, has many friends, possesses a stable and prosperous profession, and becomes a leader or lord.

### The Results of Saturn's Placement <note>original: "Sani Phala"</note>

If **Saturn** <term>Arkaje|Literally 'born of the Sun', a common epithet for Saturn</term> is in the **First House** <term>Lagne|The ascending sign or house of the self</term> while in its sign of exaltation or its own house, the person becomes equal to a ruler of the earth and the head of a country or city. In any other signs, the individual is afflicted by sorrow since childhood, remains under the power of poverty and misery, and is unclean and lazy. || 20 ||

In the **Second House** <term>Arthe|The house of wealth and speech</term>, Saturn makes one averse, destitute, and prone to injustice. However, in the **Third House** <term>Shaurye|The house of valor and siblings</term>, the **Son of the Sun** <term>Raviputrah|Saturn</term> grants a vast intellect, a generous nature, and happiness through a spouse, though he also makes the person dwell in other lands and potentially become lazy or distressed. || 21 ||

In the **Fourth House** <term>Vastubhe|Literally 'the place of the dwelling', referring to the house of comforts and the mother</term>, the person is sorrowful and separated from their home and mother; they are sickly in their childhood. In the **Fifth House** <term>Dhisthe|The house of the intellect and children</term>, they are confused, devoid of knowledge, children, wealth, and joy; they are deceitful and possess a wicked mind.

### The Results of Rahu's Placement <note>original: "Rahu Phala"</note>

If **Rahu** <term>Ahau|Literally 'the serpent', a name for Rahu</term> is in the **First House** <term>Lagne|The ascending sign</term>, the person has a short life but possesses the strength of wealth, though they are afflicted by diseases of the body or those related to the spouse. In the **Second House** <term>Vitte|The house of wealth and speech</term>, they use deceptive speech, suffer from facial diseases, yet are virtuous and gain wealth from the king; they are prone to anger but generally happy. || 25 ||

In the **Third House** <term>Shaurye|The house of valor and siblings</term>, one is proud, has conflicts with siblings, possesses a firm mind, and is long-lived and wealthy. In the **Fourth House** <term>Veshmani|The house of the home</term>, the person is foolish, a cause of sorrow, has few friends, a short life, and is only happy on rare occasions. || 26 ||

If **Rahu** is in the **Fifth House** <term>Sute|The house of children</term>, the person has nasal speech, is childless, hard-hearted, and suffers from stomach diseases...

### The Results of Ketu's Placement <note>original: "Pata Phala"; Ketu is the South Node of the Moon</term>

If **Ketu** <term>Patah|The descending node or shadow planet</term> is in the **First House** <term>Lagne|The Ascendant or house of self</term>, it makes the person ungrateful, unhappy, a slanderer, and pale in complexion. They may be displaced from their position, have a deformed body, and keep bad company.

In the **Second House** <term>Dhanasthe|The house of wealth and speech</term>, it makes one devoid of education and wealth, prone to using low or foul language, having poor eyesight, and constantly depending on others for food. || 28 ||

<page-num>64</page-num>

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

When **Ketu** is in the **Third House** <term>Sahaja-bhava|The house of siblings and brothers</term>, it causes the loss of siblings. 

In the **Fourth House** <term>Sukha-sthana|The house of happiness, mother, and property</term>, it brings about the loss of lands, fields, vehicles, mother, and one’s native birthplace; it also causes the person to live in the houses of others. || 29 ||

In the **Fifth House** <term>Suta-bhava|The house of children and progeny</term>, the planet Pata <note>original: "pataḥ"; a synonym for Ketu, the descending node of the Moon</note> causes the loss of sons, and brings suffering through the ailments of old age and "spirit" afflictions <note>original: "pisacha pida"; historically referring to mental disturbances or ailments attributed to supernatural influences</note>. It also grants a wicked intellect and a cruel nature. 

However, in the **Sixth House** <term>Shat-bhava|The house of enemies and health</term>, it bestows generosity, excellent qualities, steadiness, fame, authority, the destruction of enemies, and the successful completion of one’s desires. || 30 ||

In the **Seventh House** <term>Dyuna|The house of marriage and partners</term>, it leads to humiliation, attraction to immoral women, diseases caused by sharp instruments, separation from one's wife, and the loss of vital strength <term>mada-dhatu|Refers to physical vitality or reproductive health</term>. 

In the **Eighth House** <term>Randhra|The house of longevity and obstacles</term>, it results in a short lifespan, separation from loved ones, constant quarrels, wounds from weapons, and opposition in all one's undertakings. || 31 ||

In the **Ninth House** <term>Dharma|The house of fortune and religion</term>, it is said to incline the person toward sinful deeds and inauspiciousness. It causes a lack of paternal fortune, poverty, and brings reproach upon how one earns their living.

<meta>This page concludes the house-by-house predictions for the lunar node Ketu (referred to as Pata) and introduces general principles for planetary strength based on degrees. It then transitions into Chapter Nine, which focuses on the results of the various zodiac signs (Rashis).</meta>

<page-num>65</page-num>
<header>Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series</header>

In the **Tenth House** <term>Nabhas|The house of career and public standing</term>, Ketu causes obstacles to good deeds, impurity, and unworthy actions <note>original: "sat-karma-vighnam-ashuchitvam-avadya-krityam"</note>, yet it also makes one brilliant, courageous, and very famous. || 32 ||

In the **Eleventh House** <term>Labha|The house of gains and ambitions</term>, it leads to the accumulation of wealth, many virtues, great enjoyment, the possession of good materials and tools, and the successful attainment of all goals. 

In the **Twelfth House** <term>Ripha|The house of loss and expenditure</term>, the planet Pata <note>original: "pataḥ"; another name for Ketu</note> results in secret sins, spending on base or low things, loss of wealth, contrary or deviant behavior, and eye diseases. || 33 ||

When a planet is situated at the exact degree corresponding to the rising sign <term>Lagna|The Ascendant</term>, it bestows its full results. It is traditionally stated here that Rahu acts like Saturn <note>original: "shanivad-rahu"</note> and Ketu acts like Mars <note>original: "kujavad-ketu"</note> when delivering their effects. || 34 ||

Planets situated at the mid-point or exact degree of a house <term>Bhava|Astrological house</term> yield their full results. Depending on whether the degrees are lesser or greater than that point, the increase or decrease in the results should be predicted accordingly. || 35 ||

One born in **Aries** <term>Mesha|The first sign of the zodiac, symbolizing the Ram</term> has round eyes, weak knees, and a fierce temperament. They are fearful of water, eat sparingly, and have a strong sexual drive. They are wandering by nature, fickle, untruthful, and possess knowledge of the timing of rituals. <note>original: "vṛttākṣaṇo durbalajānu..."</note> || 1 ||

One born in **Taurus** <term>Vrishabha|The second sign, symbolizing the Bull</term> has broad thighs and a wide face, and is likely to be a farmer. They experience happiness in the middle and latter parts of their lives and are fond of women. They are generous, patient, capable of enduring hardship, and possess cattle. Such a person has a broad face and the noble qualities of a bull. || 2 ||

One born in **Gemini** <term>Mithuna|The third sign, symbolizing the Twins</term> has dark eyes and curly hair. They are fond of wealth and recreation and can easily understand the hidden intentions of others. They have a prominent nose, a love for singing and dancing, and prefer to reside within the sanctuary of their own home. || 3 ||

One born in **Cancer** <term>Karkataka or Kulira|The fourth sign, symbolizing the Crab</term> is often influenced by women, has a thick neck, and possesses many houses and great wealth. They have high hips, a short stature, a slightly crooked gait, and walk quickly. They are endowed with great intelligence, are fond of water, and have few sons. || 4 ||

One born in **Leo** <term>Simha|The fifth sign, symbolizing the Lion</term> has tawny eyes, a thick jaw, and a wide face. They are proud and courageous. They become angry at improper or unworthy acts, frequent forests and mountains, remain obedient to their mother, and stand at the forefront of steadfast righteousness. || 5 ||

One born in **Virgo** <term>Kanya|The sixth sign, symbolizing the Maiden</term> has drooping arms and is honored through the wealth and homes of others. They are devoted to the truth and have sweet, pleasant speech.

One born in **Capricorn** <term>Nakra|The tenth sign of the zodiac, symbolizing the Crocodile or Sea-Goat</term> suffers from wind-related ailments <term>Vata|In Ayurvedic medicine, the humor associated with air and movement; imbalances can cause joint pain or digestive issues</term> and is known for being bold or shameless. || 10 ||

One who hides their sins, has a body shaped like a pot, is skilled in causing harm, and is capable of enduring long travels while possessing little wealth; such a person is greedy, seeks the wealth of others, and experiences constant fluctuations of rise and fall. One born in **Aquarius** <term>Ghata|The eleventh sign of the zodiac, symbolizing the Pot or Water-bearer</term> is a lover of perfumes and flowers. || 11 ||

One who is devoted to their spouse, possesses a well-proportioned and beautiful body, and enjoys wealth derived from the sea or water <note>original: "toyaja-vitta-bhokta"; this refers to pearls, coral, or maritime trade</note>. They are learned, grateful, and overcome their enemies. Having auspicious eyes and being blessed with good fortune, such are the traits of one born in the **Final Sign** <term>Antyarashi|Pisces, the twelfth and final sign of the zodiac</term>. || 12 ||

By knowing the inherent nature, habitat, form, and color of each zodiac sign, one should determine the results that correspond to that sign.

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

One should logically state the results based on the **Ascendant** <term>Vilagna|The zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the time of birth, also known as the first house</term>; the very same results should also be stated based on the **Moon-sign** <term>Chandralagna|The zodiac sign in which the Moon is located at the time of birth, used as an alternative starting point for analysis</term>. || 13 ||

When a planet is in its **sign of exaltation** <term>Nijoccage|The specific zodiac sign where a planet reaches its maximum strength and produces its most beneficial results</term>, the person becomes a lord of the earth, which is filled with gems. They become an abode of wealth and receive the praise of kings. Endowed with noble qualities, such a person triumphs just like **King Vikramarka** <note>original: "Vikramarka"; referring to the legendary Emperor Vikramaditya, a model of the ideal, valorous, and generous ruler in Indian history</note> in matters of ethics, fame, valor, generosity, fortitude, and skill. || 14 ||

When a planet is in its own sign or under a master's influence, one obtains the favor and generosity of a ruler, or perhaps achieves authority and a stable, unwavering home. In its own time <note>original: "kale svake"; referring to the Graha Dasha or planetary period when that specific planet’s influence becomes dominant</note>, one acquires new property and lands, gains great respect among the people, and even recovers all lost items. || 15 ||

A planet situated in a **friendly sign** <term>Suhrut-kshetra|A zodiac sign ruled by a planet that is considered a "friend" to the planet occupying it</term> brings the fulfillment of tasks through the help of friends, as well as the forming of new friendships...

It leads to seeking shelter with lowly people, living in **unholy lands** <note>original: "Kikata-desha"; historically referring to the Magadha region, but used here generally to mean a place devoid of spiritual or cultural merit</note>, a state resembling death, constant wandering, and engagement in useless tasks. || 18 ||

If a planet reaches **combustion** <term>Maudyam|The state where a planet is so close to the Sun that it is obscured by its light, traditionally considered to lose its power</term>, it brings death soon, or the loss of one's wife, children, and wealth. It leads to useless quarrels, accusations, and humiliation. A planet in a neutral or stable position does not produce any special or exceptional results; the happiness or sorrow it generates remains steady and unchanging, just as it was before. || 19 ||

When a planet goes **retrograde** <term>Vakram|The apparent backwards motion of a planet as seen from Earth; in Indian astrology, this often increases the planet's strength</term>, it bestows results similar to its sign of exaltation... <note>The text breaks off here and likely continues on the next page.</note>

> Even if a planet has entered an unfavorable state, if it is situated in a **Vargottama** <term>Vargottama|A highly auspicious state where a planet occupies the same zodiac sign in both the primary birth chart and the Navamsha (ninth-part) divisional chart</term> position, it yields the same beneficial results as if it were in its **own sign** <term>Swakshetra|The specific zodiac sign ruled by that planet, where it is most comfortable and powerful</term>. || 20 ||

## Chapter Ten

### The House of the Spouse <note>original: "Kalatra Bhava"; the Seventh House in a horoscope, primarily representing marriage, the partner, and legal unions</note>

> If the houses of children <term>Suta Bhava|The 5th house</term> and the spouse <term>Kalatra Bhava|The 7th house</term>—reckoned from either the **Ascendant** <term>Lagna|The sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of birth</term> or the **Moon**—are occupied or aspected by benefic planets or by their own lords, those areas of life will flourish. Otherwise, success in these matters is denied. If **Venus** <note>original: "Sita"</note> is hemmed in by malefic planets, or is associated with or aspected by them, it indicates the death of the wife. || 1 ||

> If the Lord of the 7th house is positioned in the 5th house, the man will lose his wife or remain childless. Likewise, if the Lord of the 5th house is in the 7th, or if the Lord of the 8th house <term>Nidhaneshwara|The ruler of the house of death and transformation</term> is placed there, it certainly brings about the destruction of the wife.

> When the **waning Moon** <note>original: "Kshine-Indu"</note> is placed in the 5th house, and malefic planets occupy the 12th, 7th, and the 1st houses, the individual is deprived of both wife and children.

<summary>This page concludes the discussion on planetary strengths and begins Chapter 10, detailing the astrological combinations that determine the wellbeing or loss of a spouse and children.</summary>

<page-num>72</page-num>

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

> ...the loss of wealth and the destruction of the wife results from [certain malefic influences] in the Seventh House <term>Mada|The 7th house, also known as the house of passion or desire</term>... || 2 ||

> When **Venus** <note>original: "Shukra"</note> is in the visible half of the zodiac or in the Seventh House, the wife passes away. If **Mercury** <note>original: "Budha"</note> is placed in **Taurus** in the Seventh House, it causes the loss of the wife. Likewise, if **Jupiter** <term>Suraguru|The teacher of the gods</term> is in its sign of debilitation or is the ruler of the Seventh House in an unfavorable position, the wife's death is indicated.

> If **Saturn** <note>original: "Shani"</note> or **Mars** <note>original: "Bhauma"</note> is in the Seventh House, the wife passes away. However, if the **Sun**, **Saturn**, or **Mars** are positioned in their own signs <term>Swakshetra|The specific zodiac sign ruled by that planet</term>, the wife will be noble and virtuous. || 3 ||

> If the Seventh House or the Lord of the Seventh is associated with malefic planets, aspected by them, or hemmed between them; or if they are in a sign of debilitation or an enemy's sign, or are combust <term>Arka-kansyabhihate|Hidden by the glare of the Sun's rays</term>, one should predict the loss of the spouse.

> If **Venus** is with malefic planets in the Seventh, Fifth, or Ninth houses, the man will have a distressed or lost wife. If **Venus** is joined with or aspected by the divisions <term>Varga|Sub-divisional charts used for fine-tuned analysis</term> of **Mars**, he will be devoted to the wives of others. || 4 ||

> If **Mars** and **Saturn** are in the Seventh House, or if **Venus** and the **Moon** are weak, the person will be childless.

> If a "eunuch" planet <term>Kliba|Gender-neutral planets, specifically Mercury and Saturn</term> is in the Seventh House, or if two planets occupy that house, there may be two wives.

> If the Lord of the Seventh House and **Venus** are both in the Seventh House, the man will have two wives.

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

...by observing the planets positioned in the heavens <note>original: "gagananilayaiḥ"</note> associated with those two lords <note>referring to the lords of the 2nd and 7th houses</note>, one should determine the number of spouses. || 5 ||

---

The number of wives is determined by the planets residing in the Seventh House <term>Mada|The house of passion and marriage</term>. Their death is predicted if they are in malefic signs, while their longevity is assured if they are in benefic signs. 

If the Lord of the Seventh House <term>Dyūneśa|The planetary ruler of the house of marriage</term> is strong and is a benefic planet, the wife will be a virtuous woman <note>original: "Sādhvī"</note> and blessed with good sons. 

Even a malefic planet, if it is positioned in its own sign <term>Swagṛham|Own house</term> within the Seventh House, brings auspicious results for the wife. Barring the influence of the two "foot-drinkers" <note>original: "padvaya-ratnapān" – likely a reference to Saturn and Mars in a specific malefic role</note>, benefic planets situated in the Seventh House bring great happiness. || 6 ||

---

The destruction of the wife occurs when the Second House <term>Artha|House of wealth</term> and the Seventh House <term>Kāma|House of desire</term> are associated with or aspected by malefic planets. 

In such cases, the sages declare that inauspicious results will follow, particularly due to the influence of a "cruel glance" <term>Krūra-dṛṣṭi|A malefic aspect from a planet like Mars or Saturn</term>. 

Similarly, if these defects exist in the wife's chart regarding the Seventh House, the same misfortune applies to the husband. However, if these houses are aspected by or joined with benefic planets, the husband and wife will be truly fortunate. || 7 ||

---

If the **Moon** is positioned in an even zodiac sign <note>such as Taurus, Cancer, Virgo, Scorpio, Capricorn, or Pisces</note> within the Seventh House, there may be a second marriage <term>Punarbhū|A woman who marries a second time</term>. 

Alternatively, the husband might become childless or lose his wife. If malefic planets are in their sign of debilitation or in an enemy's sign within the Seventh House, the death of either the man or the woman may occur during the planetary periods associated with the Eighth House <term>Nidhana|The house of death</term> or the Second House <term>Dhana|The house of wealth</term>. || 8 ||

If the Ascendant <term>Lagna|The sign rising on the eastern horizon at the time of birth</term>, the Fifth House <term>Atma|The house representing the soul and children</term>, and the Seventh House <term>Lathra|The house of the spouse</term> are situated in even signs <term>Sama-rāśi|Even-numbered signs like Taurus or Cancer, often associated with feminine or nurturing qualities</term>; and if the Lord of the House and Venus <note>original: "Asurejye" — the preceptor of the Asuras, a title for Venus</note> are likewise positioned in even signs; and if the Lords of the Fifth and Seventh houses are not combusted by the Sun <note>original: "Sūryābhita-tapta-rahite" — refers to planets losing power due to proximity to the Sun's heat</note> and are endowed with strength, then the wife born to the man will be blessed with children. || 9 ||
