---

The birth sign <term>Rāśi|Zodiac sign</term> of the wife will be in the trine <note>The 1st, 5th, or 9th positions</note> of the sign or the divisional sign <term>Navāṃśa|A one-ninth subdivision of a sign used for fine-tuning predictions</term> occupied by the Lords of the First or Seventh houses. It may also be the sign where these lords are exalted or debilitated <note>original: "Nīcoccha-bhe"</note>. Furthermore, the wife’s birth sign can be predicted as the sign containing the most points in the Moon’s system of planetary strength <term>Aṣṭakavarga|A mathematical point system used to calculate the influence of planets</term>. || 11 ||

## Chapter Eleven

### Women’s Horoscopy <note>original: "Strī Jātaka"</note>

Whatever effects are described for the birth of men, O dear one, all of those should also be applied to women. However, the longevity of the husband <term>Māṅgalya|The state of being a wife whose husband is alive</term> is determined from the Eighth House <note>original: "Nidhana"</note>; children from the Ninth House; the beauty and grace of the body from the Ascendant <term>Lagna</term>; the husband and his good fortune from the Seventh House <note>original: "Asta-bhavana" — the house where the Sun sets</note>; and her character and sexual conduct from the Fourth House <note>original: "Sukha" — the house of happiness</note>. Benefic planets in the Seventh House provide auspicious results, while cruel/malefic planets <term>Krūra</term> provide inauspicious results. || 1 ||

If both the Ascendant and the Moon <note>original: "Himakara" — the cool-rayed one</note> are placed in even signs <term>Yugma-gā|Signs like Taurus, Cancer, or Virgo, considered feminine in nature</term> and are aspected by benefic planets, the woman will be endowed with good sons, a husband, ornaments, wealth, and excellent character. If they are located in odd signs <term>Oja-gā|Signs like Aries, Gemini, or Leo, considered masculine</term> and are associated with or aspected by malefic planets, she will possess a masculine nature...

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

...[if associated with or aspected by malefic planets, she will] have a crooked mind <note>original: "kuṭila-mati"</note>, be uncontrollable by her husband, harsh in nature, and live in poverty. || 2 ||

If the Seventh House <term>Mada|The house of marriage and partnership</term> or its subdivision <term>Aṃśa|The Navamsha or 1/9th divisional chart</term> is associated with a benefic sign, the woman will possess splendor, fame, knowledge, and wealth. However, if the Seventh House contains malefic signs or subdivisions, she will have a weak body <note>original: "ku-tanu"</note>, be a cheat <term>kitava</term>, lack wealth, and there may be separation <note>original: "viyoga"</note> between the couple. || 3 ||

If malefic planets are situated in the Eighth House <note>The 8th house, or 'Nidhana', governs the longevity of the marriage bond</note>, she becomes a widow. If there is a mixture of benefic and malefic planets there, she may remarry <note>original: "punar-vār-bhavēt"</note>. If cruel planets <term>Krūra</term> are in the house of lifespan <term>Āyu|The Eighth House</term>, she might even be the cause of her husband's death, while if they are in the house of wealth <term>Dhana|The Second House</term>, she herself may meet an early death while the husband is alive. || 3 ||

If the Moon <note>original: "Himagau" — the cool-rayed one</note> is in the fifth house <term>Suta-stha|The house of progeny</term> and is located in the signs of Scorpio <term>Ali</term>, Virgo <term>Stri</term>, Taurus <term>Go</term>, or Leo <term>Hari</term>, she will have few children <note>original: "alpa-tanayā"</note>. If the Seventh House <term>Madana-sadana</term> falls in the signs of Saturn or Mars <note>original: "Yamārārkāṃkarkṣē"</note>, she may suffer from diseases of the reproductive organs <note>original: "sāmaya-bhagā"</note>. || 4 ||

If the Seventh House contains a benefic planet but is also joined by malefics, she may become unfaithful <term>Kulaṭā</term>. If Saturn and Mars are in the Seventh House, or if the Ascendant <term>Lagna</term> and the Seventh House contain Venus <note>original: "Bhṛgu"</note> and are aspected by malefics, she is said to be a woman of loose character <term>Puṃścaly-abhihitā</term>. || 4 ||

If the Seventh House or its subdivision is in a benefic sign, she will be blessed with good fortune and a beautiful body. If the Ascendant and the Seventh House are occupied by benefic planets, she is a chaste and highly virtuous woman <note>original: "Sādhvī-yati-guṇā"</note>. If benefics are in the trines <term>Trikoṇa|The 1st, 5th, or 9th houses</term>, she will be endowed with happiness, children, wealth, and many virtues.

<meta>This page continues the specific astrological analysis of the Trishamsha (1/30th divisional chart) for women, determining personal character and fate based on the planetary positions at birth, specifically following the results of the Moon and the Ascendant.</meta>

<page-num>78</page-num>

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

If the planets are weak and cruel <term>Krūra|Malefic planets like Mars or Saturn</term>, the woman will be barren <note>original: "vandhyā"</note> or her children will die shortly after birth <note>original: "mṛtasunā"</note>. || 5 ||

When the Moon is in the sign of Mars <note>Aries or Scorpio</note>, the results in the various <term>Trishamsha|The 1/30th division of a zodiac sign</term> segments are respectively: a wicked woman, a servant, a virtuous woman, one possessing good character and wealth, and a deceitful, censurable woman. In the signs of Venus <note>Taurus or Libra</note>, the results are: one with many flaws, one who follows other men, one worthy of worship, and one who is wise and famous. In the signs of Mercury <note>Gemini or Virgo</note>, she will be deceitful, like a eunuch, chaste, or endowed with many good qualities and enthusiasm. || 6 ||

In the signs of Saturn <note>Capricorn or Aquarius</note>, the results are: a killer, one of highly honored qualities, an artisan <note>original: "śilpinī"</note>, and one of virtuous conduct. In the signs of the Moon <note>Cancer</note> or Jupiter <note>Sagittarius or Pisces</note>, she will be rich in virtues, possess great restraint, be known for her good character, or be unvirtuous. In the sign of the Sun <note>Leo</note>, she may be a servant, attached to others, devoted to her husband <note>original: "sthitapati"</note>, or unchaste <note>original: "asatī"</note>. In a sign of Mercury, she may be evil-minded, of low conduct, a queen <note>original: "dharaṇipati-vadhūḥ"</note>, or one who behaves like a man. || 7 ||

These are the results of the <term>Trishamsha</term> divisions when associated with the Moon or the Ascendant <term>Lagna|The sign rising on the eastern horizon at birth</term>. One should judge the outcome by considering the relative strength and weakness <note>original: "balābala"</note> of both the Moon and the Ascendant. || 8 ||

Experts in this science know that a woman born under the constellation of <term>Jyeshtha|The 18th lunar mansion, associated with seniority</term> causes the death of her husband's elder brother, his mother, his father, or his younger brother, in that specific order. <note>Traditional Vedic astrology suggests that certain lunar mansions in a bride's chart can impact the longevity of specific members of the groom's family.</note>

<page-num>79</page-num>

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

Those born under the lunar mansions <term>Nakshatra|The 27 divisions of the sky through which the Moon travels</term> of Chitra, Ardra, Ashlesha <note>original: "bhujagasvarāṭ"</note>, Shatabhisha, Mula, Krittika <note>original: "agni"</note>, or Pushya <note>original: "tiṣya"</note> will face specific hardships. They may be barren <note>original: "vandhyā"</note>, become a widow, have children who die in infancy, be abandoned by their beloved, or live in poverty. || 9 ||

If the Moon, the Ascendant <note>original: "udaya"</note>, and the Lord of the Ninth House <term>Bhāgyapa|The planet ruling the house of fortune and father</term> are joined by benefic planets, located in auspicious houses, and are radiant, the woman will be honored among her relatives. She will be skilled in performing righteous deeds, endowed with beauty and good fortune, and will bring joy to her husband. Accompanied by good sons, she will possess a virtuous character and remain a chaste woman <note>original: "satī"</note>. She will live for a long time as a <term>Sumaṅgalī|A woman whose husband is alive, considered a highly auspicious state</term> if Jupiter <note>original: "sūryāva-acyu"</note> is not in the eighth house. || 10 ||

When the Moon <note>original: "śītajyoti"</note> is in a non-increasing house <term>Anupacaya|The 1st, 2nd, 4th, 5th, 7th, 8th, 9th, and 12th houses from the Ascendant</term> and is aspected by Mars <note>original: "kuja"</note>, the menstrual cycle of a woman is fertile and capable of producing a child; otherwise, it is fruitless. If the Moon is aspected by Jupiter <note>original: "guru"</note> while the man is in his own increasing house <term>Upacaya|The 3rd, 6th, 10th, and 11th houses</term>, he should perform the act of conception <note>original: "niṣeka"</note>. This should be done at a time rich in auspicious influences, avoiding the forbidden periods of the lunar cycle <note>original: "parvādi-kālojjhite"</note>. || 11 ||

> If the lords of the fifth house <term>Sutabheśa|The planet ruling the house of children</term> and Jupiter <note>original: "jīvāḥ"</note> are well-placed from the Ascendant <term>Lagna|The sign rising on the eastern horizon at birth</term> and the Moon; if the fifth house <note>original: "sutarṣe"</note> is aspected by benefics or the lords of auspicious houses; and if the lord of the Ascendant and the lord of the fifth house are in conjunction, aspecting each other, or have exchanged houses, then the attainment of children <note>original: "putrasiddhi"</note> is certain. || 1 ||

> If malefic planets <note>original: "pāpai"</note> occupy the fifth house as measured from the Ascendant, its lord, or the Moon; if these houses are aspected by malefics and lack the aspect or presence of benefic planets; or if the fifth house and its lord are surrounded by malefics or placed in "dusthanas" <term>Duḥsthāna|Difficult houses, specifically the 6th, 8th, or 12th</term>, children will not be born by any means. || 2 ||

> If a malefic planet is in its own sign in the fifth house, one will possess children <note>original: "tanayabhāk"</note>. If a malefic is in the fifth house while the lord of the sign is also a malefic, many children will be born <note>interpretive note: this often refers to the strength of the house lord despite its nature</note>. However, if the fifth house is a benefic's sign but occupied by a malefic, it destroys children. The signs known for "few children" <note>original: "alpa sutarkṣa"</note> are recognized as Scorpio <note>original: "ali"</note>, Taurus <note>original: "vṛṣa"</note>, Virgo <note>original: "strī"</note>, and Leo <note>original: "siṃha"</note>. If the fifth house falls in one of these signs, the individual will have few children after a long period of time. || 3 ||

When the Sun is in a sign of few children, Saturn <note>original: "mande"</note> is in the eighth house <note>original: "nidhanage"</note>, and Mars <note>original: "kuje"</note> is in the Ascendant...

If Jupiter <note>original: "devedye"</note> is in the sign of the Moon <note>Cancer</note> in the fifth house, the native will have many daughters. || 5 ||

<page-num>83</page-num>

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

The sages declare that there will be many children <note>original: "bahuputratam"</note> when the lord of the fifth house <term>Sutanātha|The planetary ruler of the house of children</term> is endowed with great strength <term>Vīrya|The potency or power of a planet based on its position</term>, leading to the birth of virtuous sons. || 10 ||

If the lord of the fifth house and the fifth house itself are located in masculine signs or masculine subdivisions <term>Aṃśa|A divisional portion of a zodiac sign used for fine-tuned analysis</term>, and are conjoined with or aspected by male planets, the birth of a son is predicted. 

If the lord of the fifth house <note>original: "suteśe"</note> and the fifth house <note>original: "sutarthe"</note> are in feminine signs or subdivisions and are conjoined with or aspected by female planets, the birth of daughters will occur. || 11 ||

When the Sun <note>original: "arka"</note> and Venus <note>original: "sita"</note> are strong, located in their own signs or subdivisions, and occupy the houses of growth <term>Upachaya|The 3rd, 6th, 10th, and 11th houses which indicate increase and improvement</term> in a man's birth chart;

And if Mars <note>original: "kuja"</note> and the Moon <note>original: "candra"</note> are similarly positioned in a woman’s chart, then conception <note>original: "garbhaḥ"</note> occurs. || 12 ||

By analyzing the fifth house through its subdivisions <term>Navāṃśaka|The ninth-part division of a sign, used to see the hidden strength of planets</term>—ensuring the planets are not in signs of enemies or debilitation—

And by considering the conjunctions of the lord of the fifth house and the position of Jupiter <note>original: "guru"</note>, the wise calculate the specific number and fate of the children. || 13 ||

<page-num>84</page-num>

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

For women, the strength of the "field" <term>Kṣetra-balam|The astrological capacity of a woman to conceive, calculated using the positions of Mars and the Moon</term> is declared to be conducive to childbirth when the relevant planets occupy even signs and even divisions <term>Aṃśa|The divisional units of a zodiac sign, such as the Navamsha</term>. If they are in a mixture of even and odd signs, children are granted only after great effort.

For men, the strength of the "seed" <term>Bīja-balam|The astrological capacity of a man to father a child, calculated using the positions of the Sun, Venus, and Jupiter</term> is declared to grant a son when the Sun, Venus, and Jupiter are in odd signs and odd divisions. If these are mixed, the result is said to be mixed. || 14 ||

Multiply the position of the Moon by five and subtract the position of the Sun <note>original: "pañcāghnācchaśinaḥ... bhānussuṭaṃ śodhayet"</note> <note>This refers to the mathematical formula for calculating the 'Beeja' and 'Kshetra' longitudes to determine fertility.</note>. Having derived the lunar day <term>Tithi|A lunar day in the Vedic calendar</term> from this, if it falls in the bright fortnight <term>Śukla Pakṣa|The waxing phase of the Moon, considered auspicious</term> on a favorable day, a son will be born, even if effort is required.

In the dark fortnight <term>Kṛṣṇa Pakṣa|The waning phase of the Moon</term>, children may not be born. One should judge based on the relative strength of the two fortnights. A son is not born if the calculation falls on the New Moon <note>original: "darśe"</note>, on a "pierced" day <term>Chidra Tithi|Specific lunar days, such as the 4th, 6th, 8th, 9th, 12th, and 14th, often avoided for major life events</term>, or during the astrological periods known as Viṣṭi or Sthira Karaṇa. || 15 ||

If the astrological timing of Viṣṭi or Sthira Karaṇa is the cause of the delay, one should overcome this through the recitation of the Hymn to the Cosmic Man <term>Puruṣa Sūktam|A foundational Vedic hymn used in rituals for progeny and purification</term>. 

On the sixth lunar day, the worship of Lord Guha <note>original: "guhārcanam" — another name for Lord Subrahmanya/Skanda, the deity associated with the sixth tithi</note> should be performed. On the fourth lunar day, one should certainly worship the King of Serpents <note>original: "nāgarājam"</note>. || 16 ||

On the ninth lunar day, one should listen to the recitation of the Ramayana <note>Listening to the birth of Rama in the Ramayana is a traditional remedy for childless couples</note>.

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

If the calculation falls on the eighth lunar day, one should observe the Shravana Vow <term>Śravaṇa Vratam|A ritual fast and observance dedicated to Lord Vishnu, typically performed when the Moon is in the Shravana constellation</term>. 

If it falls on the fourteenth, one should perform the worship of Rudra <term>Rudra-pūjā|A powerful Vedic ritual dedicated to Lord Shiva to remove obstacles and negative karmic influences</term>.

For the satisfaction of the ancestors <note>original: "pitṄṇām"</note>, ritual offerings should be made here on the fifteenth lunar day <term>Pañcadaśī|The New Moon day, or Amavasya, dedicated to ancestral rites</term>. 

From the tenth day of the dark fortnight <term>Kṛṣṇa Pakṣa|The waning phase of the moon</term> onwards, one must put forth great effort. During the three divisions of the fortnight, one should worship the Serpent King <term>Nāgarājam|The king of the divine serpents; worship is often prescribed in India to remedy 'Naga Dosha' or afflictions believed to cause infertility</term>, perform ritual baths, and serve Lord Hari <note>original: "harī"</note> <note>A name for Lord Vishnu</note> in the proper order. || 18 ||

If the Lord of the House of Children <term>Putreśaḥ|The planet that rules the 5th house in a birth chart, which governs offspring</term> is located in an enemy's sign, is debilitated, is combust <note>original: "astamayago"</note> <note>A planet is 'combust' when it is too close to the Sun, losing its strength</note>, or is placed in the twelfth, eighth, or sixth houses <note>In Vedic astrology, the 6th, 8th, and 12th houses are 'Dusthanas' or houses of suffering and loss</note>.

Likewise, if a planet occupies the House of Children, or if the ruler of a difficult house <note>original: "duḥsthānapa"</note> is placed there, one should declare this to be the primary cause for the lack of children. One should then state the specific reason for the lack of offspring—whether it concerns specific deities, sacred trees, or animals—based on the nature of the planets occupying those signs. || 19 ||

<page-num>86</page-num>

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

If the affliction <term>Doṣa|A flaw, blemish, or karmic obstruction in one's astrological chart</term> is caused by Venus <note>original: "śukra"</note>, it arises from the anger of a married woman <term>Suvāsinī|A woman whose husband is alive, often honored as a living embodiment of the Goddess</term> or the Goddess herself due to a mental transgression. 

If the affliction originates from the deity residing in one's own village, and is associated with Mercury <note>original: "budhe"</note>, it is caused by the death of a young boy or the anger of Lord Vishnu. || 20 ||

Other causes include the betrayal of a priest <term>Dvija|Literally 'twice-born'; a member of the scholarly or priestly class</term>, a teacher <term>Guru</term>, or the gods, passed down through the lineage. It may also stem from cutting down divine trees or flowering trees. 

I believe it can also result from the curse of a virtuous woman <term>Sādhvī|A chaste and righteous woman</term>, sins involving a herd of cows, or the influence of nature spirits <term>Yakṣas|Nature spirits who guard the earth's treasures</term>. Furthermore, it may be caused by the destruction of the sacred fig tree <term>Aśvattha|The Ficus religiosa, considered a residence of the divine</term> or by the restless spirits <term>Pretas</term> and demons <term>Piśācas</term> belonging to the Lord of the Dead <note>original: "pitṛpateḥ", referring to Yama</note>. || 21 ||

When the Sun, Saturn, or Rahu are involved, the curse is said to come from the collective. In the case of Ketu, it is a Brahmin’s curse <term>Brāhmaṇa-śāpa|A potent curse resulting from the mistreatment of a priest or scholar</term>. If Gulika <term>Gulika|A mathematical point in an astrological chart, often called the 'son of Saturn,' signifying hidden or dark karmic debts</term> is present, one should declare it a curse arising from a ghost. 

If the Moon and Gulika are joined, they say it is due to the sin of killing a woman or a cow. If Jupiter or Ketu are present here, it is an affliction caused by a deity. || 22 ||

In this way, at the moment of birth, those who are wise in these matters describe the sins earned through the actions <term>Karma</term> of many previous lives.

## Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

The attainment of a son <term>Putraprāpti|The successful birth or arrival of a male child</term> occurs when the Lord of the Ascendant <term>Lagnanātha|The planet that rules the first house of the birth chart</term> moves into the House of Children <note>the 5th house</note> or the House of Gains <note>original: "dhīśāptabham," referring to the 11th house</note>. || 26 ||

By analyzing the combinations of the Lords of the Ascendant, the House of Desires <note>the 7th house</note>, and the House of Children <note>the 5th house</note>, one can determine the timing. The birth of a son <note>original: "sutodbhavaḥ"</note> occurs during the major or minor planetary periods <term>Daśā-Apahāra|Cycles of time in a person's life ruled by specific planets</term> of the planets situated in the 5th house, the planets aspecting that house, or the Lord of the 5th house itself. || 27 ||

The attainment of a son occurs during the periods or sub-periods of the Lord of the 5th house, Jupiter <note>original: "Guru"</note>, or the lords of the zodiac signs and divisional signs <term>Aṃśaka|The sub-divisions of a sign, such as the Navamsha chart, used for fine-tuning predictions</term> in which they are placed, provided these planets are endowed with strength. || 28 ||

When Jupiter <note>original: "Jīva"</note> transits the trine positions <term>Trikoṇa|The 1st, 5th, and 9th houses, which are considered auspicious in Vedic astrology</term> relative to the sign or divisional sign occupied by the Lord of the 5th house, the birth of a son occurs for men. A wise astrologer should determine the characteristics of the lineage by examining the time of conception <term>Adhāna|The astrological moment of fertilization</term> as well as the time of birth. || 29 ||

When Jupiter transits the point representing the combined mathematical longitudes of the Lord of the Birth Star and the Lord of the 5th house—or moves into a trine position from that point—the birth of a son occurs. || 30 ||

From the Ascendant of Conception <term>Niṣēkalagna|The specific zodiac sign rising at the moment of conception</term>, if the Sun is in the third...

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

When the Sun, through its regular transit, arrives at the zodiac sign representing the conception-ascendant <term>Ādhānalagna|The astrological sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of conception</term> or in its trines <term>Trikoṇa|The 1st, 5th, or 9th houses, considered the most auspicious positions in a chart</term>, one should predict the actual birth of human beings. || 31 ||

The birth of a child should be predicted based on the lord of the house of children <note>the 5th house</note> or the house of fortune <note>the 9th house</note> relative to the conception-ascendant, which occurs due to the influence of past merits <note>original: "puṇyavaśāt"</note>. If the conception-ascendant is aspected by or associated with benefic planets <note>such as Jupiter, Venus, or a well-placed Mercury</note>, the person born will be endowed with a long life and great wealth. || 32 ||

A wise astrologer should determine the position of the Moon at the time of birth by looking at the twelfth-division <term>Dvādaśāṃśa|A 2.5-degree sub-division of a zodiac sign</term> occupied by the Moon at the time of conception. The birth Moon will be located as many signs away from Aries—or from the sign the conception Moon occupied—as the number of twelfth-divisions the conception Moon had already passed. || 33 ||

In matters of astrological queries, the adoption of a son, the sacred thread ceremony <term>Upanayana|A rite of passage marking the start of formal education</term>, the marriage of a daughter, and the first menses—as well as the moment of conception itself—the results should be declared to be as significant as those derived from the birth chart and the birth ascendant. || 34 ||

### On the State of Longevity <note>original: "Āyurbhāva"</note>

When a boy is born, the ancient masters <note>original: "āryaiḥ"</note> state that his lifespan should be examined first, and only after that should the specific results <note>of the planetary positions</note> be considered. Those who know the signs <note>expert astrologers</note> must investigate all the qualities and virtues of the life while it exists. || 1 ||

Some scholars say the birth-ascendant <term>Lagna|The zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of birth</term> is the moment of conception. Others say it is when the head first appears <note>original: "śīrṣodayaṃ"</note>, while some say it is the moment the child's body touches the earth. Experts in the science of time <term>Horāvid|A scholar proficient in Horā Śāstra, or predictive astrology</term> declare the ascendant to be the exact moment of the separation of the consciousness and the physical body <note>from the mother</note>. || 2 ||

For those born of human wombs, the measure of longevity cannot be determined with certainty until the twelve years of childhood have passed. A child may meet destruction <note>death or severe illness</note> due to the sinful deeds committed by the mother or the father, or through the influence of childhood afflictions <term>Bālagraha|Malevolent spirits or planetary combinations believed to cause ailments in infants</term>. || 3 ||

During the first four years, the child is affected by the sins committed by the mother. In the middle four years <note>ages five to eight</note>, the child is affected by the accumulation of sins of the father. In the final four years <note>ages nine to twelve</note> of this cycle, the child—

<note>The text concludes mid-sentence; based on tradition, the final four years are usually attributed to the child's own past-life karma.</note>

Indeed, the lifespan of human beings is one hundred years, and it is divided into three parts. It is universally accepted that these divisions are Short, Medium, and Long life. || 7 ||

The ruler of the planetary period <term>Dasha|A specific time-block in a person's life ruled by a particular planet</term> may cause this in a corresponding number of months, and the lord of the ninth-division <term>Navamsha|A sub-divisional chart used to assess the hidden strength of planets</term> may cause it in that many days. One should carefully analyze these various combinations and the presence of malefic planets in the eighth house to predict the misfortune of the infant. || 13 ||

The relationship between the lord of the Ascendant and the lord of the eighth house, or between the lord of the Ascendant and the lord of the Moon-sign, should be examined through their Decanates <term>Drekkana|A division of a zodiac sign into three parts of 10 degrees each</term>, their Navamshas, and their twelfth-divisions <term>Dvadasamsha|A sub-chart where a sign is divided into 12 parts, often used to study ancestral influences</term>...

### Technical Vocabulary
**Degrees of Death** (*mṛtyubhāgāḥ*): Specific sensitive points in each zodiac sign where the presence of the Moon or Ascendant is considered dangerous.
**Starting with Aries** (*meṣādīnāṃ*): The standard order of the zodiac used for calculating these critical degrees.
**Lost Lifespan** (*gatāyuḥ*): A technical term for a birth chart that indicates a very short life or immediate death.
**Eighth House** (*randhra*): Literally "the hole" or "the void," referring to the house of death.
**Quadrants** (*krendesu*): The central houses of the chart; if occupied by bad influences, the "structure" of life collapses.
**Between Cruel Planets** (*lagnenjvogramadhye*): A condition called *Papa-kartari*, where the Moon or Ascendant is "squeezed" between two harmful planets.
**Of the Infant** (*śiśoḥ*): Referring specifically to the vulnerabilities of early childhood.
**Decanate** (*drekkāṇa*): A ten-degree division used to refine the timing of events.

> Whether a lifespan is long, medium, or short should be declared based on whether the Lord of the Ascendant and the Lord of the Moon-sign are situated in movable, fixed, or dual signs. || 14 ||
<note>original: "āyurdīrghasamālpatāṃ caranagavyāṅgeśvare’tha sthire..." This refers to a specific calculation method where the nature of the zodiac signs (Movable/Chara, Fixed/Sthira, and Dual/Dvisvabhava) containing key planets determines the longevity category.</note>

When the Lord of the Ascendant and benefic planets <term>Shubhas|Supportive planets like Jupiter, Venus, Mercury, and a strong Moon</term> are placed in the quadrants <term>Kendra|The 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses</term> and other houses in order, they grant long, medium, or short life. However, if the Lord of the eighth house <term>Randhresha|The planetary ruler of the 8th house, which governs death and longevity</term> and cruel planets are so placed, they produce the opposite result.

Long life is indicated when there is friendship between the Lord of the Moon-sign and the Lord of the eighth house, or between the Lord of the Ascendant and the Lord of the eighth. If they are enemies, life is short; if they are neutral, life is medium. || 15 ||
<note>original: "janmeśāṣṭamanādhayorudayapacchidreśayormaitrato" - This highlights the "Maitri" or planetary relationship system used to judge how harmoniously the life-force (Ascendant) interacts with the house of death (8th).</note>

If the Lord of the Ascendant, the ruler of the Ascendant's <term>Navamsha|A divisional chart that divides each sign into nine parts to see a planet's hidden strength</term>, the Lord of the Moon-sign, and the ruler of the Moon's Navamsha are all more powerful than the Lord of the eighth house, the person will have a long life. If the opposite is true, the life is not long. || 16 ||

If the Lord of the house of death is significantly stronger than the Lord of the Ascendant and is placed in a quadrant, or if the Lord of the house of death is joined by malefic planets, the person’s life will be short, or perhaps medium...

...through effort and overcoming crises, one attains the maximum lifespan. || 17 ||
<note>original: "rutsaahasankatavashaat paramaayureti" — This implies that while planetary positions set the stage, the vital energy or the resolution of "sankata" (crises) determines if one reaches the full potential of their allotted time.</note>

A person is destined for a short life if death occurs during Saturn’s first cycle <term>Bhagana|The time it takes for a planet to complete one full revolution through the twelve signs of the zodiac; for Saturn, this is approximately 30 years</term>. If death occurs during the second cycle, it is considered a medium lifespan, and if in the third cycle, it is a long life. The wise astrologer, having carefully determined the movements of Saturn, Jupiter, the Sun, and the Moon, and examining the major planetary periods <term>Dasha|A specific span of time in a person's life ruled by a particular planet</term> and sub-periods <term>Bhukti|The sub-division of a Dasha</term>, should clearly declare the timing of departure from this world. || 18 ||
<note>original: "prathamabhagane nashyati shane" — This provides a remarkably simple rule of thumb: surviving the first Saturn return (age 30) moves one out of the 'short life' category, and surviving the second (age 60) moves one into 'long life'.</note>

If the Lord of the Ascendant <term>Lagnesha|The planet ruling the first house, representing the physical body and life force</term> is associated with malefic planets, is combust <term>Ravihataruchir|Literally 'light-struck by the Sun'; a planet becomes 'combust' and loses its power when it gets too close to the Sun</term>, or is situated in a sign of debilitation or in an enemy's sign; and if that planet enters the "difficult houses" <term>Dusthanas|The 6th, 8th, or 12th houses, associated with debt, disease, and death</term> during a transit <term>Gochara|The current movement of planets through the zodiac relative to one's birth chart</term>; or if such a combination occurs within the Ascendant itself, the death of the embodied soul is predicted. This should be confirmed by also examining the divisional charts, specifically the <term>Navamsha|The 1/9th division of a sign, used to see hidden strengths</term> and the <term>Drekkana|The 1/3rd division of a sign, often used to predict the nature of death</term>, as well as the specific Rising Sign. || 19 ||

When the Moon, the Lord of the sign occupied by the Moon <term>Arudha-grihadhipa|In this context, the ruler of the Chandra Lagna or Moon-sign</term>, and the Lord of the Ascendant—these three—possess great strength and are aspected by or joined with benefic planets...
<note>The text on this page ends mid-sentence, beginning a new rule regarding the conditions for health and longevity when the three primary 'lenses' of the chart (Body, Mind, and Soul) are well-supported.</note>

### Chapter Fourteen
## Diagnosis of Disease

The concern regarding disease should be declared based on the planets situated in the House of Sickness <term>Rogabhava|The 6th house of a birth chart, which rules health, enemies, and debt</term>, or those situated in the houses of Loss <term>Vyaya|The 12th house</term> and Death <term>Mrityu|The 8th house</term>. It can also be determined by the Lord of the 6th house <term>Rogeshwara|The planet that rules the sign on the cusp of the 6th house</term> or the planets associated with him, particularly when two or three of these indicators act in combination. || 1 ||
<note>In Vedic astrology, the 6th, 8th, and 12th houses are known as "Dusthanas" or difficult houses. Their influence is primarily used to diagnose physical and mental afflictions.</note>

The Sun indicates diseases caused by bile <term>Pitta|One of the three 'doshas' or humors in Ayurvedic medicine, associated with fire and metabolism</term>, heat, fever, internal burning of the body, epilepsy, and ailments of the heart...
<note>The text here begins a list of specific ailments associated with each planet. The Sun, representing heat and the vital soul, is naturally linked to the circulatory system and "fiery" imbalances.</note>

[Text breaks off mid-verse]

Fear from fire kindled by wood, distress from women, poison, the loss of one's wife and son, danger from four-legged animals; || 2 ||

trouble from thieves and the king, fear from the God of Justice <term>Dharmadeva|Yama, the lord of death and justice</term>, snakes, and spirits that have passed on—these are the fears to be declared.

Sleepiness, laziness, phlegm-based diarrhea, boils, and cold fever are caused by the Moon; || 3 ||
<note>original: "śītajvaraṃ candramāḥ" — In Ayurveda, the Moon rules over 'Kapha' (phlegm) and fluids; its affliction leads to "cold" illnesses.</note>

so too are injuries from horns or water, a loss of digestive fire <term>Agnimandya|A weakened metabolic state or "slow digestion" which is the root of many diseases in Indian medicine</term>, a lack of appetite, pain caused by women, and jaundice.

Mental peace, disorders of the blood, fear from water, and seizures affecting children <term>Balagraha|A category of pediatric illnesses traditionally attributed to planetary or spiritual influences</term>;

one should also declare fear arising from the Goddess Durga, the Kinnaras <term>Kinnara|Celestial musicians, often depicted as half-human and half-horse</term>, the God of Justice, snakes, and the Yakshas <term>Yaksha|Nature spirits or guardians of hidden treasures</term>.

Thirst, bad dreams, anger, bile-related fever, fire, poison, weapons, severe leprosy or skin diseases, and disorders of the eyes; || 4 ||
<note>original: "tṛṣṇā svapnakopa pittajvara..." — Mars, being a "fiery" planet, is linked to heat-based conditions like fever (pitta) and external injuries.</note>

tumors <term>Gulma|A term for abdominal masses, bloating, or chronic diverticular issues</term>, epilepsy, injury to the bone marrow, a harsh temperament, and fractures or breaks in the body;

the Son of the Earth <note>original: "avani-sūnu" — A poetic name for the planet Mars</note> also brings about trouble from kings and thieves, and conflict with siblings, children, friends, and enemies.

He also causes fear from demons <term>Rakshasa|Powerful, often malevolent supernatural beings</term>, Gandharvas <term>Gandharva|Celestial singers</term>, terrifying planetary spirits, and diseases of the noble or elderly.

Confusion, harsh speech, diseases of the eyes, throat, and nose, and fever; || 5 ||
<note>original: "bhrāntiṃ durvacanaṃ..." — These symptoms, involving communication and the sensory organs of the head, are typical of Mercury (Budha), though the planet's name appears in the subsequent lines not fully captured here.</note>

fever born from an imbalance of the three humors <term>Tridosha|The three vital energies: Vata (wind), Pitta (bile), and Kapha (phlegm)</term>, poison, skin disorders, and anemia;

bad dreams, itching or scabies, accidents involving falling into fire, and exhaustion from confinement or hard labor.

...as well as distress from the Gandharvas <term>Gandharva|Celestial musicians and nature spirits</term>, the earth, mansions, and planetary influences—these are the afflictions Mercury declares. || 5 ||

Jupiter <note>original: "Jiva"</note> indicates abdominal swelling <term>Gulma|A localized abdominal swelling or tumor-like condition in Ayurveda</term>, internal wounds, fever, grief, delusion, and diseases born of phlegm <term>Kapha|One of the three bio-energetic forces (doshas) in Ayurveda, associated with structure, water, and earth</term>, as well as ailments of the ears and intense mental confusion.

It also signifies diseases arising from the desecration of temples or sacred treasures, the curse of a Brahmin <term>Mahideva|Literally 'Earth-god', a common term for a priest or Brahmin</term>, and ailments caused by Kinnaras, Yakshas, Devas, Vidyadharas <term>Vidyadhara|A class of semi-divine beings in Hindu mythology possessing magical knowledge</term>, and similar beings. Jupiter himself points toward such diseases arising from the improper treatment of wise elders or teachers. || 6 ||

One should also declare ailments of the private parts, difficulty in urination, and the involuntary loss of semen. Venus further indicates a loss of physical radiance caused by one's wife or other women, wasting diseases <term>Shosha|A state of physical emaciation or pulmonary consumption</term>, and trouble from the Yoginis <term>Yogini|A group of powerful female deities or spirits</term>, the Yakshis, the Mother-Goddesses <term>Matrugana|A group of mother-deities often associated with childhood illnesses</term>, or dear friends. || 7 ||

Saturn <note>original: "Arkaja", meaning 'Son of the Sun'</note> indicates disorders of wind and phlegm, injuries to the feet, calamities, lethargy, and exhaustion. It points toward confusion, stomach pain, the destruction of servants or dependents, and pain in the flanks <note>original: "pārśvahati"</note>.

Saturn also brings about calamities involving one's wife and children, deep internal pain, and distress of the heart...

If the lord of the fifth house is present in the ascendant, there will be stomach ailments; similarly, if the lord of the seventh or eighth house is involved, there will be rectal disease. If Venus <note>original: "Shukra"</note> is similarly afflicted, it indicates diseases of the private parts. || 10 ||

Rahu aspected by Mars indicates boils or pimples <note>original: "pilakam"</note>. Saturn joined with Rahu indicates internal tumors <term>Gulma|A localized abdominal swelling or tumor-like condition</term>. A waning Moon in watery signs joined with malefic planets indicates chronic consumption and diseases related to the primary organs. || 11 ||

### Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

->Every being who is born eventually moves toward death; I shall now describe its cause.<-

->Death occurs due to the planets situated in the eighth house, those aspecting it, or the diseases indicated by the strongest among them.<-

->One should certainy predict the cause of death based on the ailments of the eighth house <term>Randhra|The eighth house of a horoscope, representing longevity and the mode of death</term>, or by the lord of the eighth house, or the lord of the twenty-second decan <term>Khara-tribhaga-pati|The ruler of the 22nd Drekkana, a specific 10-degree division of the zodiac used in Vedic astrology to identify fatal influences</term>. || 12 ||<-

->If a planet occupies the eighth house <term>Nidhana|The house of death</term>, death is caused by the diseases associated with that planet or the planet that aspects it.<-

->If the eighth house is empty of planets, death arises from the imbalances or defects inherent to the nature of that specific zodiac sign. || 13 ||<-

->The Sun <note>original: "Ina"</note> indicates death by fire, heat, fever, bile <term>Pitta|One of the three Ayurvedic humors, associated with fire and metabolism</term>, or weapons. The Moon <note>original: "Chandra"</note> indicates death by cholera <note>original: "vishūchi"</note>, watery diseases, or abdominal ailments.<-

->Mars <note>original: "Kshitija"</note> indicates death by blood disorders, fire, lowly enemies, sorcery, or weapons.<-

->Mercury <note>original: "Budha"</note> indicates death from anemia or dizziness. Jupiter <note>original: "Guru"</note> brings a peaceful death or one caused by phlegm <term>Kapha|The Ayurvedic humor associated with water and earth</term>.<-

->Venus <note>original: "Kavi"</note> indicates death from diseases of sexual origin, while Saturn <note>original: "Shani"</note> indicates death from wind imbalances <term>Vata|The Ayurvedic humor associated with air and movement</term> or severe feverish convulsions. || 14 ||<-

->Rahu causes death by phlegm, food poisoning, venomous bites, or smallpox <note>original: "masurika"</note>.<-

->Ketu <note>original: "Shikhi"</note> causes an unnatural death <note>original: "durmaranam"</note> resulting from the opposition of enemies or from insects and vermin. || 15 ||<-

<gloss>Death, Lord of the eighth house, Death/The End, By a planet, In the house of death, Fever, Bile, Weapon, Cholera, From phlegm, Smallpox, Unnatural death</gloss>

->In Taurus <note>original: "Vrisha"</note>, death results from an imbalance of the three bodily humors <term>Tridosha|The three physiological forces: Vata (air), Pitta (fire), and Kapha (water/earth)</term>, from bites, or from weapons.<-

->In Virgo <note>original: "Stri"</note>, it is caused by women, diseases of the reproductive system <note>original: "guhya-ja"</note>, or from a fall.<-

->In Libra <note>original: "Tuladhara"</note>, death results from mental fever or delirium caused by a total humor imbalance <term>Sannipata|A critical state in Ayurveda where Vata, Pitta, and Kapha are all severely disturbed simultaneously</term>.<-

->In Scorpio <note>original: "Ali"</note>, death arises from diseases of the spleen, jaundice <note>original: "pandu"</note>, or chronic digestive disorders <term>Grahani|A disease of the small intestine or malabsorption</term>. || 21 ||<-

->In Sagittarius <note>original: "Haya"</note>, death is caused by trees, water, wood, or weapons.<-

->In Capricorn <note>original: "Mriga"</note>, it results from sharp internal pains, eye diseases, or mental confusion and delirium.<-

->In Aquarius <note>original: "Kumbha"</note>, it occurs due to cough <note>original: "kasa"</note>, fever, or consumption <term>Yakshma|A term for wasting diseases, often associated with tuberculosis</term>...<-

If the conditions are reversed, some authorities say the soul attains a downward path...<-

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

->The soul's upward path and its journey toward the heavenly light are determined by the specific divisions of the zodiac signs. || 22 ||<-

->Based on the lord of the twelfth house <term>Vyayadhipa|The planet ruling the house of expenditure, loss, and the final exit from the world</term> or the planets situated in the final portions of the signs, one should determine the soul's destination. The Sun or the Moon lead the soul to Kailasa <note>original: "Kailasam"; the sacred mountain abode of Lord Shiva</note>; Venus leads to Heaven <note>original: "Swarga"</note>; and Mars leads back to the Earthly realm. Mercury leads to Vaikuntha <note>original: "Vaikuntham"; the celestial abode of Lord Vishnu</note>; Saturn leads to the City of Death <note>original: "Yamapuram"; the realm of Yama, the god of justice and the afterlife</note>; and Jupiter leads to the sublime Realm of the Creator <note>original: "Brahmalokam"</note>. Rahu <term>Bhogivara|The serpent planet, often associated with worldly desires</term> leads the soul to various islands, while Ketu <term>Shikhi|The "tail" of the eclipse points, representing spiritual detachment or chaos</term> leads to the lower hells. || 23 ||<-

->The events of the previous birth <note>original: "Purva-janma"</note> are understood through the ruler of the ninth house <term>Dharmeshwara|The lord of the house of merit and destiny</term>. The nature of the future birth is determined by the ruler of the fifth house <term>Sutesha|The lord of the house of intellect and past-life credit</term>. The caste, the geographical direction, and the specific country associated with the lord of that house should be examined to reveal the details of that birth. || 24 ||<-

->If the lord of that house is in its own sign or its sign of exaltation, the soul reaches divine lands. If it is situated in a sign of debilitation <term>Neecha|The weakest planetary position</term> or in the sign of an enemy, the soul travels to a different island or continent. However, if the planet is in its own sign, a friendly sign, or a neutral sign, the soul will surely attain birth in the land of Bharatavarsha <note>original: "Bharatavarshameva"; the traditional Sanskrit name for the Indian subcontinent, regarded in these texts as the "land of karma" where spiritual progress is most possible</note>. || 25 ||<-

> For Jupiter, the place of birth is the Land of the Aryas <note>original: "Aryavartam"; historically the northern/central part of the Indian subcontinent where Vedic culture flourished</note>; for the Moon and Venus, it is near sacred rivers. For Mercury, the birth occurs in holy pilgrimage sites. For the Sun, it is in foreign lands <term>Mlechchhabhu|Territories outside the traditional Vedic cultural sphere</term>. Otherwise, the soul enters a living, sentient body. || 17 ||

> If the lord of the Ascendant <note>the planet ruling the sign rising on the eastern horizon at birth</note> is in its sign of exaltation, a friend's sign, or its own home, the soul attains a human birth <term>Manushyajanma|Human incarnation, considered the highest worldly form for spiritual progress</term>. In other circumstances, the soul may be born as animals or birds. These outcomes should also be carefully judged by the specific forms represented by the decanates <term>Drekkana|A ten-degree division of a zodiac sign, often depicted as specific figures or symbols</term>. || 18 ||

## Chapter Fifteen
### Analysis of the Houses
<note>original: "Bhava Chinta"; Bhava refers to the twelve "houses" or "fields" of a horoscope, each representing specific aspects of human life like wealth, siblings, or career.</note>

All houses <term>Bhāva|The twelve divisions of the zodiac in a birth chart</term> yield their full and auspicious results when they are joined by or aspected <note>viewed through an astrological "gaze"</note> by their own lords or by benefic <note>naturally positive</note> planets, provided they are free from the conjunction or gaze of malefic planets. Even if the lords of the houses are naturally malefic, they produce auspicious results in this context, so long as the planets are not in a state of debilitation <term>Nīca|The weakest zodiacal position for a planet</term>, combustion <term>Mūdha|Being so close to the Sun that the planet's influence is obscured</term>, or situated in an enemy’s sign. || 1 ||

When the trines <term>Trikoṇa|The 1st, 5th, and 9th houses, considered the most fortunate positions</term>, as well as the fourth, seventh, and tenth houses <note>original: "Svasukhamadanabhe cāspade"; respectively referring to the houses of happiness, desire, and profession</note> from a particular house are occupied by benefic planets, and that house is free from the gaze of malefics, joined by its own lord, and not associated with malefic planets, the wise say that house is nourished and brings all manner of auspiciousness. If the conditions are the opposite, the house faces destruction <note>original: "Pranāśam"; meaning the specific life area represented by that house will suffer or fail</note>. || 2 ||

<vocab>Chapter Fifteen, Analysis of the Houses, Trine, Auspicious Result, Total Auspiciousness, Malefic conjunction/aspect</vocab>

<summary>This page concludes the discussion on birth circumstances and introduces the general principles for analyzing astrological houses, stating that a house flourishes when supported by its lord and benefic planets in key positions.</summary>
<keywords>Bhava Chinta, houses, benefics, malefics, trines, Kendras, planetary strength, Vedic astrology</keywords>

--- Page 107 ---
<meta>This page continues the "Analysis of the Houses" (Bhava Chinta) from page 106. Having established the general conditions for a house's success, the text now details the specific planetary placements that lead to the "destruction" or failure of a house's potential. It specifically introduces the concept of the three "difficult houses" (6th, 8th, and 12th) which traditionally weaken any planet or house they influence.</meta>

<page-num>107</page-num>

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

...is mixed with friendly planets and great lords of the houses, as well as the lords of the physical body and other houses. || 2 ||

If the lord of a house <note>original: "Bhāvapo"</note> is situated in a house of destruction <note>referring to the 6th, 8th, or 12th houses</note>, or if the significators of the body are weak, or if that lord occupies a sign of debilitation or an enemy’s sign and is neither joined with nor aspected <note>viewed by an astrological "gaze"</note> by benefic <note>naturally positive</note> planets, it brings about the destruction of that house. Similarly, if there is another planet in such a house, it also fails to yield auspicious results; if a cruel planet <term>Ugra-graha|A "fierce" or malefic planet like Mars or Saturn</term> is present, it leads to total ruin. || 3 ||

Malefic planets situated in the sixth, eighth, or twelfth houses from the Ascendant <term>Lagna|The rising sign or first house, representing the self</term> cause the destruction of those respective houses. Even benefic planets in these specific positions do not provide their full positive results. This is what the learned say regarding the influence of the house lords. || 4 ||

When the lord of a house is located in the sixth, eighth, or twelfth houses <note>collectively known as "Dusthanas" or difficult houses</note>, or if the lord of one of these difficult houses occupies that specific house, the experts declare the destruction of that house’s prospects. However, if that house is aspected by a benefic planet, then happiness and well-being for that house will manifest instead. || 5 ||

When the lord of the house, the house itself, and the planet known as the significator <term>Kāraka|A planet that acts as the primary indicator for a specific area of life, such as Venus for marriage</term> are all devoid of strength, and when malefic planets occupy that house or if it is aspected by malefics and enemies without the gaze of other [benefic] planets;

And when malefic planets are situated in the houses of death, loss, or the trines <note>the 8th, 12th, or the 1st, 5th, and 9th houses</note> from that house—

<summary>This page outlines the astrological conditions that lead to the failure or "destruction" of specific life areas, focusing on the negative impact of the 6th, 8th, and 12th houses. It emphasizes that a house's strength depends on a "triple check" of the house itself, its ruler, and its natural significator.</summary>

<keywords>Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao, Jyotisha, Bhava, Graha, Lagna, Karaka, Phala, Dusthana, malefic planets, benefic planets, house destruction</keywords>

<page-num>108</page-num>

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

...the loss of that house clearly occurs here when two or three such negative factors coincide. || 6 ||

---

The lords of the respective houses, the lords of the 6th, 8th, or 12th houses, and the lords of the Decanates <term>Drekkana|A ten-degree subdivision of a zodiac sign, used to fine-tune the analysis of a planet's strength</term> are considered weak if they occupy the 6th, 8th, or 12th houses from the house being analyzed. During their planetary periods <term>Dasha|A specific span of time in a person's life governed by a particular planet</term>, they cause the destruction of that house’s potential. However, malefic planets in the 3rd, 6th, or 11th houses from a house, and any planets in the Angles or Trines <note>The "Angles" are the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses; "Trines" are the 1st, 5th, and 9th houses. These are the most supportive areas of a chart</note> are considered auspicious. Indeed, planets that are friends of a house's lord will ensure the fulfillment of that house's purpose. || 7 ||

The lords of the Moon sign and the Ascendant, those who aspect <note>the "gaze" or mathematical influence one planet casts upon another</note> them, Saturn, the lord of the 22nd Decanate, and Gulika <note>original: "Gulikapa"—referring to a mathematical point in the sky considered the "son of Saturn" and usually seen as a source of difficulty</note>—if the lords of the signs or divisions occupied by these are very weak at birth, or if they are in unfavorable positions, or if Rahu <note>the North Node of the Moon, often associated with sudden or chaotic energy</note> is joined with or aspected by malefic planets, then during its period, it will bring about the destruction of the house. || 8 ||

Good results accrue to a house when its lord is joined with the lord of the Ascendant <term>Lagna-lord|The planet that rules the first house, representing the person themselves</term>. One should judge the fruits of a house based on where the lord of the Ascendant is situated or which planets it is joined with. If they are located in a difficult house <term>Dusthana|Specifically the 6th, 8th, or 12th houses, which represent obstacles, illness, or loss</term>, the opposite results are declared. If the lord of the house is weak, the defect is severe; if the lord is endowed with strength, the defect is said to be minimal. || 9 ||

<summary>This page details the specific astrological conditions, such as weak lords and the influence of Rahu or Saturn, that lead to the failure of a house's goals, while emphasizing that the strength of the Ascendant lord can reduce these negative effects.</summary>

<page-num>109</page-num>

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

Even if the lord of a house is naturally malefic <note>original Sanskrit: "ashubha"—referring to planets like Mars or Saturn that are traditionally considered harsh or inauspicious</note>, if he is also the lord of the Ascendant <note>original: "udaya-pati"—the ruler of the 1st house, representing the self and vitality</note>, he will grant growth and prosperity to that house. This holds true as long as the lord of a difficult house <note>original: "dusthana"—the 6th, 8th, or 12th houses associated with challenges</note> does not possess greater strength than the Ascendant lord. An example of this is when Mars is placed in the 5th house in the sign of Leo: if it is aspected by benefic planets, the wise say that the birth of children will certainly occur. || 10 ||

If a planet has the lordship of two houses, the results of the house that falls in a Trine <term>Trikona|The 1st, 5th, and 9th houses, which are considered the most auspicious and supportive in a birth chart</term> are primary. Half of the results will come from the planet's own sign. Some say that during the first half of the planet's period, one should predict a mixture of both house results. In the second half of the period, if the planet is in an even sign, the results of the even house manifest; if it is in an odd sign, some authorities declare that the results of the odd-sign house will prevail. || 11 ||

Whichever house lord is mentioned first, or whichever planet is placed in a sign with zero points <term>Bindu|A 'dot' or unit of power in the Ashtakavarga system; a sign with zero Bindus for a planet indicates a complete lack of strength there</term>—during its planetary period <note>original: "paka"—the ripening or period of a planet’s influence</note>, it will cause the destruction of the physical body and the significations of the various houses, especially if it occupies a malefic position. || 12 ||

<page-num>110</page-num>

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

When a planet <term>Graha|The celestial bodies used in Vedic astrology, including the Sun, Moon, and the five visible planets</term> is positioned in its sign of exaltation <note>original Sanskrit: "Svocche"—the zodiac sign where a planet is at its maximum strength</note> or in the sign of a friendly planet, and even if it possesses significant strength through the six-fold divisions <term>Shadvarga|A system of six divisional charts used to calculate the refined strength of a planet</term>, it will only provide auspicious results if it is situated in a favorable house <note>original: "Sanmau"—referring to the auspicious 1st, 4th, 7th, 10th, 5th, or 9th houses</note>. This must be carefully considered when predicting the outcomes during that planet's active period. || 13 ||

A planet grants its full results only when its mathematical position in degrees and minutes exactly matches the degree of the house center <term>Bhava-madhya|The most sensitive point or "cusp" of an astrological house where its influence is strongest</term>. If the planet is situated at the junction between two houses <note>original: "sandhi"—the 'joint' or border where one house ends and another begins</note>, it yields no result. For the space in between the house center and the junction, a proportional calculation <note>original: "anupātaḥ"—a rule-of-three calculation to find the partial strength</note> should indeed be made for the planets. || 14 ||

One should judge the following matters from the **Sun**: the Self (soul), father, influence, power, and wealth. From the **Moon**: the mind, intellect, the favor of kings, the mother, and the acquisition of prosperity. From **Mars**: courage, diseases, qualities, younger siblings, enemies, and the earth. From **Mercury**: education, relatives, discrimination, the maternal uncle, friends, speech, and one's actions. || 15 ||

From **Jupiter** <note>original: "Vāgīśvara"—meaning 'Lord of Speech,' a traditional epithet for Jupiter</note>: wisdom, wealth, physical health and vitality, children, and elder siblings.

One should determine matters regarding the wife, vehicles, ornaments, sexual affairs, and general comforts from **Venus** <note>original Sanskrit: "Bhrigu"</note>. Longevity, livelihood, the cause of death, calamities, and historical background should be declared from **Saturn** <note>original: "Manda"</note>. One should consider the paternal grandfather from the **Sun** <note>original: "Sarvajna"</note> and the maternal grandfather from **Ketu** <note>original: "Shikhi"—though Ketu often represents the maternal grandfather, some traditions use Rahu; here the text specifies Shikhi (Ketu)</note>. || 19 ||

The natural significators <term>Karakas|Planets that act as the 'departmental heads' for specific areas of life, regardless of which house they rule in a specific chart</term> for the twelve houses, starting from the **Ascendant** <note>original: "Vialgna"</note>, are as follows:

1. **First House (Self):** The Sun <note>original: "Dyumani"</note>
2. **Second House (Wealth):** Jupiter <note>original: "Mantri"</note>
3. **Third House (Siblings):** Mars <note>original: "Bhusuta"</note>
4. **Fourth House (Mother/Home):** The Moon and Mercury <note>original: "Soma-Saumya"</note>
5. **Fifth House (Children):** Jupiter <note>original: "Guru"</note>
6. **Sixth House (Enemies/Debt):** Mars and Saturn <note>original: "Inatanayau"</note>
7. **Seventh House (Spouse):** Venus <note>original: "Bhargava"</note>
8. **Eighth House (Death):** Saturn <note>original: "Bhanuputra"</note>
9. **Ninth House (Fortune):** The Sun and Jupiter <note>original: "Dinakara-Dwijezhau"</note>
10. **Tenth House (Profession):** Jupiter, the Sun, Mercury, and Saturn <note>original: "Jiva-Bhanu-Jna-Mandah"</note>
11. **Eleventh House (Gains):** Jupiter <note>original: "Suraguru"</note>
12. **Twelfth House (Loss):** Saturn <note>original: "Ina-sunu"</note> || 20 ||

The results of planets situated in a friend’s sign, an enemy’s sign, a neutral sign, or their own sign must be weighed alongside the significations of the houses, such as the body <note>the 1st house</note> and others. Sage Satyacharya states that auspicious planets <term>Saumya|Benefic planets like Jupiter, Venus, or a strong Moon</term> in the houses of growth <term>Upachaya|The 3rd, 6th, 10th, and 11th houses, where planets improve over time</term> provide prosperity, while inauspicious planets <term>Papa|Malefic planets like Mars or Saturn</term> cause distress; however, this is said to be reversed when they are placed in the "evil" houses <note>original: "Rihpha-Shashta-Ashtame"—referring to the 12th, 6th, and 8th houses respectively</note>. || 21 ||

Malefic planets situated in the sixth, eighth, and twelfth houses...

> ->His brother and his qualities from the third [house]<-

<note>This passage describes the principle of **Bhavat Bhavam** (literally "from house to house"). It instructs the astrologer to treat any planet or house as a temporary "first house." For example, to understand a mother's health, one would look at the 6th house (disease) relative to the 4th house (mother). The text systematically lists the significations of the 3rd through 12th houses as applied to any starting point.</note>

<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of planetary influences on the twelve houses of a horoscope. <page-num>114</page-num>

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

->When that house, its significator, and the lord of the house<-

->are all endowed with strength, there is happiness for that house. || 25 ||<-

<note>original: "tasmin bhāve kārake bhāvanāthe vīryopete tasya bhāvasya saukhyam" - This is a fundamental rule in Vedic astrology: for a life area (like wealth or health) to be successful, the house itself, the planet that naturally represents that area (the Karaka), and the planet that rules that specific house must all be strong.</note>

->The Sun in the ninth, the Moon in the seventh house,<-

->Mars in the third, Jupiter in the fifth,<-

->Venus in the seventh, and Saturn in the eighth—<-

->others say these placements cause distress to those houses. || 26 ||<-

<note>This verse describes the concept of *Karako Bhava Nashaya*, or "the significator destroys the house." It is a paradoxical rule where a planet usually considered "good" for a certain topic might actually cause problems if it is placed directly in the house it signifies. For example, Jupiter signifies children; some traditions believe Jupiter placed in the 5th house (the house of children) can actually create obstacles regarding them.</note>

->The Lord of the Ascendant gives the results of the house<-

->in which it is placed, or the lord of the house it is joined with. |<-

->If that house lord is powerful, then<-

->one finds happiness from that house; if weak, there is misfortune. || 27 ||<-

<note>The "Lord of the Ascendant" (<term>Lagnesha|The planet ruling the first house, representing the self</term>) is the most important planet for an individual. Its position shows where the person's energy is directed. If it sits with a "strong" house lord, that area of life (e.g., career, marriage) thrives.</note>

->The Lord of the Ascendant, joined with a strong house lord or placed prominently,<-

->expands the experience of that house and the house where it resides. |<-

->But joined with a weak house lord, it yields the results of those in decline—<-

<note>The text cuts off here, but it is explaining how the "strength" of a planet is transferred to the person's overall life path. If the planet representing "You" (the Ascendant Lord) is tied to a planet that is "in decline" or "weak" (nimna), the specific areas of life governed by those planets will suffer.</note>

<term>Bhava|An astrological 'house' or department of life</term>
<term>Karaka|A significator; a planet that naturally represents a specific person or thing (e.g., the Sun for the father)</term>
<term>Bhanuputra|A name for Saturn, literally "Son of the Sun"</term>
<term>Devamantri|A name for Jupiter, literally "The Advisor to the Gods"</term>

<summary>This page outlines the criteria for a "successful" house in a horoscope, emphasizing that the house, its lord, and its significator must all be strong. It also lists specific planetary placements that traditionally cause "distress" despite the planets' usual nature.</summary>
<keywords>Vedic Astrology, Bhava, Karaka, Lagnesha, planetary strength, Karako Bhava Nashaya, Sun, Moon, Mars, Jupiter, Venus, Saturn</keywords>

->One should apply the opposite of these rules to all the other houses as well. || 28 ||<-

<note>original: "kuryāt-tad-viparītam-evam-uditaṃ" — This verse instructs the astrologer that if the previous verses described what makes a house "strong" or "happy," the opposite conditions (weakness or affliction) will naturally result in "distress" or failure for that area of life.</note>

->If the lord of a <term>Dusthana|A "difficult house," specifically the 6th, 8th, or 12th houses associated with challenges</term> is placed in its other own house,<-

->it yields the results of that own house only, and nothing else.<-

->If Saturn is in Capricorn, which is the fifth house, children are granted;<-

->the flaw of ruling the sixth house does not apply here. || 29 ||<-

<note>This is a significant rule in Vedic astrology regarding planets that rule two different signs. Saturn, for example, rules both Capricorn and Aquarius. If one of those is a "bad" house (the 6th) and the other is a "good" house (the 5th), and Saturn sits in the 5th, the text argues that the negative influence of the 6th house is neutralized.</note>

->Occupancy of a sign, mutual conjunction, and the aspect of planets;<-

->placement in an angular house or a trine;<-

->these are the five types of "connections" described. || 30 ||<-

<note>original: "pañca-prakāro bandha" — This verse defines how planets "bind" or relate to one another. In astrology, planets don't act in isolation; they influence each other through their position, their gaze (aspect), or by sharing a house.</note>

# Chapter Sixteen

## Results of the Twelve Houses

->The physical body resembles the Lord of the <term>Lagna|The Ascendant or the first house, representing the self</term> or the Lord of the <term>Navamsha|A sub-chart divided into nine parts, used to see the hidden strength of planets</term>,<-

->or it may resemble the strongest planet in the chart.<-

->The complexion follows the Lord of the Navamsha occupied by the Moon,<-

->and the body parts are divided according to the signs starting with the Ascendant. || 1 ||<-

<note>This begins the section on "Physiognomy." Ancient astrologers believed the strongest planet at the moment of birth determined a person's physical stature, build, and skin tone. The "signs starting with the Ascendant" refers to the *Kalapurusha* concept, where Aries or the 1st house represents the head, the 2nd the face, and so on down to the feet.</note>

->When the Lord of the Ascendant is in an angle or a trine, in its sign of exaltation, or its own sign...<-

<note>This verse is incomplete in the manuscript. It typically continues to explain that such a placement ensures a long life, good health, and a respectable status in society.</note>

It then begins Chapter 16, detailing how the physical appearance and complexion of a person are determined by the strongest planets and the Navamsha chart.</summary>

->If the lord of the <term>house of death|The 8th house, which governs longevity and the nature of death</term> is placed in the ascendant and is joined by a benefic planet, the person will be long-lived, wealthy, and endowed with noble virtues. They will be praised by rulers, possessed of fortune, and have eyes as beautiful as lotuses. Such an individual will have a strong physique, be fearless, righteous, and blessed with a good family. || 2 ||<-

<note>original: "khēṭrādanmṛtasaṃsthē nidhanabhavanapē saumyayuktē vilagnē" — It is a traditional astrological paradox that the lord of the 8th house (death), when placed in the 1st house (the body) with a benefic planet, grants long life rather than health issues.</note>

->When the lord of the body (the ascendant lord) is connected with benefic planets, or resides in a benefic sign, or is joined by a powerful planet, the person will find shelter under a famous king. If this lord is in its sign of exaltation, the person becomes a ruler themselves. If it is in its own house, they will remain in their birthplace. If it is placed in a <term>movable sign|Signs like Aries, Cancer, Libra, and Capricorn that represent motion and change</term>, they will be a wanderer; in a <term>fixed sign|Signs like Taurus, Leo, Scorpio, and Aquarius that represent stability</term>, they stay in one place; and in a <term>dual sign|Signs like Gemini, Virgo, Sagittarius, and Pisces that possess a double nature</term>, they experience both traveling and staying. || 3 ||<-

->The individual becomes famous if the lord of the body is brilliant with rays (not combust) and well-placed; they will be happy and prosperous. If that lord is poorly placed (in the 6th, 8th, or 12th houses), or situated in an enemy’s sign or its sign of debilitation, the person will live in a lowly or miserable place. If the lord is well-situated and powerful in the rising sign, the person is vigorous and thrives. But if it is powerless, they are struck down, repeatedly face calamities, and become broken and sickly. || 4 ||<-

<note>original: "kiraṇōjjvalē tanupatau" — This refers to a planet that is not "combust" or too close to the Sun, allowing its symbolic "light" to reach the person.</note>

->When the lord of wealth occupies a significant house, and is endowed with the prosperity of a family...<-

<summary>This page describes how the strength and placement of the planet ruling the Ascendant determine a person's vitality, social standing, and whether they will travel or stay in their birthplace. It emphasizes that a "bright" and well-placed ruler leads to fame and health, while a weak ruler leads to misfortune.</summary>

<page-num>117</page-num>

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

A person will possess the most excellent qualities, be wealthy, have a handsome face, and be far-sighted. If the lord of the <term>second house|The house governing wealth, speech, and the face</term> is well-disposed and strong, the individual is capable of doing good for the world. They shall acquire knowledge and wealth, though it may come slowly; however, if the house is influenced by lesser planets, they may be occupied with trivial or base branches of knowledge. || 5 ||

<note>original: "samyajñe saviturdvitīyapatinā" — This refers to the lord of the second house from the Sun, a specific calculation used to determine the source of one's prosperity and the clarity of their intellect.</note>

If Jupiter is placed in the second house, the person becomes an expert in <term>Vedic Dharma Shastra|Ancient Indian scriptures and legal codes</term>. If Mercury is there, they are skilled in philosophical systems. Under the influence of Venus, they are clever in romantic speech and a knower of some arts. With Mars, they are skilled in "cruel arts" <note>This likely refers to weaponry, surgery, or aggressive tactics</note> and may be a slanderer. If Rahu is present, the person is <note>original: "lohalaḥ" — interpreted as either one who works with iron/metals or one who is greedy and unsteady</note>. If Ketu is present, their speech may be faltering or deceptive. Under the influence of other malefic planets, one becomes foolish and poor. || 6 ||

If the lord of the body (the 1st house) and the lord of valor (the 3rd house) are both strong and have exchanged their signs <note>This "exchange" (Parivartana) is considered a powerful yoga or planetary combination in Indian astrology</note>, the person attains great courage, bravery, and the support of their siblings. Such an individual is known for performing daring and adventurous deeds. || 7 ||

When the lord of valor (the 3rd house lord) is strong and joined by benefic planets, and the <term>Karaka|The 'significator' or planet that naturally represents a topic; for siblings, this is Mars</term> is also placed in a favorable house, there will be an increase in the number and prosperity of one's brothers. However, if these indicators are weak or placed in <term>difficult houses|The 6th, 8th, or 12th houses, which represent obstacles and loss</term>, it results in the loss or destruction of siblings. || 8 ||

<summary>This page details the astrological effects of planets in the second and third houses, specifically addressing wealth, speech, intellectual pursuits, courage, and the fate of siblings.</summary>
<keywords>Jyotisha, second house, third house, Vedic Dharma Shastra, Rahu, Ketu, siblings, planetary exchange, astrological yoga</keywords>

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

If the two <term>significator lords|The planets responsible for representing a specific relative or life area</term> are in odd-numbered signs and are aspected by Jupiter, the Sun, and Mars; and if the <term>third house|The house of valor and siblings</term>—also called the house of prowess—is an odd-numbered sign, then the person will have male siblings according to the strength of those lineages. || 9 ||

<note>original: "ayugmarāśā yadi kārakeśau" — An odd sign (Aries, Gemini, etc.) in Vedic astrology is considered masculine in nature, which is why it is used here to predict male siblings.</note>

If the <term>lord of the fourth house|The house of happiness and the mother</term> and the Moon are both placed in a <term>difficult house|The 6th, 8th, or 12th houses, known as Dusthanas</term>, and are devoid of any aspect or conjunction from benefic planets, or if they are trapped between malefic planets or joined with them, it indicates the death of the mother. If both of these (the lord of the 4th and the Moon) are strong and aspected by benefic planets, they bring happiness to the mother. If there are benefic planets in the 4th house from the Moon, one should predict happiness for her. || 10 ||

<note>original: "pāpāntaḥsthitimatyasadgrahayute" — This refers to a condition called 'Papa Kartari Yoga', where a planet is "hemmed in" by malefic planets on either side, effectively weakening it.</note>

If the lord of the ascendant is in the 4th house, or if the lord of the 4th house is in the ascendant, or if they aspect each other, or if there is a similar connection with the Moon, the person will perform the <term>final rites|The traditional Hindu funeral ceremonies (Antyesti)</term> for their mother. However, if these two lords are in each other's enemy signs or signs of debilitation, or are in a six-eight relationship <note>original: "ṣaṣṭhāṣṭame" — This refers to the Shashtashtaka position, a challenging 6/8 house relationship between planets</note> to one another, the person may not be able to be of service to the mother at the time of her passing, unless there is a specific planetary bond between them. || 11 ||
