# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

Just as the condition of the mother was described through the fourth house, one should likewise predict the fate of the father, brothers, children, and other relatives. This is done by analyzing the <term>House|Bhava</term>, the <term>Significator|Karaka - the planet naturally representing that relative</term>, the <term>Lord of the House|Bhavesha</term>, the <term>Ascendant|Lagna</term>, and the <term>Lord of the Ascendant|Lagnaswara</term>. || 12 ||

<note>original: "mātṛbhāvoktadvadvācyam..." — The author is establishing a universal rule: to know about any relative, look at the house associated with them (e.g., 9th for father), the planet representing them (Sun for father), and the lords of those positions.</note>

If the <term>Lord of the Fourth House|Sukhesha</term> and <term>Venus|Bhriguja</term> are well-placed, possess the strength of the Ascendant, and are joined together, they grant the individual a <term>palanquin|Andolika - a traditional swinging carriage carried by men, symbolizing high status</term> and lordship over people. Such a person will possess golden banners, jewels, precious gems, ornaments, silken beds, various means of sensory enjoyment, as well as cattle, elephants, and horses. || 13 ||

<note>original: "vāndolikāṃ janapateśvaratāṃ vidhattaḥ" — This verse lists the classical markers of wealth in ancient India. While we might think of cars or luxury estates today, the Sanskrit text focuses on livestock and royal insignia like golden banners.</note>

However, if the Lord of the Fourth House is placed in a <term>difficult house|Dusthana - the 6th, 8th, or 12th houses</term>, or if <term>Mars, the Sun, and Saturn|Kuja-Surya-Arka</term> are placed in the fourth house, the individual's ancestral home may be destroyed by fire. If the house is associated with <term>the shadow planets|Tamas - Rahu or Ketu</term> or with the lords of enemy houses, the home will be dilapidated, and one's cattle, lands, and vehicles will be taken over by others. || 14 ||

<note>original: "janmagṛhaṃ pradagdham" — Literally "the birth-house is burnt." In a historical context, this often referred to the literal burning of thatched or wooden structures, but it can also metaphorically mean the loss of the family estate.</note>

If the <term>fifth house|The house of intelligence and children</term> or its lord is located in a sign or division ruled by a benefic planet, and is joined by benefics, or if it attains a <term>superior planetary division|Vaiseshika-amsha - a technical state where a planet is strong in many of the sixteen divisional charts</term> while maintaining a good nature, the individual will be highly intelligent and free from deceit. || 15 ||

<note>original: "dhīmān niṣkapaṭī bhavet" — A "dhīmān" is someone with refined intellect, while "niṣkapaṭī" describes a person who is straightforward and lacks the "cloak" of hidden agendas.</note>

If malefic planets are situated there, or if the Lord of the Second House is joined with a strong Lord of the House of Enemies...

If the <term>Lord of the Body|Tanupati - the ruler of the 1st house or Ascendant</term> is joined with, aspected by, or located in the house of the enemy (the 6th house), or if the <term>Lord of Enemies|Arisha - the ruler of the 6th house</term> is placed in a <term>Quadrant|Kendra - the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses</term> and is joined or aspected by malefic planets, it causes sudden and unavoidable suffering and distress from enemies. || 16 ||

<note>original: "ripugṛhe vā tanupatiḥ" — The "Lord of the Body" refers to the planet ruling the sign rising on the eastern horizon at birth. When this planet is "afflicted" by the house of enemies, the text suggests the person's physical presence or identity is constantly challenged by rivals.</note>

When the <term>Lord of the Ascendant|Lagnadipati</term> is more powerful than the <term>Lord of the Sixth House|Shashteshvara</term>, is situated in a benefic <term>sub-division|Amsha</term>, and is joined with or aspected by benefic planets; and if the <term>Lord of Happiness|Sukhesha - the ruler of the 4th house</term> is also strong and placed in a Quadrant or <term>Trine|Kona - the 1st, 5th, or 9th houses</term>, the individual will be blessed with health, prosperity, and a strong, sturdy physique. || 17 ||

If the Lord of Enemies is placed in a <term>difficult position|Dusthana - the 6th, 8th, or 12th houses</term>, or is weakened by being in its sign of <term>debilitation|Neecha</term>, <term>combustion|Mudha - being too close to the Sun</term>, or an enemy's sign—and if the Lord of the Ascendant is stronger than it—one will see the <term>destruction of enemies|Shatrunasham</term>. This is especially true if the Sun is favorably placed. || 18 ||

Whichever <term>house|Bhava</term> the Lord of Enemies is joined with or situated in, or whichever Lord of a house is situated in the 6th house, those specific areas of life will turn into sources of enmity. || 19 ||

<note>This is a foundational principle in Vedic astrology: the 6th house "infects" whatever it touches. For example, if the Lord of the 6th is with the Lord of the 2nd (wealth), one's finances might be a source of constant legal or personal conflict.</note>

If the seventh house is associated with benefics and its lord is endowed with strength... 

<note>The text transitions here to the analysis of the seventh house, which traditionally governs marriage and partnerships.</note>

<note>original: "patiputravatī sadhvī bhāryā" — This verse likely concludes a section on the 7th house (marriage) or 4th house (domestic bliss) from the preceding page.</note>

When the <term>Lord of the Eighth House|Randhresha - the ruler of the house of death and transformation</term> is placed anywhere other than a <term>Quadrant|Kendra - the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses</term>, and is weaker than the <term>Lord of the Ascendant|Lagnasha</term>, men will experience no mental agony, no obstacles, and no suffering; their life shall be long. || 21 ||

<note>original: "pāpamadhyagate" — This refers to a condition called 'Papa Kartari Yoga', where a house or planet is "hemmed in" by cruel planets on either side, effectively choking its positive influence.</note>

If the birth occurs during the day and the Sun is well-placed, or if it occurs at night and <term>Saturn|Manda</term> is well-placed and aspected by benefic planets—and if the Lord of the Ninth House is endowed with strength—then that person's father will live a long life. || 23 ||

If Saturn, Mars, the <term>Moon|Shitaruchi - literally 'the one with cool rays'</term>, and the Sun are all situated in <term>Trines|Trikona - the 1st, 5th, or 9th houses</term>, the child may be abandoned by both mother and father. However, if they are aspected by <term>Jupiter|Chakra-purohita - literally 'the Priest of the Discus-bearer' or the Priest of the Gods</term>, the child will instead be happy and live a long life. || 24 ||

<note>original: "tanūjo 'sti" — The '౨' symbol in the transcription is the Telugu representation of the Sanskrit avagraha, indicating the 'a' in 'asti' is elided after 'tanūjo'.</note>

If Saturn is the Lord of the Ascendant and is in its own or exalted sign, situated in a <term>Movable Sign|Chara-rashi - Aries, Cancer, Libra, or Capricorn</term>, and is not aspected by any benefic planets, and if the Sun is in a difficult house, the person will depend upon another father <gloss>such as a step-father or foster father</gloss>. || 25 ||

If the Ninth House or its Lord is joined with or aspected by Saturn in a Movable Sign, and the <term>Lord of the Twelfth House|Vyayesha - the house of loss and liberation</term> is powerful, the person born will certainly be an adopted child. || 26 ||

If a <term>benefic planet|Shubha-khage - literally "well-moving sky-goer"</term> is situated in the <term>Tenth House|Nabhasi - literally "in the sky"</term>, or in a <term>Quadrant or Trine|Kendra or Kona</term>, and is strong by being in its own sign or its sign of exaltation—or if the <term>Lord of the Ascendant|Lagnapa</term> is so placed—then the person will be endowed with great and wide-reaching fame, and be devoted to <term>righteousness and duty|Dharma and Karma</term>. When the <term>Lord of the Ascendant|Lagnapa</term> reaches the <term>Trine|Kona - the 1st, 5th, or 9th positions</term> of the sign or <term>Navamsha|Amsha - a 1/9th divisional chart</term> occupied by the <term>Lord of a House|Bhavesha</term>, or when the Lord of a House reaches the Trine of the sign or Navamsha occupied by the Lord of the Ascendant, the fulfillment of that house’s matters should be predicted. This occurs through their conjunction or mutual aspect, and by considering the <term>Significator|Karaka</term> relative to both the Ascendant and the <term>Moon|Chandra</term>. || 30 ||

<note>original: "bhāva-siddhiṃ" — This refers to the "perfection" or "manifestation" of the results promised by a specific astrological house, such as marriage for the 7th house or wealth for the 2nd.</note>

When <term>Jupiter|Guru</term>, during its <term>Transit|Gochara - the current movement of planets across the sky</term>, passes through the Trine of the sign where the Lord of a House is situated in the birth chart, one should predict the attainment of results for that specific house. || 31 ||

<note>Jupiter is considered the great benefic; its transit over key points in the horoscope is traditionally believed to "trigger" or bless the potential of those houses.</note>

In the event of a combination between the Lord of the Ascendant and the <term>Lord of the Sixth House|Ari-natha - the ruler of enemies, debts, and diseases</term>, if the enemy (the 6th Lord) is weaker than the Lord of the Ascendant, then the enemy will come under the native’s control. If the opposite is true, the results are reversed. || 33 ||

<note>Astrological strength (Bala) determines the winner in conflicts. If the person's own "self" (1st House) is stronger than the "opposition" (6th House), victory is predicted.</note>

If the Lord of a specific house and the <term>Lord of the Body|Tanupa - the Ascendant Lord</term> are mutual enemies, or if one is placed in the <term>House of Enemies|Ari - the 6th house</term> or the <term>House of Death|Mriti - the 8th house</term> relative to the other, one should predict rivalry and competition. Conversely, friendship and cooperation should be predicted based on their conjunction or mutual friendliness. || 34 ||

<note>original: "spardhāṃ" — Rivalry or competition. This verse explains how to determine if the people or themes represented by a house will be helpful or harmful to the individual.</note>

When there is a conjunction between the Lord of the Ascendant and the Lord of whichever house...

<note>The text cuts off here, beginning a new rule regarding the conjunction of the primary house (Self) and other thematic houses.</note>

### Chapter Seventeen
### Section on Death

<note>Sanskrit: "Niryāṇa-prakaraṇa" — This section deals with the most sensitive part of ancient astrology: predicting the end of life or the "destruction" of specific areas of life (houses).</note>

When <term>Saturn|Manda</term> transits the <term>Trine|Trikona - the 1st, 5th, or 9th positions</term> of the sign occupied in the <term>Navamsha|Amsha - a 1/9th divisional chart</term> by the <term>Lord of the Eighth House|Ashtamesha</term> relative to any specific house, or the sign occupied in the Navamsha by the <term>Lord of the Twelfth|Vyayesha</term>, the destruction of that house occurs. || 1 ||

<note>This verse explains how to predict the "death" of a specific life aspect. For example, if calculating for the 4th house (mother), one would look at the 8th house from the 4th.</note>

In whichever sign the Lord of the Eighth House, <term>Gulika|A mathematical point in the chart considered the "son" of Saturn and a harbinger of suffering</term>, Saturn, or even the <term>Lord of the Khara Drekkana|The ruler of the 22nd decanate (10-degree division), traditionally used to predict the nature of death</term> is placed: when <term>Saturn|Ravija - literally "son of the Sun"</term> transits the trine of that Navamsha sign, death occurs. || 2 ||

Death occurs when <term>Jupiter|Guru</term> transits the trine of the Navamsha sign occupied by the <term>Lord of the Ascendant|Udyad-guna-natha</term>, the Lord of the Eighth House, or the Lord of the Eighth Decanate. || 3 ||

<note>While Saturn often indicates the timing of death through hardship, Jupiter’s transit is sometimes interpreted as the soul's release or the final "blessing" of departure.</note>

Either in its own <term>Dwadashamsha|A 1/12th divisional chart</term> position, or in the Navamsha occupied by the Lord of the Eighth House...

<vocab>వేమూరి దుర్గా నాగేశ్వర రావుగారి స్మృతి గ్రంథావళి (Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series), సప్తదశోఽధ్యాయః (Seventeenth Chapter), నిర్యాణప్రకరణ (Section on Death), జ్యోతిష్యం (Astrology), శ్లోకములు (Verses), రన్ధ్రేశే (Lord of the 8th House), భావేశవీర్యే (Strength of the House Lord)</vocab>

It provides specific mathematical formulas involving Saturn and Jupiter transits through divisional charts like the Navamsha to predict the end of life or the loss of specific house-related benefits.</summary>

<page-num>125</page-num>

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

Death occurs when <term>Jupiter|Guru</term> or <term>Saturn|Shani</term> transits the <term>Navamsha|Amsha - the 1/9th divisional chart</term> occupied by the <term>Lord of the Ascendant|Lagnesha</term>, or its <term>Trines|Trikona - the 1st, 5th, or 9th positions from a sign</term>. || 4 ||

One should declare the time of death when the <term>Moon|Vidhu</term> reaches the trine of the sign or Navamsha belonging to the Lord of the Eighth House or the <term>Sun|Bhanu</term>. All these factors must be considered from the perspective of both the <term>Ascendant|Lagna</term> and the natal Moon. || 5 ||

<note>This verse emphasizes the "Sudarshana" principle—checking calculations from multiple starting points (Ascendant and Moon) to ensure accuracy.</note>

When Jupiter reaches the trine of the sign or Navamsha calculated by subtracting the longitude of the Lord of the Ascendant from <term>Yamakantaka|A sensitive mathematical point in the chart, considered the "son" of Jupiter and often associated with timing death</term>, or when it reaches the Navamsha of the Moon subtracted from Saturn, the person's own death occurs. Furthermore, when Jupiter transits the trine of the Navamsha resulting from <term>Mars|Bhusunu - "Son of the Earth"</term> minus <term>Rahu|The North Node of the Moon</term>, it signifies the destruction of siblings. || 6 ||

<note>The word "hina" (subtracted) refers to a specific astrological calculation where the longitude of one point is subtracted from another to find a sensitive "death-inflicting" degree.</note>

When Jupiter transits the trine or sign-Navamsha of the Sun minus Yamakantaka, the father perishes. When Saturn transits the trine of the Navamsha of <term>Gulika|A sensitive point considered the "son" of Saturn</term> minus Yamakantaka, the same may occur. If Jupiter transits the trine of the sign-Navamsha of the Sun minus the Moon, or if Saturn transits the trine of the sign-Navamsha of <term>Gulika|Mandatmaja - literally "born of the slow-moving one"</term> minus the Moon, I consider this to be the time of the mother's death. || 7 ||

One should subtract Yamakantaka from the <term>Lord of the Fifth Star|Pratya-nakshatra-natha - the ruler of the fifth star in the sequence of 27 from one’s birth star</term>. When Jupiter transits that resulting sign or its trines, it indicates the destruction of the son. || 8 ||

<summary>This page details specific mathematical calculations (sphutas) involving subtracting planetary longitudes to determine the timing of death for the self, siblings, parents, and children.</summary>

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

If the lord of the sign resulting from the combined longitudes of the <term>Ascendant|Lagna</term>, the <term>Sun|Arka</term>, and <term>Mandi|A sensitive mathematical point in the chart, also known as Gulika, representing the "son" of Saturn and often associated with karmic obstructions</term> is placed in the setting house <note>original: "yavanopagastu," referring to the western horizon or 7th house</note>, the person meets their end when <term>Saturn|Arkasuta - "Son of the Sun"</term> transits that specific sign or its <term>Trine|Trikona</term>. || 9 ||

Subtract the longitude of Saturn from the longitude of Mandi. Death may occur when Saturn transits the resulting sign, its <term>Navamsha|The 1/9th divisional chart</term>, or its trine. Furthermore, death is indicated when Saturn enters the <term>Decan|Drekkana - a 10-degree division of a zodiac sign</term> identified by the sum of the <term>Upagrahas|Shadow planets or mathematical sensitive points</term> beginning with <term>Dhuma|'Smoke'—the first of the five non-luminous points calculated from the Sun's position</term>. || 10 ||

Those learned in the science of the stars declare that the <term>month of death|Niryana-masa</term> is indicated by the Navamsha resulting from the sum of the Ascendant and Mandi. The position of the <term>Moon at the time of death|Niryana-Chandra</term> is found through the combination of Mandi and the Moon; and the <term>Ascendant at the time of death|Niryana-Lagna</term> is found by the combination of the natal Ascendant, Saturn, and the Moon. || 11 ||

When <term>Jupiter|Amarajya - "Teacher of the Immortals"</term> reaches the Navamsha occupied by Mandi, and when Saturn reaches the <term>Dwadashamsha|The 1/12th divisional chart</term> associated with it...

### Chapter Eighteen
#### Conjunctions of Two Planets

The Sun, when conjoined with the <term>Moon|upeta - literally "the one who approaches"</term>, produces a man who is a maker of machines or a stonemason.

When conjoined with Mars, the Sun makes one inclined toward sinful acts; with Mercury, one becomes skilled, intelligent, famous, and endowed with happiness.

When conjoined with <term>Jupiter|Vākpati - the Lord of Speech</term>, one is cruel and devoted to the work of others; with Venus, one earns wealth through the stage <note>original: "raṅga" - referring to theater or colors/dyes</note> or weapons.

When conjoined with <term>Saturn|Ravija - the son of the Sun</term>, one is skilled in archery or in the manufacture of various types of pottery and vessels. || 1 ||

## Results Based on the Moon’s Placement in Various <term>Navamshas|Ninth-parts</term>
## and the Effects of the Aspects of Various Planets upon It

> If the Moon is situated in the <term>Navamsha|sub-division</term> of Mars, one becomes a guard, 
> one who delights in violence, one skilled in battle, 
> a king, a man of wealth, or a creator of quarrels. 
> In the <term>Navamsha</term> of Venus, one is <unclear>a fool, or</unclear> one devoted to their own wife, 
> a happy person, a creator of fine poetry, a seeker of pleasure, or one who pursues the wives of others. || 12 ||
<note>The "Navamsha" is a micro-zodiac used in Vedic astrology to see the "inner strength" or hidden nature of a planet. Here, the author explains that even if the Moon is in one sign, its sub-division (Mars-ruled or Venus-ruled) drastically changes the personality.</note>

> In the <term>Navamsha</term> of Mercury, one is a rogue, a wanderer, a thief, a poet, 
> a famous man, one who enjoys women, a knower of songs, or skilled in crafts. 
> In the <term>Navamsha</term> of Saturn, one has a small frame, is greedy for wealth, 
> a leader among ascetics, or one dedicated to the service of women when aspected [by other planets]. || 13 ||
<note>original: "baudhe" (belonging to Mercury) and "saurāṃśe" (in the portion of Saturn). These verses categorize human archetypes—from the "skilled artisan" to the "greedy ascetic"—based on these celestial coordinates.</note>

## Chapter 19

### Results of Planetary Periods (Dasha Phala)

> With devotion, he by whom the <term>Navagraha|nine planets</term> were worshipped in many ways for a long time,
>
> They, being satisfied, granted the grace of determining the cause of the knowledge of results.
>
> That which is famous, spoken by <term>Parasara|legendary sage</term> and gathered from the <term>Horagama|astrological tradition</term>,
>
<note>Horagama refers to the traditional body of knowledge or scriptures concerning "Hora" (time/astrology).</note>
> The essence, which has been abundantly tested and found to be highly fruitful—that "Great Cycle" known as <term>Dasha|planetary period</term>, I shall now tell. || 1 ||

The lords of the stars, starting from <term>Krittika|the Pleiades</term>, are the Sun, Moon, Mars,
<note>The text lists the sequence of the "Vimshottari Dasha" system, the most common timing system in Indian astrology, which assigns periods of life to specific planets.</note>
Rahu, Jupiter, Saturn, Mercury, Ketu, and Venus.
<note>original: "Sarpa" (Snake) for Rahu, "Amarejya" (Teacher of the Gods) for Jupiter, "Chandraja" (Son of the Moon) for Mercury.</note>
By that... <unclear>te ne natah sanijaya chatuddhanyasamya</unclear>
<note>This final line appears to be a mnemonic or a coded numerical string used for calculating the duration of the planetary periods, though the OCR is partially corrupted.</note>

->In their proper place, the years assigned to them are said to be twenty. || 2 ||<-
<note>original: "Sthāne nakhā nigaditāḥ śaradastu teṣām." In Sanskrit poetic mathematics, "nakha" (fingernails/toenails) is a code word for the number 20. This refers to the 20-year period assigned to the planet Venus in the Vimshottari Dasha system.</note>

Subtract the elapsed <term>Ghatikas|units of 24 minutes</term> of the birth star and take the remaining portion;
Multiply this by the total years of the planet's period, then divide by the total duration of the star.
By multiplying the remainder by the standard numerical factors,
The months and days are calculated in their proper order. || 3 ||
<note>This verse describes the "Bhogyam" calculation—determining exactly how much of a planetary period remains at the moment of birth based on the Moon's position in a specific constellation.</note>

When the precise longitude of the Sun at the time of birth
Is reached by the Sun again each year,
The cycles of the planetary years are established;
The days should be calculated based on the progression of the degrees. || 4 ||
<note>This refers to the "Varshaphala" or Solar Return system, where a new yearly chart is cast when the Sun returns to the exact degree it occupied at birth.</note>

Sudden illness among one's own kinsmen, and a tendency to wander.

### The Effects of the Period of Mars

The effects during the planetary period of **<term>Bhauma|Mars</term>** are as follows:
Wealth is acquired through fire, trade, or battle.
Wealth also comes from the practice of medicine, through various forms of deceit, or through acts of cruelty.
The person is constantly troubled by <term>Pitta|bile</term> and blood-related fevers, and associates with lowly women.
There is enmity with children, wife, relatives, and teachers; one suffers hardship and remains envious of the fortune of others. || 9 ||
<note>In traditional medicine, Mars is often associated with heat and "Pitta" imbalances, which manifest as fevers or inflammatory blood conditions.</note>

---

### The Effects of the Period of Mercury

**<term>Saumya|Mercury</term>** brings the arrival of friends and personal happiness,
Fame that is praised by the learned, and the grace of one's teachers.
It grants eloquence in speech, a disposition toward helping others,
And ensures the well-being and greatness of one’s wife, children, and friends. || 10 ||
<note>original: "Saumya." This name for Mercury literally means "the son of the Moon" and relates to its gentle, intellectual nature.</note>

---

### The Effects of the Period of Jupiter

The <term>Amarendra-guru|Jupiter</term> bestows the attainment of righteous actions,
The blessing of children, and honor from the king.
It brings high status and praise among the noble, and travel by means of elephants and horses...
<note>original: "Amarendra-guru," meaning the "Teacher of the King of the Gods" (Indra), a common epithet for the planet Jupiter (Brihaspati).</note>

### The Effects of the Period of Venus

The effects during the planetary period of **<term>Shaukrayam|Venus</term>** are as follows:
One acquires tools for pleasure and play, and obtains excellent vehicles. 
There is the gain of cattle, gems, ornaments, and hidden treasures, along with great joy in the company of women.
Engagement in auspicious rituals, travel by way of water <note>original: "salila-yanam," referring to sea voyages or boat travel</note>, and the receipt of great honors and rewards from the lord of the land <note>a king or high official</note> are to be expected. || 12 ||

---

### The Effects of the Period of Saturn

During the period of **<term>Arkajasya|Saturn</term>**, one experiences:
Sickness involving one's own wife and children, and disorders dominated by "wind" <note>original: "Vata," one of the three humors in traditional medicine, often associated with joints, nerves, and digestion</note>.
There is destruction of agricultural work, engaging in wicked or useless speech, and infatuation with unvirtuous women. 
One suffers separation from servants and attendants, unexpected exile or foreign travel, and the loss of relatives, land, happiness, and wealth. || 13 ||
<note>Saturn is referred to here as "Arkaja," meaning "Son of the Sun." In Vedic astrology, Saturn's period is often associated with hardship, discipline, and the clearing of old debts.</note>

---

### The Effects of the Period of Rahu

The **<term>Ahi|Rahu</term>** brings about the following:
Fear of the king, thieves, poison, fire, and weapons; suffering concerning children; and the confusion or wandering of the spouse.
It causes the loss of relatives and humiliation at the hands of those of lower status. 
Exceeding boundaries leads to scandal and infamy, displacement from one's position, the loss of rank, and the failure of all undertaken tasks. || 14 ||
<note>original: "Ahi." This term literally means "Serpent," a common name for the North Node of the Moon, Rahu, which is said to "swallow" the sun and moon during eclipses.</note>

<meta>This page continues the catalog of astrological predictions for the "dashas" (planetary periods). <page-num>149</page-num>

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

If the **<term>Lord of the Second House|Dwitiyadhipati, the ruler of the house governing wealth and speech</term>** is poorly placed <note>situated in the "vulnerable" 6th, 8th, or 12th houses</note>, one experiences defects in speech, the depletion of wealth, and fear of the king. || 15 ||

When the **<term>Lord of the Third House|Dushchikyadhipati, the ruler of the house governing siblings and courage</term>** is afflicted, one may witness the death of siblings and suffer from poor judgment in one's undertakings. One experiences mental distress caused by enemies, humiliation, and the abandonment of one's righteous duties.

If the **<term>Lord of the Fourth House|Patalanatha, the ruler of the house governing the home, mother, and comforts</term>** is weak, it brings suffering to the mother, misfortunes to beloved friends, and calamities involving land and houses. One may face the loss of cattle and horses <note>original: "pashv-ashvadi," representing livestock and means of transport</note> and harbor a fear of water. || 16 ||

If the **<term>Lord of the Fifth House|Pratibhapati, the ruler of the house governing intellect and children</term>** lacks strength, there is the grief of losing children, mental confusion, and aimless wandering. One may suffer from heart ailments, the wrath of the king, and a total exhaustion of one's personal power.

During the planetary period of the **<term>Lord of the Sixth House|Shashtesha, the ruler of the house governing enemies, debts, and disease</term>**, one faces fear from thieves, misfortune, and suppression. One suffers from many diseases, falls into sinful conduct, and is reduced to a state of servitude, enduring dishonor, infamy, and physical wounds. || 17 ||

If the **<term>Lord of the Seventh House|Yanesha, the ruler of the house governing marriage and partnerships</term>** is devoid of strength, one experiences distress concerning the son-in-law, separation from the spouse, and misfortunes originating from women. One develops an inclination toward falsehood and suffers from diseases of the private organs <note>original: "guhyamayam"</note>.

During the period of the **<term>Lord of the Eighth House|Randhresha, the ruler of the house governing longevity and obstacles</term>**, one is overcome by grief, delusion, intoxicating pride, and envy, leading to a state of physical or mental collapse...

It details specific misfortunes ranging from the loss of wealth and family members to physical illness and loss of social standing.</summary>

## Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

The planetary period <note>original: "dasha"</note> of a planet situated in the **<term>Vargottama|the most auspicious divisional position, where a planet occupies the same zodiac sign in both the natal chart and the ninth harmonic (Navamsha) chart</term>** bestows auspicious results. However, if that planet is combust <note>meaning it is too close to the Sun</note> or in its sign of debilitation, the results will be mixed. Even for a well-placed planet, if its sub-period falls under a planet located in the houses of death, loss, or enemies <note>the 8th, 12th, and 6th houses respectively</note>, the outcome will be inauspicious. || 22 ||

During the planetary period of a **<term>malefic planet|krura-graha, naturally harsh planets such as Mars or Saturn</term>**, specifically during the ripening of the sub-periods of the lords of the third, fifth, or seventh asterisms <note>original: "tripanchasaptarpapate," referring to the Vipat (danger), Pratyari (obstruction), and Naidhana (death) stars in the lunar mansion system</note>, or during the sub-period of the lord of the eighth house from the birth sign, the individual suffers great misery and is tormented by thieves and enemies. || 23 ||

The fourth planetary period in one’s life if ruled by **<term>Saturn|Shani</term>**, the sixth if ruled by **<term>Jupiter|Guru</term>**, or the fifth if ruled by **<term>Mars|Kuja</term>** are considered difficult. Similarly, suffering occurs if the period belongs to the lord of a **<term>malefic house|dusthana-pati, the ruler of the 6th, 8th, or 12th houses</term>** or a planet situated in the final degrees of a zodiac sign <note>original: "rashyavasana-bhaga," indicating a state of transition or weakness</note>. || 24 ||

If the Lord of a Trine is situated in an Angle, or if the Lord of an Angle is situated in a Trine, both of them become givers of **<term>Yoga|a specific planetary union or auspicious combination</term>**. During their respective Major Periods and **<term>Sub-periods|Bhukti</term>**, these two planets mutually support each other and bestow the fruits of that Yoga. || 42 ||

Planets do not simply bestow all their results during their own Major Periods and Sub-periods. The auspicious or inauspicious fruits of the **<term>Houses|Bhava</term>** for individuals manifest in accordance with the planets' specific relationships and inherent nature. || 43 ||

## Technical Vocabulary

| Term | Meaning |
| :--- | :--- |
| **Kendra** | The "Angles" or "Pillars" of the chart (Houses 1, 4, 7, 10). |
| **Trikona** | The "Trines" or houses of Lakshmi (Houses 1, 5, 9). |
| **Dasha** | A major planetary period that dictates a phase of life. |
| **Bhukti** | A sub-period within a Dasha. |
| **Yoga** | A combination of planets that creates a specific destiny or "union." |
| **Lagnapa** | The Lord of the Ascendant (1st house), representing the self. |

The **<term>Lords of the Angles and Trines|Kendra-trikona-netarau</term>** <note>the Angle houses are 1, 4, 7, 10; the Trines are 1, 5, 9</note>, even if they are personally afflicted by flaws, become powerful **<term>Yoga-producers|Yoga-karaka</term>** <note>planets capable of bestowing success and prosperity</note> simply through their mutual relationship. || 45 ||

During its own Major Period, the **<term>Lord of a Trine|Trikonesha</term>** gives auspicious results in the Sub-period of the **<term>Lord of an Angle|Kendrapateh</term>**. This is true even if there is no specific relationship between them; otherwise, the period may produce harmful effects. || 49 ||

Furthermore, their status as **<term>Death-inflicting planets|Maraka</term>** depends on their placement in the **<term>Killer Houses|Maraka-sthana</term>** <note>typically the 2nd and 7th houses</note>. || 50 ||

Mercury and the Moon follow the same rule. A planet situated in the **<term>Eighth House|Randhra</term>** <note>the house of longevity and hidden things</note>, the Lord of the Eighth, the Lord of the Eighth from the Eighth, or the Lord of the **<term>Twenty-second Decanate|Randhra-tribhaga</term>** <note>original: "Randhra-tribhaga-adhipa"; the lord of the 22nd drekkana, a specific 10-degree division of the zodiac known for adverse effects</note>—these bring grief. Among these, whichever is the weakest will cause total destruction during its own **<term>Major or Sub-period|Dasha-apahara</term>**. || 55 ||

<page-num>160</page-num>
<header>Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series</header>

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

A planet moving away from its point of **<term>Exaltation|Tunga</term>** <note>The highest point of a planet's strength</note> is designated as **<term>Descending|Avarohana</term>**. It is considered to be in a **Middle** state when it is in a friendly sign or a high division. A planet moving away from its point of **<term>Debilitation|Nimna</term>** <note>The lowest point of a planet's strength</note> is called **<term>Ascending|Aarohini</term>**. If it is in its sign or division of debilitation, it is termed **Degraded** (Adhama). || 56 ||

Whether a planet is in an auspicious house or an auspicious division, or even if it is in its debilitation sign, an enemy’s house, or is **<term>Combust|Astasamsthita</term>** <note>Lost in the glare of the Sun</note>—its **<term>Planetary Period|Dasha</term>** will yield mixed results. It should be understood that the primary results of such a period manifest during its second half. || 57 ||

During the planetary period of a planet that lacks strength and serves as the lord of the 12th or 2nd house from any given house <note>The 2nd and 12th houses from any house are considered 'death-inflicting' or 'loss-inducing' relative to that house’s themes</note>, or the lord of the 12th house relative to that house, one may encounter death. || 58 ||

If the **Lord of the Dasha** is situated in the **<term>Ascendant|Lagna</term>**, or in the 3rd, 6th, or 11th houses from the Lagna, or in its **<term>Seven-fold Divisions|Saptavarga</term>**, or in a friend's sign, or if a **<term>Benefic planet|Shubha</term>** is in the Lagna, then that planetary period will be auspicious. || 59 ||

As many years as that Dasha may last...

**<term>Dasha|Planetary Period</term>**, **<term>Lagnamu|Ascendant</term>**, **<term>Khetamu|Planet</term>**, **<term>Saptavarga|Seven Divisional Charts</term>**, **<term>Shubhagrahamu|Benefic Planet</term>**, **<term>Phalamu|Result</term>**

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

In the course of their movement, the **<term>Lord of the Period|Dashapati</term>** <note>The planet currently ruling the individual's life cycle</note> produces results. One should determine the good and bad outcomes based on the positions of the lord of the house where the Dasha Lord is situated. || 60 ||

When the Moon is positioned in a house belonging to a friend of the **<term>Lord of the Period|Dashadhinatha</term>**, or is in its own sign of **<term>Exaltation|Uccha</term>**, or is located in the **<term>Seventh House|Smara</term>**, a **<term>Trine|Trikona</term>** <note>The 1st, 5th, or 9th houses</note>, or an **<term>Increasing House|Upachaya</term>** <note>The 3rd, 6th, 10th, or 11th houses, where planets grow in strength over time</note> relative to the Dasha Lord, it truly yields auspicious results. || 61 ||

When the Moon enters the aforementioned **<term>Zodiac Signs|Rashis</term>**, and if that sign happens to be the native's **<term>Ascendant|Murtibhava</term>** <note>The 1st house representing the self/body</note>, the house of wealth, or other such houses in the birth chart, it causes the growth and prosperity of those specific areas of life for the individual. If the Moon is situated in signs other than these, it causes the depletion or loss of those areas. || 62 ||

*Saravali* <note>A classic 8th-century astrological treatise by Kalyana Varma</note>, the system of *Stellar Periods* (Udu Dasha), and the *Horoscopy of Varahamihira* (Varaha Hora)...

Having carefully examined the **<term>results of the birth chart|Jatakaphalam</term>**, one should speak of the destiny of men. Whether the prediction is made based on the planetary positions at the time of a **<term>query|Prashna</term>** <note>Horary astrology, where a chart is cast for the moment a question is asked</note> or based on the person's own **<term>birth sign|Janma Rashi</term>** and other natal factors, one should speak; there is no fundamental difference between these two methods. || 63 ||

### Chapter Twenty-One
### Results of the Sub-sub-periods

I shall now clearly describe the sequence and characteristics of the division of sub-periods <note>The text uses the term *Apahara*, synonymous with *Antardasha*</note>. Everything that has been stated previously regarding the major periods should be applied here to the **<term>sub-sub-periods|Pratyantar-dasha</term>** and further divisions. || 1 ||

The years of the **<term>sub-period lord|Pakesha</term>** are multiplied by the years of the **<term>major period lord|Dasheshvara</term>**, and the product is divided by the total cycle <note>The text uses "Netrajya," a numerical code referring to the 120-year Vimshottari cycle</note> to find the years. The remainder is multiplied to find the months and then the days. In this manner, one should calculate the sub-sub-periods for the **<term>segments|Chidra</term>** according to the sequence of the ruling lords. || 2 ||

**The Sun**

One attains great fame from the ruler of the earth <note>The government or high-ranking officials</note>, a residence in forests or mountain regions, and the **<term>acquisition of wealth|Dhanagamam</term>**.

Fever, diseases of the mouth and lips, and fear arising from the loss of or separation from a father figure <note>original: "janakaviyoga-jam bhayam"</note> enter one's life when the **<term>Hot-Rayed One|Tikshnadidhiti</term>** <note>A poetic epithet for the Sun</note> enters its own sub-period. || 3 ||

The destruction of enemies, the subsiding of sorrows, and the arrival of wealth occur; likewise, agricultural activities, the building of a house, and reunions with friends are favored. Though it repels the "fire of consumption" <note>original: "kshayanala"; likely referring to wasting diseases like tuberculosis</note>, the **<term>Moon|Shashi</term>**, when it takes its turn, brings about diseases born of the water element <note>original: "jalodbhava rujaha"; according to Ayurvedic-astrological thought, the Moon governs fluids and phlegm</note>. || 4 ||

The onset of disease, loss of one's position or rank, and torment from enemies occurs; sores or ulcers may break out, and there is conflict with one's own kinsmen. Fear from the "protector of the earth" <note>original: "mahibhrito"; the government or ruling authorities</note> and the loss of wealth happen when **<term>Mars|Kuja</term>** takes over during the Sun's year. || 5 ||

The rise of enemies, the theft of wealth, and the emergence of calamities occur; there is also fear of poison and a state of being completely bewildered by sensory desires...

...occurs when the years of the **Serpent-born** <note>original: "Bhujagya" – here referring to the major cycle of Rahu</note> are taken over by the **Son of Bhrigu** <note>original: "Bhrigu" – an epithet for the planet Venus</note>. || 35 ||

There will be pain from enemies and intense suffering; dangers arising from eye diseases, poison, fire, weapons, and snakes will occur. Distress to one's wife and children and great fear of the king <note>In a modern context, "fear of the king" refers to trouble with government authorities or legal issues</note> arises when the years of the **Serpent-born** [Rahu] are taken by the **Enemy of Darkness** <note>original: "Timirari" – a poetic name for the Sun</note>. || 36 ||

The loss of one's wife, quarrels, and mental illness; the decline of agriculture and the loss of wealth, cattle, and progeny; misfortune to friends and fear of water occurs during the sub-period of the **Two-Fold One** <note>original: "Vidha" – likely referring to the Moon and its phases</note> within the cycle of the **Enemy of the Gods** <note>original: "Devavidvish" – an epithet for Rahu, referring to the eclipse myth</note>. || 37 ||

Fear of kings, fire, thieves, and weapons for living beings; the destruction of the physical body or great illness; loss of position and intense pain in the heart and eyes occurs when the **Son of the Earth** <note>original: "Kuja" – the planet Mars</note> moves through the years of this cycle. || 38 ||

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

### Results of the various sub-periods within the Major Cycle of Jupiter

The rise of good fortune, radiance, honors, and virtuous qualities;
The attainment of success for oneself and one's children, and receiving honors from kings;
Association with teachers <term>Acharya: a spiritual guide or instructor</term> and saintly people, and the fulfillment of all desires;
These occur during the year the **Teacher of the Gods** <note>original: "Devaguru" — an epithet for Jupiter</note> moves through his own sub-period. || 39 ||

Addiction to vices such as association with prostitutes, ignorance, intoxication, and the faults of liquor;
While there may be some elevation and happiness, there is also suffering for the family and cattle;
Expenditure of wealth and fear, along with ailments of the eyes;
These enter the lives of men when the **Maker of Day** <note>original: "Dainakara" — a name for the Sun</note> enters the cycle of **Jupiter** <note>original: "Jaiveem"</note>. || 40 ||

Great addictions arising from women, gambling, and liquor, and imbalances of the three humors <term>Tridosha: the three bodily bio-elements (Vata, Pitta, Kapha) in Ayurvedic medicine</term>;
Some also say there is purely the acquisition of knowledge;
Through the worship of gods and the twice-born <term>Dwijas: individuals from the first three social classes who have undergone spiritual initiation</term>, and the enjoyment of children and wealth...

<vocab>
Jupiter: బృహస్పతి (Brihaspati)
Major Cycle: మహాదశా (Mahadasha)
Sub-period: అంతర్దశాం (Antardasha)
Result: ఫల (Phala)
Good Fortune: సౌభాగ్య (Saubhagya)
Teacher: ఆచార్య (Acharya)
Teacher of the Gods: దేవగురౌ (Devagurau)
Family: కుటుంబ (Kutumba)
Of men: నరాణామ్ (Naranam)
Memorial: స్మృతి (Smriti)
</vocab>

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

...the state of being honored and the growth of devotion occurs when the **Son of the Moon** <note>original: "Indusuta" — Mercury</note> takes over the period. || 41 ||

Wounds from weapons occur, along with conflict with servants;
Mental distress and calamities involving children or one’s wife;
A fall from one's position <note>original: "Pranachyutir"</note> and separation from teachers and friends;
These are given by **Ketu** <note>The South Node of the Moon</note> when he takes away the years from the **Son of the Sun's Teacher** <note>original: "Saureddyam" — referring here to the sub-period following the previous cycle within Jupiter's major influence</note>. || 42 ||

Various types of wealth, cattle, grains, and household items;
The acquisition of a wife, children, food, drink, fine beds, clothing, and ornaments;
The worship of gods and the twice-born <term>Dwija: members of the three upper social classes initiated into Vedic study</term> and a focus on spiritual practice;
These occur when the **Teacher of the Demons** <note>original: "Asuredyah" — an epithet for Venus</note> takes over the life-period of the **Jupiterian cycle** <note>original: "Jaivam"</note>. || 43 ||

Victory over enemies and the attainment of fame and honors from the King;
Purity of character and the acquisition of men, horses, and vehicles;
All types of wealth coming from professional guilds, land grants <term>Agrahara: land or villages deeded to scholars and temples</term>, cities, and the state;
These rise greatly when the **Maker of Rays** <note>original: "Arke" — the Sun</note> takes over the natural years of the cycle. || 44 ||

## The Results of Various Sub-periods within the Major Period of Saturn

->Growth in agriculture, an increase in servants and livestock, and prosperity;<-
->Ailments caused by wind <note>In traditional medicine, "wind" (Vata) refers to conditions like joint pain, nerve issues, or bloating</note>, and wealth obtained from rulers or through one's own community;<-
->The acquisition of wisdom from elders, though accompanied by laziness and similar traits;<-
->These arise during the sub-period of the **Son of the Sun** <note>original: "Ravije" — Saturn</note> within his own years. || 48 ||<-

->Good fortune, a happy and devoted wife, and protection or favor from the King;<-
->Victory in endeavors and the gathering of friends;<-
->The onset of diseases of the three humors <term>Tridosha: the three biological energies—Vata, Pitta, and Kapha—that govern human health</term> and illnesses affecting one’s brothers or children;<-
->These occur when the **Son of the Moon** <note>original: "Shashankasute" — Mercury</note> steals away a portion of Saturn's period. || 49 ||<-

->Suffering from the elements of wind and fire, and distress caused by enemies;<-
->A mind constantly inclined toward quarreling with one’s wife and children;<-
->The witnessing of inauspicious events and a fear of the ignoble or wicked;<-
->These come to pass when **Lord Ketu** <note>original: "Ketupatau" — the South Node of the Moon</note> takes over the gentle years. || 50 ||<-

The performance of various religious duties and the worship of the lord of the gods;

<header>Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series</header>

Whatever results have been declared for the major planetary periods,

one should speak of them in accordance with the person’s status and authority <note>original: "varna-adhikara-anugunam"; implying results vary based on a person's social standing and character</note>.

Even in the minor divisions and the most subtle sub-periods, those results are obtained;

alternatively, there may be a "shadow" or an essence of those meanings heard <note>original: "chaya-jnavartha-shravanani"; suggesting that even if the full result doesn't manifest, its influence or "shadow" is felt</note>. || 44 ||

## Chapter Twenty-Two
### The Kala Chakra Dasha <note>The "Time-Wheel" system of planetary periods, considered one of the most advanced and accurate systems in Hindu astrology</note>

Starting from the star **Ashwini** <note>The first of the 27 lunar mansions; original: "Dasra-aditah"</note>, and following the quarters of the stars, one should count from **Aries** through to **Pisces** in the reverse order <note>original: "apasavyam"; counter-clockwise or against the usual order of the zodiac</note>. From **Scorpio** <note>original: "Keeta"</note> to **Sagittarius** <note>original: "Haya-antam"</note>, one should count in the forward order <note>original: "savyam"; the standard zodiacal direction</note> based on the sequence of the quarters from the birth star. || 1 ||

In this way, there will be both reverse and forward movements; one should understand these in groups of three stars each in succession.

<term>ద్వావింశోధ్యాయః</term> <note>Twenty-second chapter</note>
<term>కాలచక్ర దశా</term> <note>Kala Chakra Dasha; a predictive system based on the moon's position in specific quarters of a Nakshatra</note>
<term>దస్రాదితః</term> <note>Starting from the Ashwini Nakshatra</note>
<term>మేషాన్న్</term> <note>From the sign of Aries</note>
<term>మీనామ్ముకాన్తం</term> <note>Ending with the sign of Pisces</note>
<term>కీటాద్ధయాన్తం</term> <note>From Scorpio (Keeta) to Sagittarius (Haya)</note>
<term>విద్యాత్క్రమేణ</term> <note>Should be known in proper sequence</note>

# Vemuri Durga Nageswara Rao Memorial Book Series

Based on the descriptions of the zodiac signs and their specific qualities <note>original: "varna-adi"; referring to the categories or "colors" assigned to signs</note>, one speaks of the results of the planetary periods within the great brilliance of the **Kala Chakra** <note>The "Time-Wheel," a sophisticated system of Hindu astrology used to predict timing of events</note>. || 2 ||

From the great Manu <note>The legendary first man and lawgiver</note> to others, even a king who goes to the forest for penance follows this sequence. The Sun and the passing years are the primary causes for obtaining both good and bad results in life. || 3 ||

### On the sub-periods of the Time-Wheel
The statements regarding the sub-periods <note>original: "apahara"; the minor cycles within a major planetary period</note> and the cycles of the **Kala Chakra** are derived from terms beginning with **Dasra** <note>Another name for the Ashwini twins, signifying the first lunar mansion, Ashwini</note> and other foot-positions of the stars.

I shall explain the maximum longevity <note>original: "paramayur"</note> here, calculated through nine categories of syllables and the movements through the best of the zodiac signs. || 4 ||

The cow, the crossing, the friendly, and the oily;
the Moon in the star, and the earth-spear <note>original: "bhu-shulam"; these are mnemonic phrases where specific words represent numbers used in astronomical and astrological calculations</note>.

The forms of the treasure-carver and the King;
the Goddess of Speech <note>Saraswati</note>, the curd, and the star. || 5 ||

The Lord of service, the **Son of Gauri** <note>Ganesha, the elephant-headed deity and remover of obstacles, often invoked to bless the completion of a work</note>.

<vocab>Kala Chakra (Time-Wheel), Nakshatra (Lunar mansion), Gauri-putra (Son of Gauri/Ganesha), Rashi (Zodiac sign), Varna (Category/Syllable), Smriti Granthavali (Memorial Book Series)</vocab>

It introduces the technical mnemonic system used to calculate a person's maximum lifespan based on their birth star.</summary>

->Lesson on the solar-influenced lunar mansions<-

->In the holy water of Shiva, the protector of the earth;<-

->the wise and smiling one, the auspicious and beautiful. || 9 ||<-

->Arising clearly from the quarters of the lunar mansions <note>original: "nakshatra-pada"; each of the 27 stars is divided into four quarters used for precise calculations</note>,<-

->the initial planetary period <note>original: "dasha"</note> is born from the years assigned to its presiding lord.<-

->One should understand the sequence of the quarters here as previously described;<-

->such is the opinion held by some of the noble teachers. || 10 ||<-

->Starting from the quarters beginning with **Ashwini** <note>original: "Dasra"; a name for the twin deities who rule the first constellation</note> and the other stars,<-

->there are mnemonic phrases <note>original: "vakyas"</note> created from sequences of letters.<-

->The planetary periods should be determined according to their specific order;<-

->some say this is how the sequence of the phrases is successfully established. || 11 ||<-

->The sequence of phrases for the signs of Cancer, Scorpio, and Pisces,<-

->is known as the "frog's leap" <term>Manduka-gati: a calculation where the cycle "jumps" over certain signs, mimicking a frog's movement</term> and the "horse's gallop" <term>Ashva-pluti: a specific leap in the sequence resembling the jump of a horse</term>.<-
